How to scope Microservices using bounded context tech stack Java 8, spring boot?
This is an excellent architectural question. Scoping microservices using Bounded Contexts (from Domain-Driven Design) is the most effective way to avoid the "distributed monolith" anti-pattern.

Here is a step-by-step guide to scoping microservices using Bounded Contexts, specifically for a Java 8, Spring Boot tech stack.

Step 1: Understand the Core Principle
A microservice should map to one Bounded Context, not to one "entity" (e.g., User) or one technical layer (e.g., Data Access).

Bounded Context (BC): A logical boundary where a particular Ubiquitous Language (specific terms, rules, and meanings) applies.

Wrong scoping: User Service (becomes a God object), Data Service (technical), Utils Service (no domain logic).

Right scoping: Order Fulfillment Context, Inventory Management Context, Customer Support Context.

Step 2: Identify Bounded Contexts from Your Domain
Use Event Storming (whiteboard exercise) or Domain Analysis. Ask:

Does the word "Customer" mean the same thing in two different areas?

Sales Context: Customer has creditLimit, isPreferred.

Support Context: Customer has ticketHistory, satisfactionScore.

Shipping Context: Customer has address, phoneNumber.

Conclusion: These are three different Bounded Contexts → three microservices.

Can two teams work independently on these models without constant coordination?

If not, they belong in the same BC.

Step 3: Define Service Boundaries with Examples (Java 8 + Spring Boot)
Let's take an E-commerce domain.

Bad Scope (Distributed Monolith)
CustomerService (handles profile, orders, payments, shipping preferences)

InventoryService (pure CRUD)

NotificationService (email/SMS for everything)

Good Scope (Bounded Contexts)
Bounded Context	Ubiquitous Language	Spring Boot Microservice	Database
Ordering	Order, OrderLine, Payment, Checkout, Cart	ordering-service	ordering_db
Inventory	Stock, Warehouse, Reserve, Allocate	inventory-service	inventory_db
Shipping	Shipment, Parcel, Label, Carrier, Tracking	shipping-service	shipping_db
Billing	Invoice, Receipt, Tax, CreditNote	billing-service	billing_db
Notice: Order in ordering-service has paymentStatus and orderTotal.
Order in shipping-service (if referenced) is just orderId + shippingAddress + weight. Different models → different contexts.

Step 4: Implement Each Microservice in Java 8 + Spring Boot

Project Structure per Bounded Context

ordering-service/
├── src/main/java/com/example/ordering/
│   ├── domain/               # The Bounded Context's core model
│   │   ├── model/            # Order, OrderLine, Money (Value Object)
│   │   ├── repository/       # OrderRepository (interface)
│   │   └── service/          # OrderDomainService
│   ├── application/          # Use cases / orchestration
│   │   └── OrderApplicationService
│   ├── infrastructure/       # Spring Data, Kafka, REST clients
│   │   ├── persistence/      # JPA entities (separate from domain)
│   │   ├── messaging/        # Event publishers
│   │   └── client/           # Feign clients to other BCs
│   └── interfaces/           # REST controllers
│       └── OrderController
└── resources/
    └── application.yml
	
Sample Domain Model (Java 8)
java
// In ordering-service domain package
public class Order {
    private OrderId id;
    private CustomerId customerId;
    private List<OrderLine> lines;
    private OrderStatus status;
    private Money total;
    
    public void addLine(ProductId productId, Quantity qty, Money price) {
        // Domain logic inside the BC boundary
        if (this.status != OrderStatus.DRAFT) {
            throw new IllegalStateException("Only draft orders can be modified");
        }
        this.lines.add(new OrderLine(productId, qty, price));
        this.recalculateTotal();
    }
}

Anti-corruption Layer (ACL) Between Contexts
When ordering-service needs data from inventory-service:

java
// In ordering-service/infrastructure/client
@FeignClient(name = "inventory-service", url = "${inventory.service.url}")
public interface InventoryClient {
    @GetMapping("/api/products/{id}/reservable-stock")
    ReservableStockResponse getReservableStock(@PathVariable("id") Long productId);
}

// DO NOT reuse Inventory's Product entity inside Ordering domain.
// Create a local DTO (anti-corruption layer)
public class ReservableStockResponse {
    private Long productId;
    private int availableQuantity;
    // No warehouse details, no supplier info — only what Ordering needs.
}

Step 5: Communication Between Bounded Contexts
Use Events (preferred) over RPC
Spring Boot + Kafka / RabbitMQ:

java
// In ordering-service after order is placed
@EventListener
public void handleOrderPlaced(OrderPlacedEvent event) {
    // Publish to "order.placed" topic
    kafkaTemplate.send("order.events", event);
}

// In inventory-service (listening)
@KafkaListener(topics = "order.events", groupId = "inventory-group")
public void handleOrderPlaced(OrderPlacedEvent event) {
    // Reserve stock in Inventory's own model
    inventoryService.reserveStock(event.getProductQuantities());
}
Why events? They allow each BC to evolve independently without breaking synchronous calls.

Use Synchronous RPC only for queries or workflows within a single user session
Example: Checkout UI needs real-time inventory availability → ordering-service calls inventory-service via Feign with fallback.

Step 6: Database Per Bounded Context (Critical Rule)
Each microservice has its own database (or at least its own schema with no shared tables).

No direct database access from one service to another's DB.

yaml
# ordering-service/application.yml
spring:
  datasource:
    url: jdbc:mysql://localhost:3306/ordering_db
    username: ordering_user
yaml
# inventory-service/application.yml
spring:
  datasource:
    url: jdbc:mysql://localhost:3306/inventory_db
    username: inventory_user
	
Step 7: Validate Your Scoping with These Questions
For each candidate microservice, ask:

Question	If YES → Good BC	If NO → Bad scope
Can the team define "Order" without talking to the Inventory team?	✅	❌
Does this service have a clear business capability (not technical)?	✅	❌
Can it fail independently without crashing others?	✅	❌
Does it have its own database schema?	✅	❌
Would splitting it further force two-phase commits?	❌ (don't split)	✅ (split further)

Step 8: Common Pitfalls (Java 8 Specific)
Shared JPA entities across services → Don't create a common.jar with @Entity classes.

Fix: Each service defines its own minimal JPA entities.

Using Spring Boot @EnableFeignClients without fallbacks → Creates tight coupling.

Fix: Always implement Circuit Breaker (Hystrix/Resilience4j).

Distributed transactions → Trying to use JTA/XA across services.

Fix: Use Sagas or Eventual Consistency (Saga pattern with Kafka).

Example: Correctly Scoped Microservices in Java 8
bash
# Git repositories (one per bounded context)
git@github.com:company/ordering-context-service.git   # Spring Boot 2.x, Java 8
git@github.com:company/inventory-context-service.git
git@github.com:company/shipping-context-service.git

# Each has:
# - Own build.gradle (separate dependencies)
# - Own database migrations (Flyway/Liquibase)
# - Own deployment pipeline
Final Checklist for Your Architecture
Did you identify at least 3 different meanings for your core entity?

Does each service have a single, well-defined business purpose?

Are you using events for cross-context communication?

Do you have an Anti-corruption Layer (no shared domain models)?

Can you change a Product definition in ordering-service without changing inventory-service?

If you answer YES to all, you have successfully scoped your microservices using Bounded Contexts with Java 8 + Spring Boot.

How to Design and architect asynchronous microservices using java 8, spring boot, kafka and db postgresql?
This is a powerful combination for building resilient, scalable systems. Let me walk you through designing asynchronous microservices using Java 8, Spring Boot, Kafka, and PostgreSQL.

Core Architecture Pattern: Event-Driven with CQRS

┌─────────────┐    ┌─────────────┐    ┌─────────────┐
│  Command    │    │   Kafka     │    │  Query      │
│  Service    │───▶│   Topics    │───▶│  Service    │
│  (Writes)   │    │             │    │  (Reads)    │
└─────────────┘    └─────────────┘    └─────────────┘
       │                  │                   │
       ▼                  ▼                   ▼
  PostgreSQL         Event Store         PostgreSQL
   (Transactional)    (Kafka)            (Read-optimized)
Step 1: Project Structure for Async Microservice

order-service/                    # Command side
├── src/main/java/com/example/order/
│   ├── domain/
│   │   ├── model/               # Aggregates, Entities
│   │   ├── event/               # Domain events
│   │   └── repository/          # Repository interfaces
│   ├── application/
│   │   ├── command/             # Command handlers
│   │   └── service/             # Application services
│   ├── infrastructure/
│   │   ├── config/              # Kafka, DB configs
│   │   ├── messaging/           # Kafka producers
│   │   └── persistence/         # PostgreSQL repositories
│   └── interfaces/
│       └── api/                 # REST endpoints
└── resources/
    └── application.yml
Step 2: Dependencies (build.gradle)

gradle
dependencies {
    // Spring Boot 2.x (Java 8 compatible)
    implementation 'org.springframework.boot:spring-boot-starter-web'
    implementation 'org.springframework.boot:spring-boot-starter-data-jpa'
    
    // PostgreSQL
    runtimeOnly 'org.postgresql:postgresql'
    
    // Kafka
    implementation 'org.springframework.kafka:spring-kafka'
    
    // JSON serialization
    implementation 'com.fasterxml.jackson.core:jackson-databind'
    
    // Optional: Outbox pattern
    implementation 'com.vladmihalcea:hibernate-types-52:2.14.0'
}

Step 3: PostgreSQL Schema Design for Async Processing
Transactional Table (Command Side)
sql
-- order-service database
CREATE TABLE orders (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    customer_id UUID NOT NULL,
    product_id UUID NOT NULL,
    quantity INT NOT NULL,
    status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL, -- PENDING, CONFIRMED, SHIPPED, CANCELLED
    total_amount DECIMAL(10,2),
    created_at TIMESTAMP,
    updated_at TIMESTAMP,
    version INT DEFAULT 0 -- For optimistic locking
);

-- Outbox table for reliable event publishing
CREATE TABLE outbox (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    aggregate_id UUID NOT NULL,
    event_type VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    payload JSONB NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL,
    published BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE,
    published_at TIMESTAMP
);

CREATE INDEX idx_outbox_unpublished ON outbox(published, created_at);
Materialized View (Query Side - different DB/schema)
sql
-- order-read-database (separate for query service)
CREATE TABLE order_read_model (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    customer_name VARCHAR(100),
    product_name VARCHAR(100),
    quantity INT,
    total_amount DECIMAL(10,2),
    status VARCHAR(20),
    shipping_status VARCHAR(20), -- Denormalized from shipping service
    payment_status VARCHAR(20),  -- Denormalized from payment service
    last_updated TIMESTAMP
);
Step 4: Domain Events & Command Pattern
Base Event Class
java
// domain/event/BaseEvent.java
public abstract class BaseEvent {
    private final UUID eventId = UUID.randomUUID();
    private final UUID aggregateId;
    private final LocalDateTime occurredAt = LocalDateTime.now();
    
    public BaseEvent(UUID aggregateId) {
        this.aggregateId = aggregateId;
    }
    
    // Getters, toString
}
Specific Event
java
// domain/event/OrderCreatedEvent.java
public class OrderCreatedEvent extends BaseEvent {
    private final UUID customerId;
    private final UUID productId;
    private final int quantity;
    private final BigDecimal totalAmount;
    
    public OrderCreatedEvent(UUID orderId, UUID customerId, UUID productId, 
                             int quantity, BigDecimal totalAmount) {
        super(orderId);
        this.customerId = customerId;
        this.productId = productId;
        this.quantity = quantity;
        this.totalAmount = totalAmount;
    }
    
    // Getters
}
Command Handler
java
// application/command/CreateOrderCommand.java
public class CreateOrderCommand {
    private final UUID customerId;
    private final UUID productId;
    private final int quantity;
    
    // constructor, getters
}

// application/command/CreateOrderCommandHandler.java
@Component
public class CreateOrderCommandHandler {
    private final OrderRepository orderRepository;
    private final EventPublisher eventPublisher;
    
    @Transactional
    public Order handle(CreateOrderCommand command) {
        // Create order aggregate
        Order order = new Order(
            UUID.randomUUID(),
            command.getCustomerId(),
            command.getProductId(),
            command.getQuantity()
        );
        
        // Save to PostgreSQL
        Order savedOrder = orderRepository.save(order);
        
        // Publish event (will be stored in outbox)
        eventPublisher.publish(new OrderCreatedEvent(
            savedOrder.getId(),
            savedOrder.getCustomerId(),
            savedOrder.getProductId(),
            savedOrder.getQuantity(),
            savedOrder.getTotalAmount()
        ));
        
        return savedOrder;
    }
}
Step 5: Reliable Event Publishing with Outbox Pattern
Outbox Repository
java
// infrastructure/persistence/OutboxRepository.java
@Repository
public interface OutboxRepository extends JpaRepository<OutboxEvent, UUID> {
    @Query("SELECT e FROM OutboxEvent e WHERE e.published = false ORDER BY e.createdAt ASC")
    List<OutboxEvent> findUnpublishedEvents(Pageable pageable);
}
Outbox Poller (Reliable Publisher)
java
// infrastructure/messaging/OutboxPoller.java
@Component
public class OutboxPoller {
    private final OutboxRepository outboxRepository;
    private final KafkaTemplate<String, String> kafkaTemplate;
    private final ObjectMapper objectMapper;
    
    @Scheduled(fixedDelay = 5000) // Poll every 5 seconds
    @Transactional
    public void publishEvents() {
        List<OutboxEvent> events = outboxRepository.findUnpublishedEvents(
            PageRequest.of(0, 100)
        );
        
        for (OutboxEvent event : events) {
            try {
                // Send to Kafka
                kafkaTemplate.send(
                    "order.events",
                    event.getAggregateId().toString(),
                    objectMapper.writeValueAsString(event.getPayload())
                ).get(5, TimeUnit.SECONDS);
                
                // Mark as published
                event.setPublished(true);
                event.setPublishedAt(LocalDateTime.now());
                outboxRepository.save(event);
                
            } catch (Exception e) {
                log.error("Failed to publish event: {}", event.getId(), e);
                // Will retry on next poll
            }
        }
    }
}
Step 6: Kafka Configuration (Java 8 Compatible)
java
// infrastructure/config/KafkaConfig.java
@Configuration
public class KafkaConfig {
    
    @Bean
    public ProducerFactory<String, String> producerFactory() {
        Map<String, Object> config = new HashMap<>();
        config.put(ProducerConfig.BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS_CONFIG, "localhost:9092");
        config.put(ProducerConfig.KEY_SERIALIZER_CLASS_CONFIG, StringSerializer.class);
        config.put(ProducerConfig.VALUE_SERIALIZER_CLASS_CONFIG, StringSerializer.class);
        config.put(ProducerConfig.ENABLE_IDEMPOTENCE_CONFIG, true);
        config.put(ProducerConfig.RETRIES_CONFIG, 3);
        config.put(ProducerConfig.ACKS_CONFIG, "all");
        return new DefaultKafkaProducerFactory<>(config);
    }
    
    @Bean
    public ConsumerFactory<String, String> consumerFactory() {
        Map<String, Object> config = new HashMap<>();
        config.put(ConsumerConfig.BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS_CONFIG, "localhost:9092");
        config.put(ConsumerConfig.GROUP_ID_CONFIG, "order-service-group");
        config.put(ConsumerConfig.KEY_DESERIALIZER_CLASS_CONFIG, StringDeserializer.class);
        config.put(ConsumerConfig.VALUE_DESERIALIZER_CLASS_CONFIG, StringDeserializer.class);
        config.put(ConsumerConfig.AUTO_OFFSET_RESET_CONFIG, "earliest");
        config.put(ConsumerConfig.ENABLE_AUTO_COMMIT_CONFIG, false); // Manual commit
        return new DefaultKafkaConsumerFactory<>(config);
    }
    
    @Bean
    public KafkaTemplate<String, String> kafkaTemplate() {
        return new KafkaTemplate<>(producerFactory());
    }
}
Step 7: Event Consumer with Idempotency
java
// infrastructure/messaging/OrderEventConsumer.java
@Component
public class OrderEventConsumer {
    private final ProcessedEventRepository processedEventRepository;
    private final OrderQueryService orderQueryService;
    private final ObjectMapper objectMapper;
    
    @KafkaListener(topics = "order.events", groupId = "order-query-group")
    public void consume(String message, Acknowledgment acknowledgment) {
        try {
            EventWrapper wrapper = objectMapper.readValue(message, EventWrapper.class);
            
            // Idempotency check using PostgreSQL
            if (processedEventRepository.existsById(wrapper.getEventId())) {
                log.info("Duplicate event detected, skipping: {}", wrapper.getEventId());
                acknowledgment.acknowledge();
                return;
            }
            
            // Process based on event type
            switch (wrapper.getEventType()) {
                case "ORDER_CREATED":
                    orderQueryService.handleOrderCreated(wrapper.getPayload());
                    break;
                case "ORDER_SHIPPED":
                    orderQueryService.handleOrderShipped(wrapper.getPayload());
                    break;
                case "ORDER_CANCELLED":
                    orderQueryService.handleOrderCancelled(wrapper.getPayload());
                    break;
            }
            
            // Store processed event ID
            processedEventRepository.save(new ProcessedEvent(wrapper.getEventId()));
            
            // Manual commit offset
            acknowledgment.acknowledge();
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            log.error("Error processing message", e);
            // Don't acknowledge - will retry
        }
    }
}
Idempotency Table
sql
CREATE TABLE processed_events (
    event_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    processed_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
);
Step 8: REST API (Triggers Async Processing)
java
// interfaces/api/OrderController.java
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/orders")
public class OrderController {
    
    private final CreateOrderCommandHandler commandHandler;
    private final OrderQueryRepository queryRepository;
    
    @PostMapping
    public ResponseEntity<OrderResponse> createOrder(@RequestBody CreateOrderRequest request) {
        // Command - writes to transaction DB
        Order order = commandHandler.handle(new CreateOrderCommand(
            request.getCustomerId(),
            request.getProductId(),
            request.getQuantity()
        ));
        
        // Return 202 Accepted immediately
        return ResponseEntity.accepted()
            .body(new OrderResponse(order.getId(), "PENDING", "Order received"));
    }
    
    @GetMapping("/{id}")
    public ResponseEntity<OrderReadModel> getOrder(@PathVariable UUID id) {
        // Query - reads from read-optimized DB/view
        return queryRepository.findById(id)
            .map(ResponseEntity::ok)
            .orElse(ResponseEntity.notFound().build());
    }
}
Step 9: Saga Pattern for Distributed Transactions
java
// Saga orchestrator for order -> payment -> shipping
@Component
public class OrderSagaOrchestrator {
    
    @KafkaListener(topics = "order.created")
    public void handleOrderCreated(OrderCreatedEvent event) {
        // Step 1: Process payment
        kafkaTemplate.send("payment.process", event);
    }
    
    @KafkaListener(topics = "payment.processed")
    public void handlePaymentProcessed(PaymentProcessedEvent event) {
        if (event.isSuccess()) {
            // Step 2: Reserve inventory
            kafkaTemplate.send("inventory.reserve", event);
        } else {
            // Compensation: Cancel order
            kafkaTemplate.send("order.cancel", event);
        }
    }
    
    @KafkaListener(topics = "inventory.reserved")
    public void handleInventoryReserved(InventoryReservedEvent event) {
        // Step 3: Create shipment
        kafkaTemplate.send("shipping.create", event);
    }
}
Step 10: Monitoring & Error Handling
Dead Letter Queue (DLQ) Configuration
java
@Configuration
public class DeadLetterConfig {
    
    @Bean
    public KafkaTemplate<String, String> deadLetterTemplate() {
        // Template for DLQ with different config
        Map<String, Object> config = new HashMap<>();
        config.put(ProducerConfig.BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS_CONFIG, "localhost:9092");
        // ... same as main producer
        return new KafkaTemplate<>(new DefaultKafkaProducerFactory<>(config));
    }
    
    @KafkaListener(topics = "order.events.DLT")
    public void handleDeadLetter(String message) {
        log.error("Message moved to DLQ: {}", message);
        // Store in PostgreSQL for manual intervention
        deadLetterRepository.save(new DeadLetterMessage(message));
    }
}
Step 11: Complete Application.yml
yaml
spring:
  datasource:
    url: jdbc:postgresql://localhost:5432/order_db
    username: order_user
    password: ${DB_PASSWORD}
    hikari:
      maximum-pool-size: 10
      
  jpa:
    hibernate:
      ddl-auto: validate
    properties:
      hibernate:
        dialect: org.hibernate.dialect.PostgreSQL95Dialect
        jdbc:
          batch_size: 20
          
  kafka:
    bootstrap-servers: localhost:9092
    producer:
      key-serializer: org.apache.kafka.common.serialization.StringSerializer
      value-serializer: org.apache.kafka.common.serialization.StringSerializer
      properties:
        enable.idempotence: true
        acks: all
        retries: 3
    consumer:
      group-id: order-service-group
      auto-offset-reset: earliest
      enable-auto-commit: false
      key-deserializer: org.apache.kafka.common.serialization.StringDeserializer
      value-deserializer: org.apache.kafka.common.serialization.StringDeserializer
      
  task:
    scheduling:
      pool:
        size: 5

# Retry configuration
outbox:
  poll-interval: 5000
  batch-size: 100
  
management:
  endpoints:
    web:
      exposure:
        include: health,metrics,kafka
		
Key Design Principles Summary:

Principle	Implementation
Reliability	Outbox pattern + idempotent consumers
Scalability	Kafka partitions + consumer groups
Consistency	Saga pattern + compensating transactions
Resilience	Retries + DLQ + circuit breakers
Performance	CQRS + materialized views
Observability	Correlation IDs + distributed tracing

Testing Async Flow
java

@SpringBootTest
@EmbeddedKafka
class OrderServiceIntegrationTest {
    
    @Test
    void testAsyncOrderCreation() {
        // Given
        CreateOrderRequest request = new CreateOrderRequest(customerId, productId, 5);
        
        // When
        ResponseEntity<OrderResponse> response = restTemplate.postForEntity(
            "/api/orders", request, OrderResponse.class
        );
        
        // Then
        assertThat(response.getStatusCode()).isEqualTo(HttpStatus.ACCEPTED);
        
        // Wait for async processing
        await().atMost(5, TimeUnit.SECONDS).untilAsserted(() -> {
            OrderReadModel order = queryRepository.findById(response.getBody().getId());
            assertThat(order.getStatus()).isEqualTo("CONFIRMED");
        });
    }
}

This architecture gives us:

At-least-once delivery with idempotency

Transaction boundaries preserved with outbox

Scalability through Kafka partitions

Resilience with retries and DLQ

Performance with CQRS separation

The system remains Java 8 compatible while using modern async patterns!

How to architect API based microservices tech stach java 8, spring boot, rest,?
Excellent question! API-based (synchronous) microservices are often the right choice for request-response patterns, real-time queries, and workflows where users expect immediate feedback. Let me show you how to architect them properly with Java 8, Spring Boot, and REST.

Core Architecture: Synchronous Request-Response

┌────────────┐    ┌────────────┐    ┌────────────┐
│  API       │    │  Service   │    │  Database  │
│  Gateway   │───▶│  Registry  │───▶│  Per       │
│  (Optional)│    │  (Eureka)  │    │  Service   │
└────────────┘    └────────────┘    └────────────┘
       │                 │                  │
       ▼                 ▼                  ▼
  Load Balancer    Service Mesh       PostgreSQL/
  (Nginx/HAProxy)  (Resilience4j)     Multiple DBs
  
Step 1: API Service Classification

Not all API services are the same. Classify them by responsibility:

Type	Responsibility	Example	Database
Edge Service	API Gateway, Auth, Rate Limiting	Spring Cloud Gateway	Redis (sessions)
Aggregator	Compose data from multiple services	Order Dashboard	None (pure orchestration)
Core Service	Business logic, owns data	Product Service	PostgreSQL
Utility Service	Shared capabilities	Notification Service	None or lightweight

Step 2: Project Structure for API Service

bash
product-service/                    # Core service example

Step 3: Dependencies (build.gradle)
gradle
plugins {
    id 'org.springframework.boot' version '2.7.18'  // Last Java 8 compatible
    id 'io.spring.dependency-management' version '1.0.15.RELEASE'
    id 'java'
}

group = 'com.example'
version = '1.0.0'
sourceCompatibility = '1.8'

repositories {
    mavenCentral()
}

ext {
    set('springCloudVersion', "2021.0.8")  // Java 8 compatible
}

dependencies {
    // Core Spring Boot
    implementation 'org.springframework.boot:spring-boot-starter-web'
    implementation 'org.springframework.boot:spring-boot-starter-validation'

Step 4: REST API Design Best Practices
Controller with Versioning and Error Handling
java
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/v1/products")
@Slf4j
@Validated
public class ProductController {
    
    private final ProductService productService;
    private final ProductAssembler assembler;
    
    public ProductController(ProductService productService, ProductAssembler assembler) {
        this.productService = productService;
        this.assembler = assembler;
    }
    
    @GetMapping
    public ResponseEntity<Page<ProductResponse>> getAllProducts(
            @PageableDefault(size = 20, sort = "createdAt", direction = Sort.Direction.DESC) Pageable pageable,
            @RequestParam(required = false) String category,
            @RequestParam(required = false) BigDecimal minPrice) {
        
        Page<Product> products = productService.findAll(pageable, category, minPrice);
        Page<ProductResponse> response = products.map(assembler::toResponse);
        return ResponseEntity.ok(response);
    }
    
    @GetMapping("/{id}")
    public ResponseEntity<ProductResponse> getProduct(@PathVariable UUID id) {
        return productService.findById(id)
                .map(assembler::toResponse)
                .map(ResponseEntity::ok)
                .orElseThrow(() -> new ResourceNotFoundException("Product not found: " + id));
    }
    
    @PostMapping
    @ResponseStatus(HttpStatus.CREATED)
    public ResponseEntity<ProductResponse> createProduct(@Valid @RequestBody CreateProductRequest request) {
        Product product = assembler.toEntity(request);
        Product saved = productService.create(product);
        URI location = ServletUriComponentsBuilder
                .fromCurrentRequest()
                .path("/{id}")
                .buildAndExpand(saved.getId())
                .toUri();
        return ResponseEntity.created(location).body(assembler.toResponse(saved));
    }
    
    @PutMapping("/{id}")
    public ResponseEntity<ProductResponse> updateProduct(
            @PathVariable UUID id, 
            @Valid @RequestBody UpdateProductRequest request) {
        Product product = productService.update(id, request);
        return ResponseEntity.ok(assembler.toResponse(product));
    }
    
    @PatchMapping("/{id}/price")
    public ResponseEntity<ProductResponse> updatePrice(
            @PathVariable UUID id, 
            @RequestBody PriceUpdateRequest request) {
        Product product = productService.updatePrice(id, request.getNewPrice());
        return ResponseEntity.ok(assembler.toResponse(product));
    }
    
    @DeleteMapping("/{id}")
    @ResponseStatus(HttpStatus.NO_CONTENT)
    public ResponseEntity<Void> deleteProduct(@PathVariable UUID id) {
        productService.delete(id);
        return ResponseEntity.noContent().build();
    }
}
DTOs with Validation
java
public class CreateProductRequest {
    @NotBlank(message = "Product name is required")
    @Size(min = 2, max = 100, message = "Product name must be between 2 and 100 characters")
    private String name;
    
    @Size(max = 500, message = "Description cannot exceed 500 characters")
    private String description;
    
    @NotNull(message = "Price is required")
    @DecimalMin(value = "0.01", message = "Price must be greater than 0")
    @DecimalMax(value = "999999.99", message = "Price cannot exceed 999,999.99")
    private BigDecimal price;
    
    @NotNull(message = "Category is required")
    private UUID categoryId;
    
    @Min(value = 0, message = "Stock cannot be negative")
    private Integer stockQuantity = 0;
    
    // Getters and setters
}

public class ProductResponse {
    private UUID id;
    private String name;
    private String description;
    private BigDecimal price;
    private UUID categoryId;
    private String categoryName;  // Denormalized for API convenience
    private Integer stockQuantity;
    private LocalDateTime createdAt;
    private LocalDateTime updatedAt;
    private Map<String, Object> links;  // HATEOAS
}
Step 5: Service Layer with Transaction Management
java
@Service
@Transactional
@Slf4j
public class ProductService {
    
    private final ProductRepository productRepository;
    private final CategoryService categoryService;
    private final InventoryClient inventoryClient;
    private final ApplicationEventPublisher eventPublisher;
    
    public ProductService(ProductRepository productRepository, 
                          CategoryService categoryService,
                          InventoryClient inventoryClient,
                          ApplicationEventPublisher eventPublisher) {
        this.productRepository = productRepository;
        this.categoryService = categoryService;
        this.inventoryClient = inventoryClient;
        this.eventPublisher = eventPublisher;
    }
    
    @Transactional(readOnly = true)
    public Page<Product> findAll(Pageable pageable, String category, BigDecimal minPrice) {
        if (category != null && minPrice != null) {
            return productRepository.findByCategoryAndMinPrice(category, minPrice, pageable);
        } else if (category != null) {
            return productRepository.findByCategoryName(category, pageable);
        } else if (minPrice != null) {
            return productRepository.findByPriceGreaterThanEqual(minPrice, pageable);
        }
        return productRepository.findAll(pageable);
    }
    
    @Transactional(readOnly = true)
    public Optional<Product> findById(UUID id) {
        return productRepository.findById(id);
    }
    
    public Product create(Product product) {
        // Validate category exists
        Category category = categoryService.findById(product.getCategoryId())
                .orElseThrow(() -> new InvalidRequestException("Invalid category"));
        
        product.setCategory(category);
        product.setCreatedAt(LocalDateTime.now());
        product.setUpdatedAt(LocalDateTime.now());
        
        Product saved = productRepository.save(product);
        
        // Publish domain event
        eventPublisher.publishEvent(new ProductCreatedEvent(saved));
        
        log.info("Product created: {} with id: {}", saved.getName(), saved.getId());
        return saved;
    }
    
    public Product update(UUID id, UpdateProductRequest request) {
        Product product = findById(id)
                .orElseThrow(() -> new ResourceNotFoundException("Product not found"));
        
        if (request.getName() != null) {
            product.setName(request.getName());
        }
        if (request.getDescription() != null) {
            product.setDescription(request.getDescription());
        }
        if (request.getPrice() != null) {
            product.setPrice(request.getPrice());
        }
        
        product.setUpdatedAt(LocalDateTime.now());
        return productRepository.save(product);
    }
    
    @Transactional(noRollbackFor = {InventoryUnavailableException.class})
    public void delete(UUID id) {
        Product product = findById(id)
                .orElseThrow(() -> new ResourceNotFoundException("Product not found"));
        
        // Check if product has any orders (synchronous call to order service)
        boolean hasOrders = orderService.hasOrdersForProduct(id);
        if (hasOrders) {
            throw new BusinessRuleViolationException("Cannot delete product with existing orders");
        }
        
        // Soft delete
        product.setDeleted(true);
        product.setUpdatedAt(LocalDateTime.now());
        productRepository.save(product);
        
        log.info("Product soft-deleted: {}", id);
    }
}

Step 6: Feign Client for Service-to-Service Communication
java
@FeignClient(
    name = "inventory-service",
    url = "${services.inventory.url:http://localhost:8081}",
    configuration = FeignConfig.class,
    fallback = InventoryFallback.class
)
public interface InventoryClient {
    
    @GetMapping("/api/v1/inventory/products/{productId}")
    ResponseEntity<InventoryResponse> getInventory(@PathVariable("productId") UUID productId);
    
    @PostMapping("/api/v1/inventory/reserve")
    ResponseEntity<ReservationResponse> reserveStock(@RequestBody ReserveStockRequest request);
    
    @PostMapping("/api/v1/inventory/release")
    ResponseEntity<Void> releaseStock(@RequestBody ReleaseStockRequest request);
}

// Feign Configuration
@Configuration
public class FeignConfig {
    
    @Bean
    public RequestInterceptor requestInterceptor() {
        return requestTemplate -> {
            // Propagate correlation ID
            String correlationId = MDC.get("correlationId");
            if (correlationId != null) {
                requestTemplate.header("X-Correlation-Id", correlationId);
            }
            
            // Propagate auth token
            Authentication auth = SecurityContextHolder.getContext().getAuthentication();
            if (auth != null && auth.getCredentials() instanceof String) {
                requestTemplate.header("Authorization", "Bearer " + auth.getCredentials());
            }
        };
    }
    
    @Bean
    public Decoder feignDecoder() {
        return new OptionalDecoder(new ResponseEntityDecoder(new JacksonDecoder()));
    }
    
    @Bean
    public ErrorDecoder errorDecoder() {
        return new RetryableErrorDecoder();
    }
}

// Fallback implementation
@Component
public class InventoryFallback implements InventoryClient {
    
    private static final Logger log = LoggerFactory.getLogger(InventoryFallback.class);
    
    @Override
    public ResponseEntity<InventoryResponse> getInventory(UUID productId) {
        log.warn("Inventory service unavailable for product: {}", productId);
        return ResponseEntity.status(HttpStatus.SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE).build();
    }
    
    @Override
    public ResponseEntity<ReservationResponse> reserveStock(ReserveStockRequest request) {
        log.error("Cannot reserve stock, inventory service unavailable for request: {}", request);
        throw new ServiceUnavailableException("Inventory service is currently unavailable");
    }
    
    @Override
    public ResponseEntity<Void> releaseStock(ReleaseStockRequest request) {
        log.warn("Cannot release stock, inventory service unavailable for request: {}", request);
        return ResponseEntity.status(HttpStatus.SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE).build();
    }
}

  circuitbreaker:
    instances:
      inventory-service:
        register-health-indicator: true
        sliding-window-size: 10
        minimum-number-of-calls: 5
        permitted-number-of-calls-in-half-open-state: 3
        automatic-transition-from-open-to-half-open-enabled: true
        wait-duration-in-open-state: 10s
        failure-rate-threshold: 50
        event-consumer-buffer-size: 10
  retry:
    instances:
      inventory-service:
        max-attempts: 3
        wait-duration: 1s
        retry-exceptions:
          - org.springframework.web.client.HttpServerErrorException
          - java.io.IOException
  bulkhead:
    instances:
      inventory-service:
        max-concurrent-calls: 10
        max-wait-duration: 10ms
  timelimiter:
    instances:
      inventory-service:
        timeout-duration: 3s
Service with Resilience Annotations
java
@Service
@Slf4j
public class InventoryIntegrationService {
    
    private final InventoryClient inventoryClient;
    
    @CircuitBreaker(name = "inventory-service", fallbackMethod = "getInventoryFallback")
    @Retry(name = "inventory-service")
    @TimeLimiter(name = "inventory-service")
    @Bulkhead(name = "inventory-service")
    public CompletableFuture<InventoryResponse> getInventory(UUID productId) {
        return CompletableFuture.supplyAsync(() -> {
            ResponseEntity<InventoryResponse> response = inventoryClient.getInventory(productId);
            return response.getBody();
        });
    }
    
    private CompletableFuture<InventoryResponse> getInventoryFallback(UUID productId, Exception ex) {
        log.warn("Fallback for inventory of product {} due to: {}", productId, ex.getMessage());
        return CompletableFuture.completedFuture(
            InventoryResponse.unavailable(productId)
        );
    }
}
Step 8: Global Exception Handling
java
@RestControllerAdvice
@Slf4j
public class GlobalExceptionHandler {
    
    @ExceptionHandler(ResourceNotFoundException.class)
    @ResponseStatus(HttpStatus.NOT_FOUND)
    public ErrorResponse handleNotFound(ResourceNotFoundException ex) {
        log.error("Resource not found: {}", ex.getMessage());
        return new ErrorResponse(
            HttpStatus.NOT_FOUND.value(),
            "NOT_FOUND",
            ex.getMessage(),
            LocalDateTime.now()
        );
    }
    
    @ExceptionHandler(InvalidRequestException.class)
    @ResponseStatus(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST)
    public ErrorResponse handleInvalidRequest(InvalidRequestException ex) {
        return new ErrorResponse(
            HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST.value(),
            "INVALID_REQUEST",
            ex.getMessage(),
            LocalDateTime.now()
        );
    }
    
    @ExceptionHandler(BusinessRuleViolationException.class)
    @ResponseStatus(HttpStatus.UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY)
    public ErrorResponse handleBusinessRuleViolation(BusinessRuleViolationException ex) {
        return new ErrorResponse(
            HttpStatus.UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY.value(),
            "BUSINESS_RULE_VIOLATION",
            ex.getMessage(),
            LocalDateTime.now()
        );
    }
    
    @ExceptionHandler(ServiceUnavailableException.class)
    @ResponseStatus(HttpStatus.SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE)
    public ErrorResponse handleServiceUnavailable(ServiceUnavailableException ex) {
        log.error("Service unavailable: {}", ex.getMessage());
        return new ErrorResponse(
            HttpStatus.SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE.value(),
            "SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE",
            "Dependency service is temporarily unavailable. Please try again later.",
            LocalDateTime.now()
        );
    }
    
    @ExceptionHandler(MethodArgumentNotValidException.class)
    @ResponseStatus(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST)
    public ValidationErrorResponse handleValidationExceptions(MethodArgumentNotValidException ex) {
        ValidationErrorResponse response = new ValidationErrorResponse();
        response.setStatus(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST.value());
        response.setTimestamp(LocalDateTime.now());
        
        ex.getBindingResult().getAllErrors().forEach(error -> {
            String fieldName = ((FieldError) error).getField();
            String errorMessage = error.getDefaultMessage();
            response.addViolation(fieldName, errorMessage);
        });
        
        return response;
    }
    
    @ExceptionHandler(Exception.class)
    @ResponseStatus(HttpStatus.INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR)
    public ErrorResponse handleGenericException(Exception ex) {
        log.error("Unexpected error", ex);
        return new ErrorResponse(
            HttpStatus.INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR.value(),
            "INTERNAL_ERROR",
            "An unexpected error occurred. Our team has been notified.",
            LocalDateTime.now()
        );
    }
}

// Error response DTOs
public class ErrorResponse {
    private int status;
    private String code;
    private String message;
    private LocalDateTime timestamp;
    private String path;
    private String correlationId;
    // constructors, getters
}

          uri: lb://product-service
          predicates:
            - Path=/api/v1/products/**
            - Method=GET,POST,PUT,DELETE
          filters:
            - name: CircuitBreaker
              args:
                name: productService
                fallbackUri: forward:/fallback/products
            - name: Retry
              args:
                retries: 3
                statuses: SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE
            - name: RequestRateLimiter
              args:
                redis-rate-limiter.replenishRate: 10
                redis-rate-limiter.burstCapacity: 20
            - name: RewritePath
              args:
                regexp: '/api/v1/(?<segment>.*)'
                replacement: '/${segment}'
        
        - id: order-service
          uri: lb://order-service
          predicates:
            - Path=/api/v1/orders/**
          filters:
            - name: RequestHeaderToRequestUri
            - DedupeResponseHeader=Access-Control-Allow-Credentials Access-Control-Allow-Origin

Step 10: Security with JWT
java
@Component
public class JwtAuthFilter extends OncePerRequestFilter {
    
    @Autowired
    private JwtTokenProvider tokenProvider;
    
    @Override
    protected void doFilterInternal(HttpServletRequest request, 
                                   HttpServletResponse response,
                                   FilterChain filterChain) throws ServletException, IOException {
        
        String token = extractJwtFromRequest(request);
        
        if (StringUtils.hasText(token) && tokenProvider.validateToken(token)) {
            Authentication auth = tokenProvider.getAuthentication(token);
            SecurityContextHolder.getContext().setAuthentication(auth);
            
            // Add correlation ID
            String correlationId = request.getHeader("X-Correlation-Id");
            if (correlationId == null) {
                correlationId = UUID.randomUUID().toString();
            }
            MDC.put("correlationId", correlationId);
            MDC.put("userId", auth.getName());
        }
        
        filterChain.doFilter(request, response);
        
        // Clean up MDC
        MDC.clear();
    }
    
    private String extractJwtFromRequest(HttpServletRequest request) {
        String bearerToken = request.getHeader("Authorization");
        if (StringUtils.hasText(bearerToken) && bearerToken.startsWith("Bearer ")) {
            return bearerToken.substring(7);
        }
        return null;
    }
}

    public void delete(UUID id) {

    }

        // clear all product cache
    }
}
java
@Configuration
@EnableCaching
public class CacheConfig {
    
    @Bean
    public CacheManager cacheManager() {
        CaffeineCacheManager cacheManager = new CaffeineCacheManager("products", "categories");
        cacheManager.setCaffeine(Caffeine.newBuilder()
            .initialCapacity(100)
            .maximumSize(1000)
            .expireAfterWrite(10, TimeUnit.MINUTES)
            .recordStats());
        return cacheManager;
    }
}

Testing Strategy
java

@SpringBootTest

@Testcontainers
class ProductControllerIntegrationTest {

    static PostgreSQLContainer<?> postgres = new PostgreSQLContainer<>("postgres:13")
            .withDatabaseName("testdb")
            .withUsername("test")
            .withPassword("test");

    private MockMvc mockMvc;
    
    @Test
    void testCreateProduct() throws Exception {
        CreateProductRequest request = new CreateProductRequest();
        request.setName("Test Product");
        request.setPrice(new BigDecimal("99.99"));
        request.setCategoryId(UUID.randomUUID());
        
        mockMvc.perform(post("/api/v1/products")
                .contentType(MediaType.APPLICATION_JSON)
                .content(asJsonString(request)))
                .andExpect(status().isCreated())
                .andExpect(jsonPath("$.name").value("Test Product"));
    }
