        mavenBom "org.springframework.cloud:spring-cloud-dependencies:${springCloudVersion}"
    }
}
Step 2: YAML Configuration (application.yml)
yaml
server:
  port: 8080

spring:
  application:
    name: order-service

resilience4j:
  circuitbreaker:
    instances:
      # Configuration for inventory service circuit breaker
      inventory-service:
        # Register circuit breaker in health indicator
        register-health-indicator: true
        
        # Sliding window configuration
        sliding-window-type: COUNT_BASED
        sliding-window-size: 10
        minimum-number-of-calls: 5
        
        # Failure rate threshold (percentage)
        failure-rate-threshold: 50
        
        # Wait duration in OPEN state before transitioning to HALF-OPEN
        wait-duration-in-open-state: 10s
        
        # HALF-OPEN configuration
        permitted-number-of-calls-in-half-open-state: 3
        automatic-transition-from-open-to-half-open-enabled: true
        
        # Record exceptions
        record-exceptions:
          - org.springframework.web.client.HttpServerErrorException
          - java.io.IOException
          - java.util.concurrent.TimeoutException
        
        # Ignore exceptions (don't count as failures)
        ignore-exceptions:
          - com.example.exception.BusinessException
          - org.springframework.web.client.HttpClientErrorException.BadRequest
        
        # Event consumer buffer size
        event-consumer-buffer-size: 10
        
        # Slow call configuration
        slow-call-rate-threshold: 100
        slow-call-duration-threshold: 5s
        
      # Configuration for payment service circuit breaker
      payment-service:
        register-health-indicator: true
        sliding-window-type: TIME_BASED
        sliding-window-size: 60  # 60 seconds
        minimum-number-of-calls: 10
        failure-rate-threshold: 30
        wait-duration-in-open-state: 30s
        permitted-number-of-calls-in-half-open-state: 5
        automatic-transition-from-open-to-half-open-enabled: true
        
      # Configuration for shipping service circuit breaker
      shipping-service:
        register-health-indicator: true
        sliding-window-type: COUNT_BASED
        sliding-window-size: 20
        minimum-number-of-calls: 10
        failure-rate-threshold: 60
        wait-duration-in-open-state: 15s
        permitted-number-of-calls-in-half-open-state: 2
        automatic-transition-from-open-to-half-open-enabled: false  # Manual transition only

  timelimiter:
    instances:
      inventory-service:
        timeout-duration: 3s
        cancel-running-future: true
      payment-service:
        timeout-duration: 5s
      shipping-service:
        timeout-duration: 10s

  retry:
    instances:
      inventory-service:
        max-attempts: 3
        wait-duration: 1s
        retry-exceptions:
          - java.io.IOException
          - org.springframework.web.client.HttpServerErrorException

  bulkhead:
    instances:
      inventory-service:
        max-concurrent-calls: 10
        max-wait-duration: 10ms

management:
  endpoints:
    web:
      exposure:
        include: health,circuitbreakers,circuitbreakerevents,metrics,prometheus
      base-path: /actuator
  endpoint:
    health:
      show-details: always
    circuitbreakers:
      enabled: true
    circuitbreakerevents:
      enabled: true
  metrics:
    export:
      prometheus:
        enabled: true

logging:
  level:
    io.github.resilience4j: DEBUG
    com.example: DEBUG
Step 3: Service with Circuit Breaker
java
package com.example.order.service;

import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.annotation.CircuitBreaker;
import io.github.resilience4j.retry.annotation.Retry;
import io.github.resilience4j.timelimiter.annotation.TimeLimiter;
import io.github.resilience4j.bulkhead.annotation.Bulkhead;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;
import org.springframework.web.client.RestTemplate;

import java.util.concurrent.CompletableFuture;

@Service
@Slf4j
public class InventoryIntegrationService {
    
    @Autowired
    private RestTemplate restTemplate;
    
    @Autowired
    private InventoryFallbackService fallbackService;
    
    /**
     * Synchronous call with Circuit Breaker
     * 
     * State Machine:
     * CLOSED: Calls pass through, failures counted
     * OPEN: Calls fail immediately, no attempt
     * HALF-OPEN: Limited calls allowed to test recovery
     */
    @CircuitBreaker(name = "inventory-service", fallbackMethod = "getInventoryFallback")
    @Retry(name = "inventory-service")  // Retry before circuit opens
    @TimeLimiter(name = "inventory-service")  // Timeout
    public InventoryResponse getInventorySync(String productId) {
        log.info("Calling inventory service for product: {}", productId);
        
        String url = "http://inventory-service/api/inventory/" + productId;
        InventoryResponse response = restTemplate.getForObject(url, InventoryResponse.class);
        
        log.info("Inventory response received: {}", response);
        return response;
    }
    
    /**
     * Asynchronous call with Circuit Breaker
     */
    @CircuitBreaker(name = "inventory-service", fallbackMethod = "getInventoryAsyncFallback")
    @TimeLimiter(name = "inventory-service")
    public CompletableFuture<InventoryResponse> getInventoryAsync(String productId) {
        log.info("Async calling inventory service for product: {}", productId);
        
        return CompletableFuture.supplyAsync(() -> {
            String url = "http://inventory-service/api/inventory/" + productId;
            return restTemplate.getForObject(url, InventoryResponse.class);
        });
    }
    
    /**
     * With Bulkhead (limits concurrent calls)
     */
    @CircuitBreaker(name = "inventory-service", fallbackMethod = "getInventoryFallback")
    @Bulkhead(name = "inventory-service", type = Bulkhead.Type.SEMAPHORE)
    public InventoryResponse getInventoryWithBulkhead(String productId) {
        String url = "http://inventory-service/api/inventory/" + productId;
        return restTemplate.getForObject(url, InventoryResponse.class);
    }
    
    /**
     * Fallback method when circuit is OPEN or call fails
     * Must have same signature as original method + Exception parameter
     */
    private InventoryResponse getInventoryFallback(String productId, Exception ex) {
        log.warn("Circuit breaker fallback triggered for product: {}. Reason: {}", 
                 productId, ex.getMessage());
        
        // Return cached or default response
        return InventoryResponse.fallback(productId, "Inventory service temporarily unavailable");
    }
    
    /**
     * Async fallback
     */
    private CompletableFuture<InventoryResponse> getInventoryAsyncFallback(String productId, Exception ex) {
        log.warn("Async fallback triggered for product: {}", productId);
        return CompletableFuture.completedFuture(
            InventoryResponse.fallback(productId, "Service unavailable")
        );
    }
}
Step 4: REST Controller with Circuit Breaker Monitoring
java
package com.example.order.controller;

import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreaker;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreakerRegistry;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.event.CircuitBreakerEvent;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.event.CircuitBreakerOnStateTransitionEvent;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;

import javax.annotation.PostConstruct;
import java.util.HashMap;
import java.util.Map;

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/orders")
@Slf4j
public class OrderController {
    
    @Autowired
    private InventoryIntegrationService inventoryService;
    
    @Autowired
    private CircuitBreakerRegistry circuitBreakerRegistry;
    
    @GetMapping("/inventory/{productId}")
    public Map<String, Object> checkInventory(@PathVariable String productId) {
        long startTime = System.currentTimeMillis();
        
        InventoryResponse response = inventoryService.getInventorySync(productId);
        
        long duration = System.currentTimeMillis() - startTime;
        
        Map<String, Object> result = new HashMap<>();
        result.put("productId", productId);
        result.put("inventory", response);
        result.put("responseTimeMs", duration);
        result.put("circuitBreakerState", getCircuitBreakerState("inventory-service"));
        
        return result;
    }
    
    @GetMapping("/circuit-breaker/state")
    public Map<String, String> getCircuitBreakerStates() {
        Map<String, String> states = new HashMap<>();
        
        states.put("inventory-service", getCircuitBreakerState("inventory-service"));
        states.put("payment-service", getCircuitBreakerState("payment-service"));
        states.put("shipping-service", getCircuitBreakerState("shipping-service"));
        
        return states;
    }
    
    @PostMapping("/circuit-breaker/reset")
    public Map<String, String> resetCircuitBreaker(@RequestParam String name) {
        circuitBreakerRegistry.circuitBreaker(name).reset();
        
        Map<String, String> result = new HashMap<>();
        result.put("status", "reset");
        result.put("circuitBreaker", name);
        result.put("newState", getCircuitBreakerState(name));
        
        return result;
    }
    
    @PostMapping("/circuit-breaker/transition")
    public Map<String, String> transitionCircuitBreaker(@RequestParam String name, 
                                                         @RequestParam String state) {
        CircuitBreaker circuitBreaker = circuitBreakerRegistry.circuitBreaker(name);
        
        switch (state.toUpperCase()) {
            case "OPEN":
                circuitBreaker.transitionToOpenState();
                break;
            case "CLOSED":
                circuitBreaker.transitionToClosedState();
                break;
            case "HALF_OPEN":
                circuitBreaker.transitionToHalfOpenState();
                break;
            case "FORCED_OPEN":
                circuitBreaker.transitionToForcedOpenState();
                break;
            case "DISABLED":
                circuitBreaker.transitionToDisabledState();
                break;
            default:
                throw new IllegalArgumentException("Invalid state: " + state);
        }
        
        Map<String, String> result = new HashMap<>();
        result.put("status", "transitioned");
        result.put("circuitBreaker", name);
        result.put("newState", getCircuitBreakerState(name));
        
        return result;
    }
    
    @GetMapping("/circuit-breaker/metrics")
    public Map<String, Object> getCircuitBreakerMetrics(@RequestParam String name) {
        CircuitBreaker circuitBreaker = circuitBreakerRegistry.circuitBreaker(name);
        CircuitBreaker.Metrics metrics = circuitBreaker.getMetrics();
        
        Map<String, Object> result = new HashMap<>();
        result.put("name", name);
        result.put("state", circuitBreaker.getState().name());
        result.put("failureRate", metrics.getFailureRate());
        result.put("slowCallRate", metrics.getSlowCallRate());
        result.put("numberOfBufferedCalls", metrics.getNumberOfBufferedCalls());
        result.put("numberOfFailedCalls", metrics.getNumberOfFailedCalls());
        result.put("numberOfSuccessfulCalls", metrics.getNumberOfSuccessfulCalls());
        result.put("numberOfSlowCalls", metrics.getNumberOfSlowCalls());
        result.put("numberOfSlowFailedCalls", metrics.getNumberOfSlowFailedCalls());
        result.put("numberOfSlowSuccessfulCalls", metrics.getNumberOfSlowSuccessfulCalls());
        
        return result;
    }
    
    private String getCircuitBreakerState(String name) {
        return circuitBreakerRegistry.circuitBreaker(name).getState().name();
    }
    
    @PostConstruct
    public void registerEventListeners() {
        // Register event listener for circuit breaker state changes
        circuitBreakerRegistry.getAllCircuitBreakers().forEach(circuitBreaker -> {
            circuitBreaker.getEventPublisher()
                .onStateTransition(event -> {
                    log.info("Circuit Breaker '{}' transitioned from {} to {}", 
                             event.getCircuitBreakerName(),
                             event.getStateTransition().getFromState(),
                             event.getStateTransition().getToState());
                })
                .onFailureRateExceeded(event -> {
                    log.warn("Circuit Breaker '{}' failure rate exceeded: {}%", 
                             event.getCircuitBreakerName(), 
                             event.getFailureRate());
                })
                .onCallNotPermitted(event -> {
                    log.warn("Circuit Breaker '{}' is OPEN, call not permitted", 
                             event.getCircuitBreakerName());
                })
                .onError(event -> {
                    log.error("Circuit Breaker '{}' recorded an error", 
                              event.getCircuitBreakerName(), event.getThrowable());
                });
        });
    }
}
Step 5: Feign Client with Circuit Breaker
java
package com.example.order.client;

import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.annotation.CircuitBreaker;
import org.springframework.cloud.openfeign.FeignClient;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;

@FeignClient(
    name = "inventory-service",
    url = "${services.inventory.url:http://localhost:8081}",
    fallback = InventoryClientFallback.class
)
public interface InventoryClient {
    
    @GetMapping("/api/inventory/{productId}")
    InventoryResponse getInventory(@PathVariable("productId") String productId);
    
    @PostMapping("/api/inventory/reserve")
    ReservationResponse reserveStock(@RequestBody ReserveStockRequest request);
    
    @PostMapping("/api/inventory/release")
    void releaseStock(@RequestBody ReleaseStockRequest request);
}

// Fallback implementation
@Component
@Slf4j
class InventoryClientFallback implements InventoryClient {
    
    @Override
    public InventoryResponse getInventory(String productId) {
        log.warn("Fallback: Inventory service unavailable for product: {}", productId);
        return InventoryResponse.fallback(productId, "Service unavailable - using cached data");
    }
    
    @Override
    public ReservationResponse reserveStock(ReserveStockRequest request) {
        log.error("Fallback: Cannot reserve stock, service unavailable for request: {}", request);
        throw new ServiceUnavailableException("Inventory service is currently unavailable");
    }
    
    @Override
    public void releaseStock(ReleaseStockRequest request) {
        log.warn("Fallback: Cannot release stock, service unavailable for request: {}", request);
        // Log but don't throw - this is a compensation action
    }
}
Step 6: Programmatic Circuit Breaker Configuration
java
package com.example.order.config;

import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreaker;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreakerConfig;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreakerRegistry;
import io.github.resilience4j.timelimiter.TimeLimiter;
import io.github.resilience4j.timelimiter.TimeLimiterConfig;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;

import java.io.IOException;
import java.time.Duration;
import java.util.function.Supplier;

@Configuration
public class CircuitBreakerProgrammaticConfig {
    
    @Bean
    public CircuitBreakerRegistry circuitBreakerRegistry() {
        // Custom configuration for product service
        CircuitBreakerConfig productServiceConfig = CircuitBreakerConfig.custom()
            .slidingWindowType(CircuitBreakerConfig.SlidingWindowType.COUNT_BASED)
            .slidingWindowSize(20)
            .minimumNumberOfCalls(10)
            .failureRateThreshold(40)
            .waitDurationInOpenState(Duration.ofSeconds(15))
            .permittedNumberOfCallsInHalfOpenState(5)
            .automaticTransitionFromOpenToHalfOpenEnabled(true)
            .recordExceptions(IOException.class, TimeoutException.class)
            .ignoreExceptions(BusinessException.class)
            .slowCallRateThreshold(80)
            .slowCallDurationThreshold(Duration.ofSeconds(4))
            .build();
        
        return CircuitBreakerRegistry.of(productServiceConfig);
    }
    
    @Bean
    public CircuitBreaker customCircuitBreaker(CircuitBreakerRegistry registry) {
        return registry.circuitBreaker("custom-service");
    }
    
    // Example of using circuit breaker programmatically
    public <T> T executeWithCircuitBreaker(String circuitBreakerName, Supplier<T> supplier, Supplier<T> fallback) {
        CircuitBreaker circuitBreaker = circuitBreakerRegistry().circuitBreaker(circuitBreakerName);
        
        return circuitBreaker.executeSupplier(() -> {
            try {
                return supplier.get();
            } catch (Exception e) {
                // Circuit breaker will record this as failure
                throw new RuntimeException(e);
            }
        }, throwable -> fallback.get());
    }
}
Step 7: Complete State Transition Example
java
package com.example.order.demo;

import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreaker;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreakerConfig;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;

import java.time.Duration;
import java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicInteger;

@Slf4j
public class CircuitBreakerStateDemo {
    
    public static void main(String[] args) throws InterruptedException {
        // Configure circuit breaker
        CircuitBreakerConfig config = CircuitBreakerConfig.custom()
            .slidingWindowSize(5)
            .minimumNumberOfCalls(3)
            .failureRateThreshold(50)  // 50% failure rate opens circuit
            .waitDurationInOpenState(Duration.ofSeconds(5))
            .permittedNumberOfCallsInHalfOpenState(2)
            .build();
        
        CircuitBreaker circuitBreaker = CircuitBreaker.of("demo-service", config);
        
        AtomicInteger callCount = new AtomicInteger(0);
        
        log.info("=== Circuit Breaker State Transition Demo ===");
        
        // Simulate calls
        for (int i = 1; i <= 20; i++) {
            final int callNum = i;
            
            // Decorate supplier with circuit breaker
            Supplier<String> decoratedSupplier = CircuitBreaker
                .decorateSupplier(circuitBreaker, () -> {
                    callCount.incrementAndGet();
                    
                    // Simulate failures for first 5 calls
                    if (callCount.get() <= 5) {
                        log.info("Call {}: SIMULATING FAILURE", callNum);
                        throw new RuntimeException("Service failed");
                    }
                    // After half-open, simulate success
                    else {
                        log.info("Call {}: SUCCESS", callNum);
                        return "Success response";
                    }
                });
            
            try {
                String result = decoratedSupplier.get();
                log.info("Call {} result: {}", callNum, result);
            } catch (Exception e) {
                log.error("Call {} failed: {}", callNum, e.getMessage());
            }
            
            // Print current state
            log.info("Circuit Breaker State: {}", circuitBreaker.getState());
            
            // Wait between calls
            Thread.sleep(1000);
        }
        
        // Print metrics
        CircuitBreaker.Metrics metrics = circuitBreaker.getMetrics();
        log.info("=== Final Metrics ===");
        log.info("Failure Rate: {}%", metrics.getFailureRate());
        log.info("Buffered Calls: {}", metrics.getNumberOfBufferedCalls());
        log.info("Failed Calls: {}", metrics.getNumberOfFailedCalls());
        log.info("Successful Calls: {}", metrics.getNumberOfSuccessfulCalls());
    }
}

📈 State Transition Diagram with Metrics
text
CLOSED State (Normal Operation)
├── Calls: 100% allowed
├── Metrics being collected: sliding window of 10 calls
├── Current failure rate: 20%
└── Threshold: 50% → If failure rate exceeds 50%, transition to OPEN

    │
    ▼ (Failure Rate > 50%)

OPEN State (Circuit Tripped)
├── Calls: 0% allowed (all fail fast)
├── Wait duration: 10 seconds
├── No calls to downstream service
└── Fallback responses returned immediately

    │
    ▼ (After 10 seconds)

HALF-OPEN State (Testing Recovery)
├── Calls: Limited to 3 permitted calls
├── Behavior: 
│   ├── If any call fails → back to OPEN (reset timer)
│   └── If all 3 calls succeed → transition to CLOSED
└── Slow recovery: Can test with 1-2 calls initially

    │
    ▼ (3 successful calls)

CLOSED State (Recovered)
├── Normal operation resumes
├── Metrics reset
└── Failure count starts fresh
🎯 Best Practices Summary
Aspect	Recommendation
Sliding Window	Use COUNT_BASED for low-volume APIs, TIME_BASED for high-volume
Failure Threshold	Start with 50%, adjust based on your SLAs
Wait Duration	Set based on service recovery time (typical: 10-30 seconds)
Half-Open Calls	2-5 calls is usually sufficient
Timeout	Always set lower than client timeout
Fallback	Provide meaningful fallback (cached data, default values)
Monitoring	Expose metrics via actuator + Prometheus
🔍 Testing Circuit Breaker Behavior
java
@SpringBootTest
@AutoConfigureMockMvc
class CircuitBreakerIntegrationTest {
    
    @Autowired
    private MockMvc mockMvc;
    
    @Test
    void testCircuitBreakerOpensAfterFailures() throws Exception {
        // Make 5 failing calls
        for (int i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
            mockMvc.perform(get("/api/orders/inventory/product-fail"))
                   .andExpect(status().is5xxServerError());
        }
        
        // Wait for circuit to open
        Thread.sleep(500);
        
        // Next call should use fallback (circuit open)
        mockMvc.perform(get("/api/orders/inventory/product-any"))
               .andExpect(status().isOk())
               .andExpect(jsonPath("$.inventory.status").value("UNAVAILABLE"));
    }
}
This complete implementation gives you a production-ready circuit breaker with proper state management, monitoring, and fallback strategies!

How to make microservice backward compatible using spring boot and java, kafka?
Read 10 web pages
Achieving backward compatibility in event-driven microservices is critical because producers and consumers evolve independently and cannot be deployed simultaneously. In a distributed system, your producer deploys today, but some consumers may not update for days or weeks. If you break compatibility, old consumers will crash or misprocess events.

The golden rule from industry leaders like Netflix and Uber is: Producers must always be backward compatible; consumers must always be forward compatible.

The Core Problem: Schema Evolution
When your event schema changes, old consumers must still understand new events, and new consumers must handle old events still in Kafka.











Strategy 1: Additive Changes Only (The Easiest Path)
The simplest way to maintain backward compatibility is to only add optional fields and never remove or rename existing ones.

Safe Changes
json
// Original v1 event
{
  "orderId": 12345,
  "customerId": 67890,
  "totalAmount": 99.99
}

// v2 event - SAFE (added optional fields)
{
  "orderId": 12345,
  "customerId": 67890,
  "totalAmount": 99.99,
  "discountCode": "SAVE10",     // new optional field
  "priority": "HIGH"             // new optional field
}
Breaking Changes (NEVER Do)
json
// DANGER - Will crash consumers
{
  "orderRef": 12345,        // renamed from orderId
  "customerRef": 67890,     // renamed from customerId
  "amount": 99.99           // renamed from totalAmount
}
Strategy 2: Configure Jackson to Ignore Unknown Fields
In Spring Boot, configure your ObjectMapper to tolerate unknown fields. This single line prevents 80% of consumer crashes.

java
@Configuration
public class KafkaConsumerConfig {
    
    @Bean
    public ObjectMapper objectMapper() {
        return new ObjectMapper()
            .configure(DeserializationFeature.FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES, false)
            .registerModule(new JavaTimeModule());
    }
    
    @Bean
    public ConsumerFactory<String, Object> consumerFactory() {
        Map<String, Object> props = new HashMap<>();
        props.put(ConsumerConfig.BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS_CONFIG, "localhost:9092");
        props.put(ConsumerConfig.GROUP_ID_CONFIG, "order-consumer-group");
        props.put(ConsumerConfig.KEY_DESERIALIZER_CLASS_CONFIG, StringDeserializer.class);
        props.put(ConsumerConfig.VALUE_DESERIALIZER_CLASS_CONFIG, JsonDeserializer.class);
        props.put(JsonDeserializer.TRUSTED_PACKAGES, "*");
        props.put(JsonDeserializer.VALUE_DEFAULT_TYPE, "com.example.OrderEvent");
        
        return new DefaultKafkaConsumerFactory<>(props);
    }
}
Strategy 3: Version Field Inside Event (Recommended)
Embed a version number in your event payload. This allows consumers to handle multiple versions gracefully.

Event Class with Version
java
@Data
@AllArgsConstructor
@NoArgsConstructor
public class OrderEvent {
    private Integer version;      // Version field first!
    private Long orderId;
    private String status;
    private String priority;      // Added in v2
    private String paymentMethod; // Added in v3
    
    // Constructors, getters, setters
}
Producer: Always Include Version
java
@Service
@Slf4j
public class OrderEventProducer {
    
    @Autowired
    private KafkaTemplate<String, OrderEvent> kafkaTemplate;
    
    public void sendOrderEvent(Long orderId, String status) {
        OrderEvent event = new OrderEvent();
        event.setVersion(2);              // Current version
        event.setOrderId(orderId);
        event.setStatus(status);
        event.setPriority("NORMAL");      // v2 field
        
        kafkaTemplate.send("order-events", event);
        log.info("Sent event v{}: {}", event.getVersion(), event);
    }
}
Consumer: Handle Multiple Versions
java
@Component
@Slf4j
public class OrderEventConsumer {
    
    @KafkaListener(topics = "order-events", groupId = "order-processing-group")
    public void consume(OrderEvent event) {
        log.info("Received event version: {}", event.getVersion());
        
        switch (event.getVersion()) {
            case 1:
                processV1(event);
                break;
            case 2:
                processV2(event);
                break;
            case 3:
                processV3(event);
                break;
            default:
                log.warn("Unknown version {}, using latest processor", event.getVersion());
                processLatest(event);
        }
    }
    
    private void processV1(OrderEvent event) {
        // Only use v1 fields (orderId, status)
        log.info("V1 processing: Order {} status {}", event.getOrderId(), event.getStatus());
    }
    
    private void processV2(OrderEvent event) {
        // Use v2 fields including priority
        log.info("V2 processing: Order {} status {} priority {}", 
                 event.getOrderId(), event.getStatus(), event.getPriority());
    }
    
    private void processV3(OrderEvent event) {
        // Use all available fields
        log.info("V3 processing: Order {} status {} priority {} payment {}", 
                 event.getOrderId(), event.getStatus(), event.getPriority(), event.getPaymentMethod());
    }
}
Strategy 4: Avro with Schema Registry
For stricter contract management, use Avro serialization with Confluent Schema Registry. This provides automatic compatibility validation.

Avro Schema (v1)
avro
{
  "type": "record",
  "name": "OrderEvent",
  "namespace": "com.example",
  "fields": [
    { "name": "orderId", "type": "long" },
    { "name": "customerId", "type": "long" },
    { "name": "status", "type": "string" }
  ]
}
Avro Schema (v2 - Backward Compatible)
avro
{
  "type": "record",
  "name": "OrderEvent",
  "namespace": "com.example",
  "fields": [
    { "name": "orderId", "type": "long" },
    { "name": "customerId", "type": "long" },
    { "name": "status", "type": "string" },
    { "name": "priority", "type": ["null", "string"], "default": null }
  ]
}
Dependencies for Avro
gradle
dependencies {
    implementation 'org.springframework.kafka:spring-kafka'
    implementation 'io.confluent:kafka-avro-serializer:7.5.0'
    implementation 'org.apache.avro:avro:1.11.1'
}
Producer Configuration with Schema Registry
yaml
spring:
  kafka:
    properties:
      schema.registry.url: http://localhost:8081
    producer:
      key-serializer: org.apache.kafka.common.serialization.StringSerializer
      value-serializer: io.confluent.kafka.serializers.KafkaAvroSerializer
Consumer Configuration
yaml
spring:
  kafka:
    properties:
      schema.registry.url: http://localhost:8081
    consumer:
      key-deserializer: org.apache.kafka.common.serialization.StringDeserializer
      value-deserializer: io.confluent.kafka.serializers.KafkaAvroDeserializer
      properties:
        specific.avro.reader: true
Strategy 5: Topic Versioning (For Breaking Changes)
When you must make breaking changes (rare), create a new topic with a version suffix.

text
orders.v1    ← Old consumers listen here
orders.v2    ← New consumers listen here (migrated)
Producer Writing to Multiple Topics During Migration
java
@Service
@Slf4j
public class MigrationProducer {
    
    @Autowired
    private KafkaTemplate<String, Object> kafkaTemplate;
    
    /**
     * During migration period, write to BOTH topics
     */
    public void sendDuringMigration(OrderEvent event) {
        // Write to v1 topic (for old consumers)
        kafkaTemplate.send("orders.v1", event.getOrderId().toString(), event);
        
        // Write to v2 topic (for new consumers)
        kafkaTemplate.send("orders.v2", event.getOrderId().toString(), event);
        
        log.info("Event written to both topics: {}", event.getOrderId());
    }
}
Strategy 6: Dead Letter Queue for Failed Events
When a consumer encounters an unprocessable event, send it to a Dead Letter Topic (DLT) instead of crashing.

Consumer with Error Handler
java
@Component
@Slf4j
public class ResilientConsumer {
    
    @KafkaListener(
        topics = "order-events",
        groupId = "order-group",
        errorHandler = "deadLetterErrorHandler"
    )
    public void consume(OrderEvent event) {
        // Processing logic
    }
    
    @Bean
    public KafkaListenerErrorHandler deadLetterErrorHandler() {
        return (message, exception) -> {
            log.error("Failed to process message: {}", message.getPayload(), exception);
            
            // Send to DLT
            kafkaTemplate.send("order-events.DLT", message.getPayload());
            
            // Return null to acknowledge and continue
            return null;
        };
    }
}
Strategy 7: Contract Testing with Spring Cloud Contract
Use consumer-driven contract testing to validate compatibility before deployment.

Consumer-Side Contract (Groovy DSL)
groovy
// In consumer service: src/test/resources/contracts/orderEvent.groovy
org.springframework.cloud.contract.spec.Contract.make {
    description("Order event contract v1")
    
    input {
        triggeredBy("orderPlaced()")
    }
    
    outputMessage {
        sentTo("order-events")
        body([
            version: 1,
            orderId: 12345,
            customerId: 67890,
            status: "PLACED"
        ])
        headers {
            header("contentType", "application/json")
        }
    }
}
Producer Verifies Contract
gradle
// In producer service build.gradle
contracts {
    contractDependency {
        stringNotation = "com.example:order-consumer-contracts:1.0.0"
    }
    contractsMode = "REMOTE"
}
Complete 4-Phase Rollout Plan
Industry best practice for safe deployment:

Phase	Action	Risk	Duration
Phase 1	Add new fields (code supports both old and new). Producer emits both versions.	Very Low	Day 1
Phase 2	Update consumers to handle v2 using version field. Monitor for errors.	Low	Days 1-7
Phase 3	Switch producers to new field logic. Old fields remain but unused.	Medium	Day 7
Phase 4	Remove old fields after 100% of consumers upgraded (2-8 weeks).	Low	Week 8
Compatibility Checklist
Action	Backward Compatible	Forward Compatible
Add optional field	✅	✅
Add required field	❌	❌
Remove field	❌	❌
Rename field	❌	❌
Change field type	❌	❌
Make optional → required	❌	❌
Make required → optional	✅	✅
Add enum value	✅	Requires handling
Remove enum value	❌	❌
Summary: Choose Your Strategy
Strategy	Complexity	Best For
Additive changes only	Low	Simple, fast-moving teams
Version field in payload	Medium	Most use cases (recommended)
Avro + Schema Registry	High	Strict governance, large teams
Topic versioning	Medium	Breaking changes (rare)
Contract testing	Medium	Multiple consumer teams
The most practical approach for most teams: Version field in payload + Jackson ignore unknown properties + additive changes only. This gives you safe evolution without complex infrastructure.

How to define and document microservice contracts, spring boot, java, rest api, open swagger api?
Read 10 web pages
Defining and documenting microservice contracts is a critical practice for ensuring that teams can work in parallel and services can evolve independently. For a Java 8 and Spring Boot stack using REST APIs, the most effective approach is to adopt an OpenAPI-first strategy with a tool like SpringDoc OpenAPI. This approach uses the OpenAPI Specification (formerly known as Swagger) to create a single source of truth for your API contract .

🧩 What is a Microservice Contract?
A microservice contract is a formal, agreed-upon description of a service's API. It acts as the "glue" between a provider and its consumers, detailing the exact requests a service accepts and the responses it returns.

Think of it as a legal agreement:

Provider: Promises to adhere to the contract.

Consumer: Can rely on the contract to build its logic.

The contract defines the inputs (HTTP method, path, headers, query parameters, request body schema) and the outputs (HTTP status codes, response body schema, response headers) of an API endpoint .

🛠️ Choosing the Right Tooling for Java 8 & Spring Boot
For your specific stack (Java 8, Spring Boot), you have two main generations of tools. The recommended path is SpringDoc OpenAPI.

Aspect	SpringFox (Swagger 2)	SpringDoc OpenAPI (Recommended)
Maintenance	Stale/Inactive (last major release was in 2020) 	Actively Maintained by the Spring community 
Specification	Generates Swagger 2.0 (older spec)	Generates OpenAPI 3.0 (modern, feature-rich spec) 
Ease of Use	Requires explicit configuration (e.g., Docket beans)	Zero-config auto-configuration for most Spring Boot apps 
Annotations	Uses @Api, @ApiOperation, etc. 	Uses @Tag, @Operation, etc. (but can also read many older annotations) 
Decision: Given that SpringFox is no longer actively maintained, you should use SpringDoc OpenAPI for all new projects on Java 8 and Spring Boot .

🚀 Hands-On: Defining a Contract with SpringDoc OpenAPI
Here is a practical guide to defining your contract in code.

Step 1: Add the Dependency
Add the following dependency to your pom.xml (Maven) or build.gradle file.

For Spring Web MVC (spring-boot-starter-web) :

xml
<dependency>
    <groupId>org.springdoc</groupId>
    <artifactId>springdoc-openapi-ui</artifactId>
    <version>1.7.0</version> <!--- Check for the latest 1.x version -->
</dependency>
This single dependency automatically generates the OpenAPI documentation and includes the Swagger UI web interface .

Step 2: Write Your Contract-First Code
You can define the contract by annotating your REST controllers and DTOs. SpringDoc will automatically read these annotations to generate the OpenAPI JSON.

java
import io.swagger.v3.oas.annotations.Operation;
import io.swagger.v3.oas.annotations.Parameter;
import io.swagger.v3.oas.annotations.media.Content;
import io.swagger.v3.oas.annotations.media.Schema;
import io.swagger.v3.oas.annotations.responses.ApiResponse;
import io.swagger.v3.oas.annotations.responses.ApiResponses;
import io.swagger.v3.oas.annotations.tags.Tag;
import org.springframework.http.HttpStatus;
import org.springframework.http.ResponseEntity;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;

import jakarta.validation.Valid;
import jakarta.validation.constraints.NotNull;

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/v1/users")
@Tag(name = "User Management", description = "Endpoints for creating and managing users")
public class UserController {

    @PostMapping
    @Operation(summary = "Create a new user", description = "Creates a user account with the provided details.")
    @ApiResponses(value = {
        @ApiResponse(responseCode = "201", description = "User created successfully",
                     content = @Content(mediaType = "application/json",
                                        schema = @Schema(implementation = UserResponse.class))),
        @ApiResponse(responseCode = "400", description = "Invalid input provided",
                     content = @Content),
        @ApiResponse(responseCode = "409", description = "User with this email already exists",
                     content = @Content)
    })
    public ResponseEntity<UserResponse> createUser(
            @Valid @RequestBody CreateUserRequest request) {
        // ... your implementation
        UserResponse response = new UserResponse(1L, request.getEmail());
        return ResponseEntity.status(HttpStatus.CREATED).body(response);
    }
}

// --- DTOs (Your Contract's Data Shapes) ---

class CreateUserRequest {
    @NotNull @Schema(example = "john.doe@example.com", description = "User's email address")
    private String email;

    @NotNull @Schema(example = "strongP@ssw0rd", description = "User's password")
    private String password;

    // Getters and Setters
}

class UserResponse {
    private Long id;
    private String email;

    // Constructors, Getters, Setters
}
Explanation of the annotations:

@Tag: Groups and describes all operations in this controller.

@Operation: Describes a specific endpoint (its summary and description).

@ApiResponses: A container for multiple @ApiResponse annotations.

@ApiResponse: Documents a specific HTTP response, including its status code, description, and the schema of its body .

@Schema: Provides metadata for a field in a DTO, such as an example value or description.

Step 3: View and Share Your Contract
Once your application is running, SpringDoc provides two key URLs:

OpenAPI JSON: http://localhost:8080/v3/api-docs – This is the machine-readable contract file.

Swagger UI: http://localhost:8080/swagger-ui/index.html – An interactive web page that visualizes the contract and allows you to test the API .

You can share the URL to the OpenAPI JSON with consumer teams, or use it as input for other tools like code generators.

🔄 Extending Contracts: API Versioning
API contracts must evolve. The safest and most explicit way to do this is through URI Path Versioning. This means embedding the version number in the API path .

http://api.example.com/api/v1/users

http://api.example.com/api/v2/users

How to implement in Spring Boot:
You can create separate controllers or use @RequestMapping at the class level.

java
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/v1/users")
public class UserV1Controller { ... }

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/v2/users")
public class UserV2Controller { ... }
This approach makes the version part of the contract, making it very clear and easy to manage for both providers and consumers .

🧪 Advanced Pattern: Contract Testing
Annotations and documentation are crucial, but for true reliability, you need Contract Testing. This ensures your provider service actually fulfills the contract it claims to offer.

Spring Cloud Contract is a powerful tool for this. You define the expected request/response pairs in a Groovy DSL or YAML file, and it automatically generates tests for your provider to verify its behavior against the contract .

groovy
// Example of a Spring Cloud Contract definition (Contract.groovy)
import org.springframework.cloud.contract.spec.Contract

Contract.make {
    description("Should return a user when a valid ID is provided")
    request {
        method 'GET'
        url '/api/v1/users/1'
        headers {
            header('Content-Type': 'application/json')
        }
    }
    response {
        status 200
        body([
            id: 1,
            email: "john.doe@example.com"
        ])
        headers {
            header('Content-Type': 'application/json')
        }
    }
}
Once defined, this contract can also be used by consumer teams to generate stubs, allowing them to develop and test against a mock service that perfectly emulates the real API .

📋 Summary & Best Practices
Choose Modern Tools: For Java 8 + Spring Boot, SpringDoc OpenAPI is the clear, recommended choice over the older, inactive SpringFox .

Annotate for Clarity: Use @Operation, @ApiResponses, and @Schema to create a rich, self-documenting API that serves as the definitive contract .

Version Explicitly: Use URI path versioning (/v1/, /v2/) for a simple and unambiguous versioning strategy .

Validate with Contracts: Adopt Spring Cloud Contract to create executable API contracts. This allows you to automatically test your provider and generate stubs for consumers, ensuring true contract compliance .

How to implement microservice centralised logging using kibana, spring boot, java, zipkin?
Centralized logging and distributed tracing are essential for debugging and monitoring microservices. When you have 10+ services, logs are scattered across multiple containers/VMs. Centralized logging aggregates everything into a single view, while distributed tracing tracks a request across service boundaries.

Step 1: Docker Compose Setup for ELK + Zipkin
Create docker-compose.yml to spin up all required services:

yaml
version: '3.8'

services:
  # Elasticsearch - Storage
  elasticsearch:
    image: docker.elastic.co/elasticsearch/elasticsearch:7.17.0
    container_name: elasticsearch
    environment:
      - discovery.type=single-node
      - ES_JAVA_OPTS=-Xms512m -Xmx512m
      - xpack.security.enabled=false
    ports:
      - "9200:9200"
      - "9300:9300"
    networks:
      - logging-network
    volumes:
      - elasticsearch-data:/usr/share/elasticsearch/data

  # Logstash - Log Aggregator
  logstash:
    image: docker.elastic.co/logstash/logstash:7.17.0
    container_name: logstash
    ports:
      - "5000:5000/tcp"
      - "5000:5000/udp"
      - "9600:9600"
    volumes:
      - ./logstash/pipeline:/usr/share/logstash/pipeline
    environment:
      - LS_JAVA_OPTS=-Xmx256m -Xms256m
    networks:
      - logging-network
    depends_on:
      - elasticsearch

  # Kibana - Visualization
  kibana:
    image: docker.elastic.co/kibana/kibana:7.17.0
    container_name: kibana
    ports:
      - "5601:5601"
    environment:
      - ELASTICSEARCH_HOSTS=http://elasticsearch:9200
    networks:
      - logging-network
    depends_on:
      - elasticsearch

  # Zipkin - Distributed Tracing
  zipkin:
    image: openzipkin/zipkin:latest
    container_name: zipkin
    ports:
      - "9411:9411"
    environment:
      - STORAGE_TYPE=elasticsearch
      - ES_HOSTS=http://elasticsearch:9200
    networks:
      - logging-network
    depends_on:
      - elasticsearch

networks:
  logging-network:
    driver: bridge

volumes:
  elasticsearch-data:
Step 2: Logstash Configuration
Create logstash/pipeline/logstash.conf:

ruby
input {
  # TCP input for JSON logs
  tcp {
    port => 5000
    codec => json
    type => "microservice-logs"
  }
  
  # UDP input (lighter weight)
  udp {
    port => 5000
    codec => json
    type => "microservice-logs"
  }
}

filter {
  # Parse timestamp if present
  if [timestamp] {
    date {
      match => ["timestamp", "ISO8601"]
      target => "@timestamp"
    }
  }
  
  # Add correlation ID for tracing
  if [traceId] {
    mutate {
      add_field => { "traceId_analyzed" => "%{traceId}" }
    }
  }
  
  # Extract service name for easier filtering
  if [serviceName] {
    mutate {
      add_tag => ["service_%{serviceName}"]
    }
  }
  
  # Parse stack traces into separate field
  if [stackTrace] {
    mutate {
      gsub => ["stackTrace", "\n", "\\n"]
    }
  }
}

output {
  # Send to Elasticsearch
  elasticsearch {
    hosts => ["elasticsearch:9200"]
    index => "microservice-logs-%{+YYYY.MM.dd}"
    user => ""
    password => ""
  }
  
  # Debug output (remove in production)
  stdout {
    codec => rubydebug
  }
}

    file: "%d{yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss.SSS} ${LOG_LEVEL_PATTERN:-%5p} [%t] %-40.40logger{39} : %m%n${LOG_EXCEPTION_CONVERSION_WORD:-%wEx}"

    com.example: DEBUG

      max-history: 30

  port: 5000
Logback Configuration (logback-spring.xml)
Create src/main/resources/logback-spring.xml:

xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<configuration>
    <include resource="org/springframework/boot/logging/logback/base.xml"/>
    
    <!-- Property for service name -->
    <springProperty scope="context" name="serviceName" source="spring.application.name"/>
    
    <!-- JSON Appender for Logstash -->
    <appender name="LOGSTASH" class="net.logstash.logback.appender.LogstashTcpSocketAppender">
        <destination>${logstash.host:-localhost}:${logstash.port:-5000}</destination>
        
        <!-- Wait for connection before logging -->
        <reconnectionDelay>5000</reconnectionDelay>
        <keepAliveDuration>30000</keepAliveDuration>
        
        <!-- JSON Encoder -->
        <encoder class="net.logstash.logback.encoder.LogstashEncoder">
            <!-- Include MDC fields -->
            <includeMdcKeyName>traceId</includeMdcKeyName>
            <includeMdcKeyName>spanId</includeMdcKeyName>
            <includeMdcKeyName>parentId</includeMdcKeyName>
            <includeMdcKeyName>userId</includeMdcKeyName>
            <includeMdcKeyName>correlationId</includeMdcKeyName>
            
            <!-- Custom fields -->
            <customFields>{"serviceName":"${serviceName}","environment":"${ENVIRONMENT:-development}"}</customFields>
            
            <!-- Timezone -->
            <timeZone>UTC</timeZone>
        </encoder>
    </appender>
    
    <!-- Console appender for development -->
    <appender name="CONSOLE" class="ch.qos.logback.core.ConsoleAppender">
        <encoder>
            <pattern>%d{ISO8601} [%thread] %-5level %logger{36} - %msg%n</pattern>
        </encoder>
    </appender>
    
    <!-- Async appender for performance -->
    <appender name="ASYNC_LOGSTASH" class="ch.qos.logback.classic.AsyncAppender">
        <appender-ref ref="LOGSTASH"/>
        <queueSize>5000</queueSize>
        <discardingThreshold>0</discardingThreshold>
        <includeCallerData>false</includeCallerData>
    </appender>
    
    <!-- Root logger -->
    <root level="INFO">
        <appender-ref ref="CONSOLE"/>
        <appender-ref ref="ASYNC_LOGSTASH"/>
    </root>
    
    <!-- Service-specific loggers -->
    <logger name="com.example" level="DEBUG" additivity="false">
        <appender-ref ref="CONSOLE"/>
        <appender-ref ref="ASYNC_LOGSTASH"/>
    </logger>
</configuration>
Step 4: Implement Logging in Services
Base Controller with Automatic Logging
java
package com.example.order.controller;

import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.slf4j.MDC;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;

import javax.annotation.PostConstruct;
import java.util.UUID;

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/orders")
@Slf4j
public class OrderController {
    
    @PostConstruct
    public void init() {
        log.info("Order Service started successfully");
    }
    
    @PostMapping
    public OrderResponse createOrder(@RequestBody CreateOrderRequest request) {
        // Add correlation ID for tracking
        String correlationId = UUID.randomUUID().toString();
        MDC.put("correlationId", correlationId);
        
        try {
            log.info("Creating order for customer: {}, product: {}, quantity: {}", 
                     request.getCustomerId(), request.getProductId(), request.getQuantity());
            
            // Business logic
            Order order = processOrder(request);
            
            log.info("Order created successfully: {}", order.getId());
            
            // Log with structured data
            log.debug("Order details - ID: {}, Total: {}, Status: {}", 
                      order.getId(), order.getTotalAmount(), order.getStatus());
            
            return new OrderResponse(order.getId(), order.getStatus());
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            log.error("Failed to create order for customer: {}", request.getCustomerId(), e);
            throw new OrderCreationException("Order creation failed", e);
        } finally {
            MDC.remove("correlationId");
        }
    }
    
    @GetMapping("/{orderId}")
    public OrderResponse getOrder(@PathVariable Long orderId) {
        log.info("Fetching order: {}", orderId);
        
        if (orderId == null) {
            log.warn("Attempted to fetch order with null ID");
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Order ID cannot be null");
        }
        
        // Simulate service call
        Order order = orderService.findById(orderId);
        
        log.debug("Retrieved order: {} with status: {}", orderId, order.getStatus());
        
        return new OrderResponse(order.getId(), order.getStatus());
    }
}
Service Layer with Distributed Tracing
java
package com.example.order.service;

import brave.Span;
import brave.Tracer;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;
import org.springframework.web.client.RestTemplate;

@Service
@Slf4j
public class OrderProcessingService {
    
    @Autowired
    private RestTemplate restTemplate;
    
    @Autowired
    private Tracer tracer;  // For custom spans
    
    public Order processOrder(CreateOrderRequest request) {
        log.info("Processing order for product: {}", request.getProductId());
        
        // Create custom span for inventory check
        Span inventorySpan = tracer.nextSpan().name("inventory-check").start();
        
        try (Tracer.SpanInScope ws = tracer.withSpanInScope(inventorySpan)) {
            log.debug("Checking inventory for product: {}", request.getProductId());
            
            // Call inventory service (trace propagates automatically)
            InventoryResponse inventory = restTemplate.getForObject(
                "http://inventory-service/api/inventory/" + request.getProductId(),
                InventoryResponse.class
            );
            
            if (inventory.getQuantity() < request.getQuantity()) {
                log.warn("Insufficient inventory for product: {}. Available: {}, Requested: {}", 
                         request.getProductId(), inventory.getQuantity(), request.getQuantity());
                throw new InsufficientInventoryException("Not enough stock");
            }
            
            log.info("Inventory check passed for product: {}", request.getProductId());
            
        } finally {
            inventorySpan.finish();
        }
        
        // Process payment with its own span
        Span paymentSpan = tracer.nextSpan().name("payment-processing").start();
        
        try (Tracer.SpanInScope ws = tracer.withSpanInScope(paymentSpan)) {
            log.info("Processing payment for order");
            
            PaymentResponse payment = restTemplate.postForObject(
                "http://payment-service/api/payments/process",
                new PaymentRequest(request.getCustomerId(), request.getTotalAmount()),
                PaymentResponse.class
            );
            
            log.info("Payment processed: {}", payment.getTransactionId());
            
        } finally {
            paymentSpan.finish();
        }
        
        log.info("Order processing completed successfully");
        return new Order(UUID.randomUUID(), OrderStatus.CONFIRMED);
    }
}
Step 5: MDC Filter for Consistent Correlation
java
package com.example.order.filter;

import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.slf4j.MDC;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;
import org.springframework.web.filter.OncePerRequestFilter;

import javax.servlet.FilterChain;
import javax.servlet.ServletException;
import javax.servlet.http.HttpServletRequest;
import javax.servlet.http.HttpServletResponse;
import java.io.IOException;
import java.util.UUID;

@Component
@Slf4j
public class CorrelationIdFilter extends OncePerRequestFilter {
    
    private static final String CORRELATION_ID_HEADER = "X-Correlation-Id";
    private static final String CORRELATION_ID_MDC = "correlationId";
    private static final String USER_ID_HEADER = "X-User-Id";
    private static final String USER_ID_MDC = "userId";
    
    @Override
    protected void doFilterInternal(HttpServletRequest request, 
                                   HttpServletResponse response, 
                                   FilterChain filterChain) throws ServletException, IOException {
        
        try {
            // Get or generate correlation ID
            String correlationId = request.getHeader(CORRELATION_ID_HEADER);
            if (correlationId == null || correlationId.isEmpty()) {
                correlationId = UUID.randomUUID().toString();
            }
            
            // Add to MDC
            MDC.put(CORRELATION_ID_MDC, correlationId);
            
            // Add user ID if present
            String userId = request.getHeader(USER_ID_HEADER);
            if (userId != null) {
                MDC.put(USER_ID_MDC, userId);
            }
            
            // Add request information
            MDC.put("requestMethod", request.getMethod());
            MDC.put("requestUri", request.getRequestURI());
            MDC.put("remoteAddr", request.getRemoteAddr());
            
            // Add correlation ID to response headers
            response.setHeader(CORRELATION_ID_HEADER, correlationId);
            
            log.debug("Processing request: {} {}", request.getMethod(), request.getRequestURI());
            
            filterChain.doFilter(request, response);
            
        } finally {
            // Clean up MDC
            MDC.clear();
        }
    }
}
Step 6: RestTemplate Configuration with Tracing
java
package com.example.order.config;

import brave.Tracing;
import brave.http.HttpTracing;
import brave.spring.web.TracingClientHttpRequestInterceptor;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;
import org.springframework.http.client.ClientHttpRequestInterceptor;
import org.springframework.web.client.RestTemplate;

import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;

@Configuration
public class TracingConfig {
    
    @Bean
    public RestTemplate restTemplate(Tracing tracing) {
        RestTemplate restTemplate = new RestTemplate();
        
        // Add tracing interceptor
        List<ClientHttpRequestInterceptor> interceptors = new ArrayList<>();
        interceptors.add(TracingClientHttpRequestInterceptor.create(tracing));
        restTemplate.setInterceptors(interceptors);
        
        return restTemplate;
    }
}
Step 7: Kafka Producer/Consumer with Trace Propagation
java
package com.example.order.messaging;

import brave.Span;
import brave.Tracer;
import brave.propagation.CurrentTraceContext;
import brave.propagation.TraceContext;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.apache.kafka.clients.consumer.ConsumerRecord;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.kafka.annotation.KafkaListener;
import org.springframework.kafka.core.KafkaTemplate;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;

import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;

@Component
@Slf4j
public class KafkaTracePropagator {
    
    @Autowired
    private KafkaTemplate<String, String> kafkaTemplate;
    
    @Autowired
    private Tracer tracer;
    
    @Autowired
    private CurrentTraceContext currentTraceContext;
    
    /**
     * Producer with trace headers
     */
    public void sendWithTrace(String topic, String key, String message) {
        Span currentSpan = tracer.currentSpan();
        
        if (currentSpan != null) {
            // Inject trace context into Kafka headers
            TraceContext context = currentSpan.context();
            
            kafkaTemplate.send(topic, key, message)
                .headers(headers -> {
                    headers.add("X-B3-TraceId", context.traceIdString().getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));
                    headers.add("X-B3-SpanId", context.spanIdString().getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));
                    if (context.parentIdString() != null) {
                        headers.add("X-B3-ParentSpanId", context.parentIdString().getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));
                    }
                    headers.add("X-B3-Sampled", "1".getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));
                });
            
            log.debug("Sent Kafka message with trace: traceId={}, spanId={}", 
                      context.traceIdString(), context.spanIdString());
        } else {
            kafkaTemplate.send(topic, key, message);
            log.debug("Sent Kafka message without trace context");
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Consumer extracting trace context
     */
    @KafkaListener(topics = "order-events")
    public void consumeWithTrace(ConsumerRecord<String, String> record) {
        // Extract trace context from headers
        TraceContextOrSamplingFlags extracted = extractTraceContext(record);
        
        // Run with extracted context
        currentTraceContext.scoped(extracted).wrap(() -> {
            Span span = tracer.nextSpan().name("kafka-consume").start();
            
            try (Tracer.SpanInScope ws = tracer.withSpanInScope(span)) {
                log.info("Processing Kafka message: topic={}, partition={}, offset={}", 
                         record.topic(), record.partition(), record.offset());
                
                // Process message
                processMessage(record.value());
                
                span.tag("topic", record.topic());
                span.tag("partition", String.valueOf(record.partition()));
                span.tag("offset", String.valueOf(record.offset()));
                
            } finally {
                span.finish();
            }
        });
    }
    
    private TraceContextOrSamplingFlags extractTraceContext(ConsumerRecord<String, String> record) {
        brave.propagation.TraceContext.Extractor<ConsumerRecord<String, String>> extractor = 
            tracer.propagation().extractor(ConsumerRecord::headers);
        
        return extractor.extract(record);
    }
}
Step 8: Kibana Queries and Dashboards
Useful Kibana Queries
bash
# Find all logs for a specific trace
traceId: "1234567890abcdef"

# Find all errors in order service
serviceName: "order-service" AND level: "ERROR"

# Find slow requests (> 2 seconds)
duration: >2000

# Find logs with specific user
userId: "user123"

# Find logs with correlation ID
correlationId: "abc-123-def"

# Find all payment failures in last hour
serviceName: "payment-service" AND level: "ERROR" AND message: "*failed*" AND @timestamp: now-1h

# Search by HTTP method and status
requestMethod: "POST" AND responseStatus: "500"

Step 9: Health Check for Logging Infrastructure
java
package com.example.order.health;

import org.springframework.boot.actuate.health.Health;
import org.springframework.boot.actuate.health.HealthIndicator;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;
import org.springframework.web.client.RestTemplate;

@Component
public class LoggingInfrastructureHealthIndicator implements HealthIndicator {
    
    private final RestTemplate restTemplate = new RestTemplate();
    
    @Override
    public Health health() {
        try {
            // Check Elasticsearch
            String esUrl = "http://localhost:9200/_cluster/health";
            var esResponse = restTemplate.getForObject(esUrl, String.class);
            
            // Check Kibana
            String kibanaUrl = "http://localhost:5601/api/status";
            var kibanaResponse = restTemplate.getForObject(kibanaUrl, String.class);
            
            // Check Zipkin
            String zipkinUrl = "http://localhost:9411/health";
            var zipkinResponse = restTemplate.getForObject(zipkinUrl, String.class);
            
            return Health.up()
                .withDetail("elasticsearch", "available")
                .withDetail("kibana", "available")
                .withDetail("zipkin", "available")
                .build();
                
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return Health.down()
                .withDetail("error", e.getMessage())
                .build();
        }
    }
}

# 4. Start your Spring Boot services

# 5. Access Kibana UI
open http://localhost:5601

# 6. Configure Kibana index pattern:

# 7. Access Zipkin UI

  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
