# CloudFront CDN
resource "aws_cloudfront_distribution" "microservices" {
  enabled = true
  
  origin {
    domain_name = aws_api_gateway_rest_api.microservices.domain_name
    origin_id   = "api-gateway"
    
    custom_origin_config {
      http_port              = 80
      https_port             = 443
      origin_protocol_policy = "https-only"
      origin_ssl_protocols   = ["TLSv1.2"]
    }
  }
  
  default_cache_behavior {
    allowed_methods  = ["DELETE", "GET", "HEAD", "OPTIONS", "PATCH", "POST", "PUT"]
    cached_methods   = ["GET", "HEAD"]
    target_origin_id = "api-gateway"
    
    forwarded_values {
      query_string = true
      headers      = ["Authorization", "X-Correlation-Id"]
      
      cookies {
        forward = "none"
      }
    }
    
    viewer_protocol_policy = "redirect-to-https"
    min_ttl                = 0
    default_ttl            = 60
    max_ttl                = 300
    
    compress = true
  }
  
  price_class = "PriceClass_100"
  
  restrictions {
    geo_restriction {
      restriction_type = "none"
    }
  }
  
  viewer_certificate {
    cloudfront_default_certificate = true
  }
  
  tags = {
    Environment = var.environment
  }
}
Step 2: CI/CD Pipeline (AWS CodePipeline)
yaml
# buildspec.yml - AWS CodeBuild
version: 0.2

phases:
  pre_build:
    commands:
      - echo Logging in to Amazon ECR...
      - aws ecr get-login-password --region $AWS_DEFAULT_REGION | docker login --username AWS --password-stdin $AWS_ACCOUNT_ID.dkr.ecr.$AWS_DEFAULT_REGION.amazonaws.com
      - COMMIT_HASH=$(echo $CODEBUILD_RESOLVED_SOURCE_VERSION | cut -c 1-7)
      - IMAGE_TAG=${COMMIT_HASH:=latest}
      
  build:
    commands:
      - echo Build started on `date`
      - echo Building the Docker image...
      - docker build -t $REPOSITORY_URI:latest .
      - docker tag $REPOSITORY_URI:latest $REPOSITORY_URI:$IMAGE_TAG
      
  post_build:
    commands:
      - echo Build completed on `date`
      - echo Pushing the Docker images...
      - docker push $REPOSITORY_URI:latest
      - docker push $REPOSITORY_URI:$IMAGE_TAG
      - echo Writing image definitions file...
      - printf '[{"name":"%s","imageUri":"%s"}]' $SERVICE_NAME $REPOSITORY_URI:$IMAGE_TAG > imagedefinitions.json
      
artifacts:
  files:
    - imagedefinitions.json
    - scripts/*.sh
    - appspec.yaml
CodePipeline with Blue/Green Deployment
yaml
# pipeline.yaml - CloudFormation template for CI/CD
AWSTemplateFormatVersion: '2010-09-09'
Description: 'Microservices CI/CD Pipeline'

Parameters:
  ServiceName:
    Type: String
    Description: Name of the microservice
  Environment:
    Type: String
    AllowedValues: [dev, staging, production]

Resources:
  CodePipeline:
    Type: AWS::CodePipeline::Pipeline
    Properties:
      Name: !Sub "${ServiceName}-${Environment}-pipeline"
      RoleArn: !GetAtt PipelineRole.Arn
      
      Stages:
        # Source Stage
        - Name: Source
          Actions:
            - Name: Source
              ActionTypeId:
                Category: Source
                Owner: AWS
                Provider: CodeCommit
                Version: '1'
              Configuration:
                RepositoryName: !Sub "${ServiceName}-repo"
                BranchName: main
              OutputArtifacts:
                - Name: SourceOutput
              RunOrder: 1
        
        # Build Stage
        - Name: Build
          Actions:
            - Name: Build
              ActionTypeId:
                Category: Build
                Owner: AWS
                Provider: CodeBuild
                Version: '1'
              Configuration:
                ProjectName: !Ref BuildProject
              InputArtifacts:
                - Name: SourceOutput
              OutputArtifacts:
                - Name: BuildOutput
              RunOrder: 1
        
        # Deploy Stage (Blue/Green)
        - Name: Deploy
          Actions:
            - Name: DeployToEKS
              ActionTypeId:
                Category: Deploy
                Owner: AWS
                Provider: EKS
                Version: '1'
              Configuration:
                ClusterName: !Sub "microservices-${Environment}"
                ManifestFiles: BuildOutput/k8s/
                Namespace: !Ref ServiceName
              RunOrder: 1
        
        # Integration Tests Stage
        - Name: IntegrationTests
          Actions:
            - Name: RunTests
              ActionTypeId:
                Category: Test
                Owner: AWS
                Provider: CodeBuild
                Version: '1'
              Configuration:
                ProjectName: !Ref TestProject
              InputArtifacts:
                - Name: BuildOutput
              RunOrder: 1
        
        # Canary Deployment Stage (Production only)
        - Name: CanaryDeployment
          Actions:
            - Name: CanaryDeploy
              ActionTypeId:
                Category: Deploy
                Owner: AWS
                Provider: EKS
                Version: '1'
              Configuration:
                ClusterName: !Sub "microservices-${Environment}"
                ManifestFiles: BuildOutput/k8s-canary/
                Namespace: !Ref ServiceName
              RunOrder: 1
        
        # Manual Approval for Production
        - Name: Approval
          Actions:
            - Name: ManualApproval
              ActionTypeId:
                Category: Approval
                Owner: AWS
                Provider: Manual
                Version: '1'
              Configuration:
                NotificationArn: !Ref ApprovalSNSTopic
              RunOrder: 1
        
        # Full Production Deployment
        - Name: FullDeployment
          Actions:
            - Name: DeployToProduction
              ActionTypeId:
                Category: Deploy
                Owner: AWS
                Provider: EKS
                Version: '1'
              Configuration:
                ClusterName: microservices-production
                ManifestFiles: BuildOutput/k8s-prod/
                Namespace: !Ref ServiceName
              RunOrder: 1
Step 3: Kubernetes Configuration for Cloud
yaml
# k8s/order-service/deployment.yaml
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: order-service
  namespace: order-service
  annotations:
    # Cloud-specific annotations
    prometheus.io/scrape: "true"
    prometheus.io/port: "8080"
    prometheus.io/path: "/actuator/prometheus"
spec:
  replicas: 3
  strategy:
    type: RollingUpdate
    rollingUpdate:
      maxSurge: 25%
      maxUnavailable: 0
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app: order-service
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app: order-service
        version: v1
    spec:
      serviceAccountName: order-service-sa
      containers:
      - name: order-service
        image: ${AWS_ACCOUNT_ID}.dkr.ecr.${AWS_REGION}.amazonaws.com/microservices/order-service:${IMAGE_TAG}
        imagePullPolicy: Always
        ports:
        - containerPort: 8080
          name: http
        env:
        - name: SPRING_PROFILES_ACTIVE
          value: "cloud"
        - name: AWS_REGION
          value: ${AWS_REGION}
        - name: DB_HOST
          valueFrom:
            secretKeyRef:
              name: rds-credentials
              key: host
        - name: DB_PASSWORD
          valueFrom:
            secretKeyRef:
              name: rds-credentials
              key: password
        - name: REDIS_ENDPOINT
          valueFrom:
            configMapKeyRef:
              name: microservices-config
              key: redis.endpoint
        - name: KAFKA_BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS
          valueFrom:
            configMapKeyRef:
              name: microservices-config
              key: kafka.bootstrap
        resources:
          requests:
            memory: "512Mi"
            cpu: "250m"
          limits:
            memory: "1Gi"
            cpu: "500m"
        livenessProbe:
          httpGet:
            path: /actuator/health/liveness
            port: 8080
          initialDelaySeconds: 60
          periodSeconds: 10
        readinessProbe:
          httpGet:
            path: /actuator/health/readiness
            port: 8080
          initialDelaySeconds: 30
          periodSeconds: 5
        envFrom:
        - configMapRef:
            name: order-service-config
      # Use spot instances for non-critical workloads
      nodeSelector:
        workload-type: general
      affinity:
        podAntiAffinity:
          preferredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution:
          - weight: 100
            podAffinityTerm:
              labelSelector:
                matchExpressions:
                - key: app
                  operator: In
                  values:
                  - order-service
              topologyKey: topology.kubernetes.io/zone
Step 4: Cloud-Native Service Mesh (AWS App Mesh)
yaml
# appmesh/virtual-node.yaml
apiVersion: appmesh.k8s.aws/v1beta2
kind: VirtualNode
metadata:
  name: order-service
  namespace: order-service
spec:
  podSelector:
    matchLabels:
      app: order-service
  listeners:
    - portMapping:
        port: 8080
        protocol: http
      healthCheck:
        healthyThreshold: 2
        intervalMillis: 5000
        path: /actuator/health
        port: 8080
        protocol: http
        timeoutMillis: 2000
        unhealthyThreshold: 2
  serviceDiscovery:
    dns:
      hostname: order-service.order-service.svc.cluster.local
  backends:
    - virtualService:
        virtualServiceRef:
          name: payment-service
          namespace: payment-service
    - virtualService:
        virtualServiceRef:
          name: inventory-service
          namespace: inventory-service
  logging:
    accessLog:
      file:
        path: /dev/stdout
---
apiVersion: appmesh.k8s.aws/v1beta2
kind: VirtualRouter
metadata:
  name: order-service
  namespace: order-service
spec:
  listeners:
    - portMapping:
        port: 8080
        protocol: http
  routes:
    - name: stable-route
      httpRoute:
        match:
          prefix: /
        action:
          weightedTargets:
            - virtualNodeRef:
                name: order-service
              weight: 100
    - name: canary-route
      httpRoute:
        match:
          headers:
            - name: canary
              match:
                exact: "true"
        action:
          weightedTargets:
            - virtualNodeRef:
                name: order-service-canary
              weight: 100
Step 5: Cloud-Native Observability
AWS X-Ray Integration
java
// X-Ray Configuration for Spring Boot
@Configuration
public class XRayConfig {
    
    @Bean
    public TracingFilter tracingFilter() {
        return new AWSXRayServletFilter("order-service");
    }
    
    @Bean
    public SegmentNamingStrategy segmentNamingStrategy() {
        return new DynamicSegmentNamingStrategy("microservices", "order-service");
    }
}

// Service with X-Ray tracing
@Service
@Slf4j
public class OrderService {
    
    @Autowired
    private RestTemplate restTemplate;
    
    public Order createOrder(OrderRequest request) {
        // Create subsegment for database call
        Subsegment dbSubsegment = AWSXRay.beginSubsegment("database-operation");
        try {
            Order order = orderRepository.save(request);
            dbSubsegment.addMetadata("orderId", order.getId());
            dbSubsegment.addMetadata("customerId", order.getCustomerId());
            return order;
        } catch (Exception e) {
            dbSubsegment.addException(e);
            throw e;
        } finally {
            AWSXRay.endSubsegment();
        }
    }
    
    @XRayEnabled
    public PaymentResponse processPayment(PaymentRequest request) {
        // This method is automatically traced
        return restTemplate.postForObject("http://payment-service/payments", request, PaymentResponse.class);
    }
}
CloudWatch Dashboards (Terraform)
hcl
# cloudwatch-dashboards.tf
resource "aws_cloudwatch_dashboard" "microservices" {
  dashboard_name = "microservices-dashboard-${var.environment}"
  
  dashboard_body = jsonencode({
    widgets = [
      {
        type = "metric"
        properties = {
          metrics = [
            ["AWS/EKS", "cluster_failed_pod_count", { stat = "Sum" }],
            ["AWS/EKS", "node_count", { stat = "Average" }],
            ["AWS/ApplicationELB", "TargetResponseTime", { stat = "Average" }]
          ]
          period = 300
          stat   = "Average"
          region = var.aws_region
          title  = "EKS Cluster Health"
        }
      },
      {
        type = "log"
        properties = {
          query = "SOURCE '/aws/containerinsights/${module.eks.cluster_name}/application' | fields @timestamp, @message | sort @timestamp desc | limit 100"
          region = var.aws_region
          title = "Application Logs"
          view = "table"
        }
      },
      {
        type = "metric"
        properties = {
          metrics = [
            ["AWS/RDS", "DatabaseConnections", { "DBInstanceIdentifier" = module.rds.db_instance_id }],
            ["AWS/RDS", "CPUUtilization", { "DBInstanceIdentifier" = module.rds.db_instance_id }],
            ["AWS/RDS", "FreeStorageSpace", { "DBInstanceIdentifier" = module.rds.db_instance_id }]
          ]
          period = 300
          title = "RDS Database Metrics"
        }
      }
    ]
  })
}

      operator: In
      values: ["on-demand", "spot"]
    - key: node.kubernetes.io/instance-type
      operator: In
      values: ["m5.large", "m5.xlarge", "m5.2xlarge", "c5.large", "c5.xlarge"]
    - key: topology.kubernetes.io/zone
      operator: In
      values: ["us-east-1a", "us-east-1b", "us-east-1c"]
  
  # TTL for node cleanup
  ttlSecondsUntilExpired: 604800  # 7 days
  ttlSecondsAfterEmpty: 30
  
  # Resource limits
  limits:
    cpu: 1000
    memory: 2000Gi
  
  # Scheduling constraints
  kubeletConfiguration:
    clusterDNS:
    - 10.100.0.10

kind: PodSecurityPolicy

  name: restricted-psp
spec:
  privileged: false
  allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
  requiredDropCapabilities:
    - ALL
  volumes:
    - 'configMap'
    - 'emptyDir'
    - 'secret'
  hostNetwork: false
  hostIPC: false
  hostPID: false
  runAsUser:
    rule: 'MustRunAsNonRoot'

  supplementalGroups:

        max: 65535

Cloud Provider Comparison
Feature	AWS	Azure	GCP
Managed K8s	EKS	AKS	GKE (Best)
Serverless Containers	Fargate	ACI	Cloud Run
Managed DB	RDS (Best)	PostgreSQL Flexible	Cloud SQL
Managed Kafka	MSK	Event Hubs	Pub/Sub
API Gateway	API Gateway (Good)	API Management	API Gateway (Best)
Service Mesh	App Mesh	Service Mesh	Traffic Director
Observability	CloudWatch (Good)	Monitor (Good)	Cloud Operations (Best)
CI/CD	CodePipeline	DevOps	Cloud Build
Cost	Pay-as-you-go	Enterprise friendly	Sustained use discounts
Deployment Checklist for Cloud
Account Structure: Multi-account strategy with AWS Organizations

Networking: VPC design with public/private subnets, NAT gateways, VPC peering

Compute: EKS cluster with managed node groups and Fargate

Storage: S3 for artifacts, EBS for persistent volumes, EFS for shared storage

Database: RDS with Multi-AZ, backups, encryption

Caching: ElastiCache Redis cluster

Messaging: MSK Kafka with TLS encryption

CI/CD: CodePipeline with Blue/Green deployments

Service Mesh: App Mesh for traffic management

Observability: CloudWatch, X-Ray, Prometheus, Grafana

Security: IAM roles, security groups, WAF, Shield

Cost: Budget alerts, cost explorer, Savings Plans

Disaster Recovery: Multi-region failover, RDS read replicas

Compliance: Config rules, GuardDuty, Security Hub

Key Cloud-Native Principles
Everything as Code: Infrastructure, policies, configuration

Managed Services First: RDS over self-managed PostgreSQL

Ephemeral Infrastructure: Immutable containers, auto-scaling groups

Pay for Value: Serverless where possible (Fargate, Lambda)

Security by Default: Encryption everywhere, least privilege

Observability Built-in: Metrics, logs, traces from day one

Chaos Engineering: Test failures with AWS Fault Injection Simulator

The cloud approach dramatically reduces operational overhead but requires understanding of cloud-specific patterns and cost management. Start with a single cloud provider, master it, then consider multi-cloud if business requires it!

# Subnets (multiple regions for HA)

  for_each = var.subnets
  
  name          = each.value.name
  ip_cidr_range = each.value.cidr
  region        = each.value.region
  network       = google_compute_network.vpc.id

  # Secondary IP ranges for pods and services (GKE)
  secondary_ip_range {
    range_name    = "pods-range"
    ip_cidr_range = each.value.pods_cidr
  }

    range_name    = "services-range"
    ip_cidr_range = each.value.services_cidr
  }
}

# GKE Cluster (Standard or Autopilot)
resource "google_container_cluster" "primary" {
  name     = "microservices-cluster"
  location = var.region
  
  # Remove default node pool
  remove_default_node_pool = true
  initial_node_count       = 1
  
  # Network configuration
  network    = google_compute_network.vpc.id
  subnetwork = google_compute_subnetwork.subnets["${var.region}"].id
  
  # Private cluster for security
  private_cluster_config {
    enable_private_nodes    = true
    enable_private_endpoint = false
    master_ipv4_cidr_block  = "172.16.0.0/28"
  }
  
  # IP allocation for pods and services
  ip_allocation_policy {
    cluster_secondary_range_name  = "pods-range"
    services_secondary_range_name = "services-range"
  }
  
  # Workload Identity (GCP IAM for K8s)
  workload_identity_config {
    workload_pool = "${var.project_id}.svc.id.goog"
  }
  
  # Release channel for automatic upgrades
  release_channel {
    channel = var.environment == "production" ? "STABLE" : "REGULAR"
  }
  
  # Monitoring and logging
  monitoring_config {
    enable_components = ["SYSTEM_COMPONENTS", "WORKLOADS"]
    managed_prometheus {
      enabled = true
    }
  }
  
  logging_config {
    enable_components = ["SYSTEM_COMPONENTS", "WORKLOADS"]
  }
  
  # Maintenance policy
  maintenance_policy {
    daily_maintenance_window {
      start_time = "03:00"
    }
  }
  
  # Addons
  addons_config {
    http_load_balancing {
      disabled = false
    }
    horizontal_pod_autoscaling {
      disabled = false
    }
    network_policy_config {
      disabled = false
    }
    gce_persistent_disk_csi_driver_config {
      enabled = true
    }
  }
  
  # Database encryption
  database_encryption {
    state = "ENCRYPTED"
  }
  
  # Binary Authorization
  binary_authorization {
    evaluation_mode = var.environment == "production" ? "PROJECT_SINGLETON_POLICY_ENFORCE" : "DISABLED"
  }
  
  depends_on = [google_project_service.services]
}

# Artifact Registry for Docker images
resource "google_artifact_registry_repository" "microservices" {
  provider = google-beta
  
  location      = var.region
  repository_id = "microservices"
  description   = "Docker repository for microservices"
  format        = "DOCKER"
  
  docker_config {
    immutable_tags = false
  }
  
  cleanup_policies {
    id     = "delete-old-images"
    action = "DELETE"
    condition {
      tag_state        = "TAGGED"
      tag_prefixes     = ["v"]
      older_than       = "604800s"  # 7 days
    }
  }
  
  cleanup_policies {
    id     = "delete-untagged"
    action = "DELETE"
    condition {
      tag_state = "UNTAGGED"
      older_than = "86400s"  # 1 day
    }
  }
}

  display_name      = "Microservices Redis Cache"
  reserved_ip_range = "10.0.0.0/29"
  
  auth_enabled = true
  
  connect_mode = "PRIVATE_SERVICE_ACCESS"
  
  maintenance_policy {
    day {
      day  = "SUNDAY"
      start_time {
        hours   = 3
        minutes = 0
      }
    }
  }
  
  persistence_config {
    persistence_mode = var.environment == "production" ? "RDB" : "DISABLED"
    rdb_snapshot_period = "TWELVE_HOURS"
  }
}

Step 4: CI/CD with Cloud Build
Cloud Build Configuration
yaml
# cloudbuild.yaml
steps:
  # Step 1: Build JAR with Gradle
  - name: 'gradle:8.0-jdk11'
    entrypoint: 'gradle'
    args: ['clean', 'build', '-x', 'test']
    id: 'gradle-build'
    
  # Step 2: Run tests
  - name: 'gradle:8.0-jdk11'
    entrypoint: 'gradle'
    args: ['test']
    id: 'unit-tests'
    
  # Step 3: Build Docker image
  - name: 'gcr.io/cloud-builders/docker'
    args: ['build', '-t', '${_REGION}-docker.pkg.dev/${PROJECT_ID}/microservices/${_SERVICE_NAME}:${SHORT_SHA}', '.']
    id: 'docker-build'
    
  # Step 4: Push to Artifact Registry
  - name: 'gcr.io/cloud-builders/docker'
    args: ['push', '${_REGION}-docker.pkg.dev/${PROJECT_ID}/microservices/${_SERVICE_NAME}:${SHORT_SHA}']
    id: 'docker-push'
    
  # Step 5: Security scan (Container Analysis)
  - name: 'gcr.io/cloud-builders/gcloud'
    args:
      - 'beta'
      - 'container'
      - 'vulnerability'
      - 'occurrences'
      - 'list'
      - '--image=${_REGION}-docker.pkg.dev/${PROJECT_ID}/microservices/${_SERVICE_NAME}:${SHORT_SHA}'
      - '--filter=severity:CRITICAL'
    id: 'security-scan'
    
  # Step 6: Deploy to GKE or Cloud Run
  - name: 'gcr.io/cloud-builders/gke-deploy'
    args:
      - 'run'
      - '--cluster=${_CLUSTER_NAME}'
      - '--location=${_REGION}'
      - '--namespace=microservices'
      - '--image=${_REGION}-docker.pkg.dev/${PROJECT_ID}/microservices/${_SERVICE_NAME}:${SHORT_SHA}'
      - '--output=/tmp/output'
    id: 'deploy-to-gke'
    
  # Step 7: Integration tests
  - name: 'gcr.io/cloud-builders/curl'
    args:
      - '-f'
      - 'https://${_SERVICE_NAME}-${SHORT_SHA}.${_REGION}.run.app/actuator/health'
    id: 'smoke-test'
    
  # Step 8: Traffic migration (Cloud Run only)
  - name: 'gcr.io/google.com/cloudsdktool/cloud-sdk'
    entrypoint: 'gcloud'
    args:
      - 'run'
      - 'services'
      - 'update-traffic'
      - '${_SERVICE_NAME}'
      - '--to-revisions=${SHORT_SHA}=100'
      - '--region=${_REGION}'
    id: 'traffic-migration'
    
  # Step 9: Clean up old revisions
  - name: 'gcr.io/google.com/cloudsdktool/cloud-sdk'
    entrypoint: 'gcloud'
    args:
      - 'run'
      - 'revisions'
      - 'delete'
      - '--region=${_REGION}'
      - '--quiet'
      - '${_SERVICE_NAME}-${SHORT_SHA}'
    id: 'cleanup'
    waitFor: ['smoke-test']

# Substitutions
substitutions:
  _SERVICE_NAME: order-service
  _CLUSTER_NAME: microservices-cluster
  _REGION: us-central1

# Images to store
images:
  - '${_REGION}-docker.pkg.dev/${PROJECT_ID}/microservices/${_SERVICE_NAME}:${SHORT_SHA}'

# Service account
serviceAccount: projects/${PROJECT_ID}/serviceAccounts/cloud-build-sa@${PROJECT_ID}.iam.gserviceaccount.com

resource "google_cloudbuild_trigger" "microservices" {
  for_each = toset(var.microservices)
  
  name        = "${each.value}-trigger"
  description = "Trigger for ${each.value}"
  location    = var.region
  
  github {
    owner = var.github_owner
    name  = var.github_repo
    
    pull_request {
      branch = "^main$"
      comment_control = "COMMENTS_ENABLED_FOR_EXTERNAL_CONTRIBUTORS_ONLY"
    }
  }
  
  build {
    step {
      name = "gcr.io/cloud-builders/git"
      args = ["clone", "https://github.com/${var.github_owner}/${var.github_repo}"]
    }
    
    step {
      name = "gcr.io/cloud-builders/docker"
      args = ["build", "-t", "${var.region}-docker.pkg.dev/${var.project_id}/microservices/${each.value}:$SHORT_SHA", "."]
    }
    
    step {
      name = "gcr.io/cloud-builders/docker"
      args = ["push", "${var.region}-docker.pkg.dev/${var.project_id}/microservices/${each.value}:$SHORT_SHA"]
    }
    
    images = ["${var.region}-docker.pkg.dev/${var.project_id}/microservices/${each.value}"]
    
    tags = ["microservices", each.value]
  }
  
  substitutions = {
    _SERVICE_NAME = each.value
  }
}

Java Configuration for GCP Services
java
package com.example.order.config;

import com.google.api.gax.core.CredentialsProvider;
import com.google.api.gax.core.NoCredentialsProvider;
import com.google.cloud.pubsub.v1.TopicAdminClient;
import com.google.cloud.pubsub.v1.TopicAdminSettings;
import com.google.cloud.spring.pubsub.core.PubSubTemplate;
import com.google.cloud.spring.pubsub.integration.inbound.PubSubInboundChannelAdapter;
import com.google.cloud.spring.pubsub.support.GcpPubSubHeaders;
import com.google.cloud.spring.pubsub.support.converter.JacksonPubSubMessageConverter;
import com.google.cloud.trace.v1.TraceServiceClient;
import com.google.cloud.trace.v1.TraceServiceSettings;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Value;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Profile;
import org.springframework.integration.annotation.ServiceActivator;
import org.springframework.integration.channel.DirectChannel;
import org.springframework.messaging.MessageChannel;

import java.io.IOException;

@Configuration
@Slf4j
@Profile("gcp")
public class GCPConfiguration {
    
    @Value("${spring.cloud.gcp.project-id}")
    private String projectId;
    
    @Value("${spring.cloud.gcp.credentials.location:}")
    private String credentialsPath;
    
    @Bean
    public PubSubTemplate pubSubTemplate() throws IOException {
        PubSubTemplate pubSubTemplate = new PubSubTemplate(
            () -> TopicAdminClient.create(TopicAdminSettings.newBuilder()
                .setCredentialsProvider(credentialsProvider())
                .build()),
            () -> null, // SubscriptionAdminClient
            credentialsProvider()
        );
        pubSubTemplate.setMessageConverter(new JacksonPubSubMessageConverter());
        return pubSubTemplate;
    }
    
    @Bean
    public CredentialsProvider credentialsProvider() {
        if (credentialsPath != null && !credentialsPath.isEmpty()) {
            return GoogleCredentials.fromStream(new FileInputStream(credentialsPath))
                .createScoped(List.of("https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-platform"));
        }
        return NoCredentialsProvider.create();
    }
    
    // Pub/Sub Outbound Channel
    @Bean
    public MessageChannel pubSubOutputChannel() {
        return new DirectChannel();
    }
    
    @Bean
    @ServiceActivator(inputChannel = "pubSubOutputChannel")
    public PubSubTemplate.PubSubMessageHandler messageSender(PubSubTemplate pubSubTemplate) {
        return pubSubTemplate.publish("order-events");
    }
    
    // Pub/Sub Inbound Channel (Subscription)
    @Bean
    public MessageChannel pubSubInputChannel() {
        return new DirectChannel();
    }
    
    @Bean
    public PubSubInboundChannelAdapter pubSubAdapter(PubSubTemplate pubSubTemplate) {
        PubSubInboundChannelAdapter adapter = new PubSubInboundChannelAdapter(
            pubSubTemplate, "order-events-sub"
        );
        adapter.setOutputChannel(pubSubInputChannel());
        adapter.setAckMode(AckMode.MANUAL);
        return adapter;
    }
    
    @Bean
    @ServiceActivator(inputChannel = "pubSubInputChannel")
    public void handlePubSubMessage(String payload, Map<String, Object> headers) {
        String ackId = (String) headers.get(GcpPubSubHeaders.ORIGINAL_MESSAGE);
        log.info("Received Pub/Sub message: {}", payload);
        
        // Process message
        processMessage(payload);
        
        // Acknowledge message
        // ack(ackId); - Manual ack if needed
    }
    
    // Cloud Trace configuration
    @Bean
    public TraceServiceClient traceServiceClient() throws IOException {
        return TraceServiceClient.create(TraceServiceSettings.newBuilder()
            .setCredentialsProvider(credentialsProvider())
            .build());
    }
}
Service with GCP Integrations
java
package com.example.order.service;

import com.google.cloud.spring.pubsub.core.PubSubTemplate;
import com.google.cloud.spring.trace.TraceId;
import com.google.cloud.trace.v1.TraceServiceClient;
import com.google.devtools.cloudtrace.v1.Trace;
import com.google.devtools.cloudtrace.v1.TraceSpan;
import com.google.protobuf.Timestamp;
import lombok.RequiredArgsConstructor;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;

import java.util.concurrent.TimeUnit;

@Service
@RequiredArgsConstructor
@Slf4j
public class OrderService {
    
    private final OrderRepository orderRepository;
    private final PubSubTemplate pubSubTemplate;
    private final TraceServiceClient traceServiceClient;
    
    @TraceId
    public Order createOrder(OrderRequest request) {
        // Custom trace span
        long startTime = System.nanoTime();
        
        try {
            log.info("Creating order for customer: {}", request.getCustomerId());
            
            // Save to Cloud SQL
            Order order = orderRepository.save(Order.from(request));
            
            // Publish event to Pub/Sub
            publishOrderEvent(order);
            
            // Store in Cloud Storage (if needed)
            storeOrderDocument(order);
            
            log.info("Order created: {}", order.getId());
            return order;
            
        } finally {
            long endTime = System.nanoTime();
            recordTrace("create-order", startTime, endTime);
        }
    }
    
    private void publishOrderEvent(Order order) {
        OrderEvent event = OrderEvent.builder()
            .orderId(order.getId())
            .customerId(order.getCustomerId())
            .totalAmount(order.getTotalAmount())
            .timestamp(Instant.now())
            .build();
        
        pubSubTemplate.publish("order-events", event)
            .addCallback(
                result -> log.info("Event published: {}", result),
                error -> log.error("Failed to publish event", error)
            );
    }
    
    private void storeOrderDocument(Order order) {
        // Store as JSON in Cloud Storage
        String json = objectMapper.writeValueAsString(order);
        storageClient.write("orders/" + order.getId() + ".json", json.getBytes());
    }
    
    private void recordTrace(String spanName, long startTime, long endTime) {
        TraceSpan span = TraceSpan.newBuilder()
            .setSpanId(UUID.randomUUID().toString())
            .setName(spanName)
            .setStartTime(Timestamp.newBuilder()
                .setSeconds(TimeUnit.NANOSECONDS.toSeconds(startTime))
                .setNanos((int) (startTime % TimeUnit.SECONDS.toNanos(1)))
                .build())
            .setEndTime(Timestamp.newBuilder()
                .setSeconds(TimeUnit.NANOSECONDS.toSeconds(endTime))
                .setNanos((int) (endTime % TimeUnit.SECONDS.toNanos(1)))
                .build())
            .build();
        
        Trace trace = Trace.newBuilder()
            .setProjectId(projectId)
            .setTraceId(UUID.randomUUID().toString())
            .addSpans(span)
            .build();
        
        traceServiceClient.patchTraces(projectId, Traces.newBuilder().addTraces(trace).build());
    }
}
Step 6: Service Mesh with Anthos (Istio)
Anthos Service Mesh Configuration
yaml
# istio/virtual-service.yaml
apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1beta1
kind: VirtualService
metadata:
  name: order-service
  namespace: microservices
  annotations:
    # Anthos specific
    alpha.istio.io/member-roll: '{"name": "default"}'

  hosts:
  - order-service
  gateways:
  - mesh
  - anthos-ingressgateway
  http:
  # Canary routing based on header
  - match:
    - headers:
        canary:
          exact: "true"
    route:
    - destination:
        host: order-service
        subset: v2
      weight: 100
  # A/B testing
  - match:
    - headers:
        user-type:
          exact: "premium"
    route:
    - destination:
        host: order-service
        subset: v2
      weight: 80
    - destination:
        host: order-service
        subset: v1
      weight: 20
  # Normal traffic
  - route:
    - destination:
        host: order-service
        subset: v1
      weight: 90
    - destination:
        host: order-service
        subset: v2
      weight: 10
    retries:
      attempts: 3
      perTryTimeout: 2s
      retryOn: "5xx,gateway-error,connect-failure,refused-stream"
    timeout: 5s
    fault:
      delay:
        percentage:
          value: 1
        fixedDelay: 1s
---
apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1beta1
kind: DestinationRule
metadata:
  name: order-service
  namespace: microservices
spec:
  host: order-service
  trafficPolicy:
    connectionPool:
      tcp:
        maxConnections: 100
      http:
        http1MaxPendingRequests: 10
        http2MaxRequests: 100
        maxRequestsPerConnection: 10
    loadBalancer:
      simple: ROUND_ROBIN
    outlierDetection:
      consecutive5xxErrors: 5
      interval: 30s
      baseEjectionTime: 30s
      maxEjectionPercent: 50
    tls:
      mode: ISTIO_MUTUAL
  subsets:
  - name: v1
    labels:
      version: v1
  - name: v2
    labels:
      version: v2
    trafficPolicy:
      connectionPool:
        http:
          http2MaxRequests: 50

# Alert Policies
resource "google_monitoring_alert_policy" "high_error_rate" {
  display_name = "High Error Rate"
  combiner     = "OR"
  
  conditions {
    display_name = "Error rate > 5%"
    
    condition_threshold {
      filter = "metric.type=\"run.googleapis.com/container/request_count\" resource.type=\"cloud_run_revision\" metric.label.\"response_code_class\"=\"5xx\""
      
      aggregations {
        alignment_period     = "300s"
        per_series_aligner   = "ALIGN_RATE"
        cross_series_reducer = "REDUCE_SUM"
        group_by_fields      = ["resource.labels.service_name"]
      }
      
      comparison      = "COMPARISON_GT"
      threshold_value = 0.05
      duration        = "0s"
      
      trigger {
        count = 1
      }
    }
  }
  
  alert_strategy {
    notification_rate_limit {
      period = "300s"
    }
    auto_close = "1800s"
  }
  
  notification_channels = [google_monitoring_notification_channel.email.name]
  
  user_labels = {
    severity = "critical"
    team     = "microservices"
  }
}

        per_series_aligner = "ALIGN_MEAN"

    }
  }

Step 8: Serverless Workflows with Cloud Composer/Airflow
python
# dags/microservices_dag.py
from datetime import datetime, timedelta
from airflow import DAG
from airflow.providers.google.cloud.operators.cloud_run import CloudRunExecuteJobOperator
from airflow.providers.google.cloud.operators.bigquery import BigQueryInsertJobOperator
from airflow.providers.google.cloud.operators.pubsub import PubSubPublishMessageOperator
from airflow.operators.bash import BashOperator

default_args = {
    'owner': 'microservices-team',
    'depends_on_past': False,
    'start_date': datetime(2024, 1, 1),
    'email_on_failure': True,
    'email_on_retry': False,
    'email': ['alerts@example.com'],
    'retries': 1,
    'retry_delay': timedelta(minutes=5),
}

dag = DAG(
    'microservices_daily_report',
    default_args=default_args,
    description='Generate daily microservices reports',
    schedule_interval='0 2 * * *',  # Daily at 2 AM
    catchup=False,
    tags=['microservices', 'reporting'],
)

# Task 1: Trigger Cloud Run job for order aggregation
trigger_aggregation = CloudRunExecuteJobOperator(
    task_id='trigger_order_aggregation',
    project_id='{{ var.value.gcp_project }}',
    region='us-central1',
    job_name='order-aggregation-job',
    overrides={
        'container_overrides': [{
            'name': 'aggregator',
            'args': ['--date', '{{ ds }}']
        }]
    },
    dag=dag,
)

# Task 2: Wait for completion (via Pub/Sub)
# This would be implemented with a Pub/Sub sensor

# Task 3: Load results to BigQuery
load_to_bigquery = BigQueryInsertJobOperator(
    task_id='load_to_bigquery',
    project_id='{{ var.value.gcp_project }}',
    configuration={
        'query': {
            'query': """
                INSERT INTO `microservices_reports.daily_orders`
                SELECT 
                    DATE(order_date) as order_date,
                    COUNT(*) as total_orders,
                    SUM(total_amount) as total_revenue,
                    COUNT(DISTINCT customer_id) as unique_customers
                FROM `microservices.order_events`
                WHERE DATE(order_date) = '{{ ds }}'
                GROUP BY order_date
            """,
            'useLegacySql': False,
        }
    },
    dag=dag,
)

# Task 4: Notify via Pub/Sub
notify_completion = PubSubPublishMessageOperator(
    task_id='notify_completion',
    project_id='{{ var.value.gcp_project }}',
    topic='reporting-events',
    messages=[{'data': 'Daily report completed for {{ ds }}'}],
    dag=dag,
)

# Set dependencies
trigger_aggregation >> load_to_bigquery >> notify_completion

GCP Services Comparison
Service Type	GCP Service	Best For
Compute	GKE (Standard)	Complex workloads, full control
GKE (Autopilot)	Most workloads, reduced ops
Cloud Run	APIs, event-driven, stateless
Cloud Functions	Simple, single-purpose functions
Database	Cloud SQL	Traditional RDBMS (PostgreSQL/MySQL)
Cloud Spanner	Global scale, strong consistency
Firestore	Real-time, mobile, serverless
Bigtable	Time-series, IoT, large scale
Messaging	Pub/Sub	Reliable, async messaging
Eventarc	Event-driven from GCP services
Observability	Cloud Monitoring	Metrics, dashboards, alerting
Cloud Trace	Distributed tracing
Cloud Logging	Log aggregation
Cloud Profiler	Continuous profiling
CI/CD	Cloud Build	Container builds, pipelines
Cloud Deploy	Delivery pipelines
Cloud Run for Anthos	Hybrid deployments
Deployment Checklist for GCP
Project Structure: Multi-project hierarchy with shared VPC

Networking: VPC, subnets, Cloud NAT, Private Google Access

Compute: GKE cluster (Standard or Autopilot) + Cloud Run

Storage: Artifact Registry, Cloud Storage, Filestore if needed

Database: Cloud SQL (PostgreSQL) with backups and HA

Caching: Memorystore (Redis) cluster

Messaging: Pub/Sub topics and subscriptions

CI/CD: Cloud Build triggers and Cloud Deploy pipelines

Service Mesh: Anthos Service Mesh (Istio)

Observability: Cloud Monitoring dashboards and alerts

Security: Workload Identity, Secret Manager, VPC Service Controls

Cost: Budget alerts, committed use discounts, cost labels

Compliance: Binary Authorization, Container Analysis

Key GCP Differentiators
GKE Autopilot: GCP's fully managed K8s where you pay per pod, not per node

Cloud Run: Most mature serverless container platform (scale to zero, HTTP/gRPC)

Workload Identity: Best-in-class K8s to IAM integration

Global Network: Google's private backbone for lower latency

Cloud Spanner: Only globally distributed, strongly consistent SQL database

Start with Cloud Run for new services (simplest, most cost-effective), use GKE Autopilot for complex stateful workloads, and only use GKE Standard when you need node-level control. GCP's managed services dramatically reduce operational overhead compared to other clouds!

What are the things need to take in to considerations while migrating from On Prem to GCP?
Migrating On-Prem Microservices to GCP: Complete Considerations Guide
Migrating from on-premise to Google Cloud Platform (GCP) is a complex journey that requires careful planning across technical, operational, financial, and organizational dimensions. Unlike lift-and-shift, microservices migration demands a strategic approach to fully leverage cloud benefits.

The Migration Spectrum: From Lift-and-Shift to Cloud-Native
text
ON-PREM                               GCP CLOUD-NATIVE
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Lift & Shift (Rehost) ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────►
├── Same VMs, just moved to Compute Engine
├── Minimal changes, fastest migration
├── Limited cloud benefits
└── "Same problems, different location"

Replatform ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────►
├── Move to managed services (Cloud SQL, Memorystore)
├── Moderate changes, good balance
├── Reduced operational overhead
└── "Better, but not fully optimized"

Refactor (Re-architect) ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────►
├── Cloud-native redesign (Cloud Run, GKE, Pub/Sub)
├── Significant changes, longer timeline
├── Full cloud benefits (scale, resilience, cost)
└── "Built for cloud, not just running in cloud"

Retire ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────►
├── Decommission unused/legacy services
├── Zero migration effort
├── Eliminate technical debt
└── "Why migrate what you don't need?"

Retain ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────►
├── Keep certain workloads on-prem
├── Hybrid architecture
├── For compliance, latency, or data gravity
└── "Not everything belongs in cloud"
1. Technical Considerations
1.1 Compute & Container Strategy
yaml
Decision Matrix:
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ WORKLOAD TYPE              │ RECOMMENDED GCP SERVICE                        │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Stateless APIs, web apps   │ Cloud Run (serverless, scale to zero)         │
│ Batch processing           │ Cloud Run Jobs or GKE with spot instances     │
│ Stateful workloads         │ GKE Standard with persistent disks           │
│ Legacy apps (not container)│ Compute Engine (migrate VMs)                  │
│ ML training/inference      │ Vertex AI, GKE with GPUs                      │
│ Event-driven functions     │ Cloud Functions (2nd gen)                     │
│ General K8s workloads      │ GKE Autopilot (managed nodes)                 │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
Key Considerations:

Container readiness: Are your services containerized? If not, plan containerization before migration

State management: Stateful services need careful handling with persistent disks or Cloud Spanner

Startup time: Cloud Run has cold starts; use minimum instances for latency-sensitive services

Compute pricing: Committed use discounts (1-3 years) vs on-demand vs preemptible/spot

hcl
# Example: Choosing the right compute for different workloads
resource "google_compute_instance" "legacy_service" {
  # Lift-and-shift for complex legacy
  machine_type = "n2-standard-4"
  scheduling {
    automatic_restart = true
    preemptible       = false  # Production, no spot
  }
}

resource "google_cloud_run_v2_service" "modern_api" {

  template {
    scaling {
      min_instance_count = 1  # Avoid cold starts
      max_instance_count = 10
    }
  }
}

  enable_autopilot = true
}
1.2 Database Migration
Database migration is often the most challenging aspect. GCP offers multiple paths:

yaml
Database Migration Strategies:
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ON-PREM DB          │ GCP TARGET                    │ MIGRATION METHOD      │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ PostgreSQL          │ Cloud SQL for PostgreSQL      │ Database Migration    │
│                     │                              │ Service (DMS)         │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Oracle              │ Cloud SQL (PostgreSQL)        │ DMS + conversion      │
│                     │ Bare Metal Solution (Oracle)  │ (minimal changes)     │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ SQL Server          │ Cloud SQL for SQL Server      │ DMS + backup/restore  │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ MongoDB             │ Firestore or Bigtable         │ Dataflow templates    │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ MySQL               │ Cloud SQL for MySQL           │ DMS (CDC supported)   │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Cassandra           │ Bigtable                      │ Custom Dataflow job   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
Critical Database Considerations:

sql
-- 1. Data volume and transfer time
-- Calculate: Time = Data_Size_TB * 1000 / Bandwidth_Gbps / 3600 / 24 (days)

-- 2. Cutover strategy
-- Options:
-- a) Big Bang: All data at once (downtime required)
-- b) CDC: Change Data Capture (minimal downtime)
-- c) Dual-write: Write to both during migration (complex)

-- 3. Schema differences
-- GCP Cloud SQL may have different:
--   - Character set defaults
--   - Timezone handling
--   - Stored procedure syntax
--   - Reserved keywords

-- 4. Backup and recovery testing
-- Test restore procedures BEFORE cutover
Database Migration Service (DMS) Example:

hcl
# Database Migration Service configuration
resource "google_database_migration_service_connection_profile" "source" {
  connection_profile_id = "source-onprem-postgres"
  location             = "us-central1"
  
  postgresql {
    host     = var.onprem_db_host  # Via VPN/Interconnect
    port     = 5432
    username = var.onprem_db_user
    password = var.onprem_db_password
  }
}

resource "google_database_migration_service_migration_job" "main" {
  migration_job_id = "migrate-orders-db"
  location         = "us-central1"
  
  type = "CONTINUOUS"  # CDC for minimal downtime
  
  source      = google_database_migration_service_connection_profile.source.id
  destination = google_sql_database_instance.target.id
  
  dump_flags {
    dump_flag {
      name  = "pg_dump"
      value = "--no-owner"
    }
  }
}

Service Account Design for Migrated Services:
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ PRINCIPLE                     │ IMPLEMENTATION                             │

├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ No user-managed keys          │ Use Workload Identity for GKE             │
│                               │ Use service account impersonation         │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Environment separation        │ Different projects for dev/staging/prod   │
│                               │ (not just different SAs)                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Logging Migration Example:

yaml
# Fluent Bit configuration for sending on-prem logs to Cloud Logging
service:
  flush: 1
  log_level: info

pipeline:
  inputs:
    - name: tail
      path: /var/log/app/*.log
      parser: json
      tag: app-logs

  filters:
    - name: parser
      match: app-logs
      key_name: log
      parser: json
      reserve_data: true

  outputs:
    - name: stackdriver
      match: app-logs
      resource: k8s_container
      project_id: ${PROJECT_ID}
      location: ${REGION}
      credentials_file: /etc/fluent/service-account.json
2.2 CI/CD Pipeline Transformation
yaml
Before (On-Prem Jenkins) → After (Cloud Build + Cloud Deploy):

Old Pipeline:
├── Jenkins master (on-prem VM)
├── Build agents (on-prem)
├── Artifacts in Nexus (on-prem)
├── Deploy to on-prem K8s
└── Approval via email

New Pipeline:
├── Cloud Build (managed, pay-per-use)
├── Artifact Registry (managed, integrated)
├── Cloud Deploy (managed delivery pipeline)
├── GKE/Cloud Run deployment
└── Approval via Cloud Deploy (console or API)

Migration Strategy:
1. Run Cloud Build alongside Jenkins
2. Migrate services incrementally
3. Decommission Jenkins when all services migrated
Cloud Build Migration Configuration:

yaml
# cloudbuild.yaml - Equivalent of your Jenkins pipeline
steps:
  # Build - replaces Jenkins freestyle/maven job
  - name: 'gradle:8.0-jdk17'
    entrypoint: 'gradle'
    args: ['clean', 'build', '-x', 'test']
    
  # Test - replaces Jenkins test stage
  - name: 'gradle:8.0-jdk17'
    entrypoint: 'gradle'
    args: ['test']
    
  # Security scan - replaces SonarQube
  - name: 'gcr.io/cloud-builders/gcloud'
    args:
      - 'beta'
      - 'container'
      - 'scan'
      - '$_IMAGE_NAME'
      
  # Push to registry - replaces Nexus
  - name: 'gcr.io/cloud-builders/docker'
    args: ['push', '$_IMAGE_NAME']
    
  # Deploy - replaces kubectl apply
  - name: 'gcr.io/cloud-builders/gke-deploy'
    args:
      - 'run'
      - '--cluster=${_CLUSTER_NAME}'
      - '--location=${_LOCATION}'
      - '--image=${_IMAGE_NAME}'
2.3 Disaster Recovery & Business Continuity
yaml
DR Strategy for Migrated Services:
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ASPECT                    │ ON-PREM TO GCP MIGRATION                       │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ RTO (Recovery Time)       │ Define new targets (GCP may offer better)     │
│                           │ Typical: 4 hours → 1 hour                     │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ RPO (Recovery Point)      │ Evaluate GCP capabilities                     │
│                           │ Cloud SQL: Point-in-time recovery (up to 7d) │
│                           │ GCS: Object versioning, multi-region          │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Multi-Region Strategy     │ GCP regions: us-central1, us-east1, us-west1  │
│                           │ Consider: data sovereignty, latency, cost     │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Backup Strategy           │ On-prem backups + GCP backups during migration│
│                           │ Post-migration: Cloud-native backups only    │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Failover Testing          │ Test GCP failover BEFORE cutover             │
│                           │ Document differences in procedure            │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
Velero (formerly Heptio Ark) for K8s DR:

yaml
# Velero backup schedule for GCP
apiVersion: velero.io/v1
kind: Schedule
metadata:
  name: microservices-backup
  namespace: velero
spec:
  schedule: "0 2 * * *"
  template:
    includedNamespaces:
      - microservices
    includedResources:
      - deployments
      - services
      - configmaps
      - secrets
      - persistentvolumeclaims
    storageLocation: gcp-primary
    volumeSnapshotLocations:
      - gcp-snapshot-location
    ttl: 720h

Cost Estimation Example:

python
# GCP Pricing Calculator script
compute_costs = {
    "GKE_Autopilot": {
        "cpu_per_hour": 0.033,  # per vCPU
        "memory_per_gb_hour": 0.008,  # per GB
    },
    "Cloud_Run": {
        "cpu_per_hour": 0.0324,  # per vCPU
        "memory_per_gb_hour": 0.005,
        "requests_per_million": 0.40,
    },
    "Cloud_SQL_PostgreSQL": {
        "db_n1_standard_4": 0.36,  # per hour
        "storage_per_gb_month": 0.17,
        "backup_storage_per_gb_month": 0.085,
    }
}

def estimate_monthly_cost():
    # Your on-prem workload metrics
    on_prem_metrics = {
        "total_vcpus": 200,
        "total_memory_gb": 512,
        "database_size_gb": 500,
        "api_requests_per_month": 50_000_000,
    }
    
    # Estimate GCP cost
    gke_cost = (on_prem_metrics["total_vcpus"] * 730 * 0.033 +  # 730 hours/month
                on_prem_metrics["total_memory_gb"] * 730 * 0.008)
    
    print(f"Estimated GKE Autopilot cost: ${gke_cost:.2f}/month")

│                             │ Match access patterns                     │

7. Risk Mitigation Checklist
Pre-Migration Risks
yaml
☐ Data Transfer: Test transfer speeds; have backup plan (Transfer Appliance)
☐ Network Latency: Measure baseline; identify latency-sensitive workloads
☐ IP Address Conflicts: Plan addressing for hybrid period
☐ DNS Resolution: Test split-brain DNS scenarios
☐ Certificate Management: Migrate certs to Cloud Load Balancing
☐ Backup Validation: Restore from GCP backups before cutover
☐ Rollback Plan: Document step-by-step rollback procedure
During-Migration Risks
yaml
☐ Application Compatibility: Test all integrations
☐ Performance Degradation: Baseline on-prem, compare to GCP
☐ Data Consistency: Verify CDC tools are capturing all changes
☐ Session Management: Handle session affinity during migration
☐ API Gateway: Update routes gradually
☐ Monitoring Gaps: Ensure observability on both sides
Post-Migration Risks
yaml
☐ Cost Overrun: Set budgets and alerts
☐ Security Misconfiguration: Run Security Command Center
☐ Compliance Violation: Validate Assured Workloads settings
☐ Documentation Outdated: Update runbooks and architecture docs
☐ Skill Gaps: Plan for ongoing training
8. Success Criteria & KPIs
yaml
Technical KPIs:
├── Application Performance: ≤ 10% degradation from on-prem
├── Availability: ≥ 99.9% (or better than on-prem)
├── Response Time: P99 < baseline + 20%
├── Error Rate: < 0.1% of all requests
├── Recovery Time: RTO achieved (e.g., 4 hours)
├── Recovery Point: RPO achieved (e.g., 1 hour)

Business KPIs:
├── Migration Cost: Within 20% of estimate
├── Timeline: Within 20% of plan
├── User Satisfaction: No increase in tickets
├── Team Velocity: Return to normal within 2 weeks
└── Cloud Maturity: Achieve target cloud-native score

Operational KPIs:
├── MTTR (Mean Time to Repair): Better than on-prem
├── Deployment Frequency: Increased by 50%
├── Lead Time for Changes: Decreased by 40%
├── Change Failure Rate: < 15%
└── Cost per Transaction: Lower than on-prem
9. Quick Reference: Migration Decision Tree
yaml
Start with service assessment:

Is service still needed?
├── No → Retire/Decommission
└── Yes → Continue

Does service have compliance restrictions?
├── Yes → Retain on-prem or use Assured Workloads
└── Yes → Can modernize?

Can service be containerized easily?
├── Yes → Use GKE (Autopilot for most, Standard for complex)
└── No → Is service critical?

Is service stateless API?
├── Yes → Cloud Run (serverless, scale to zero)
├── No, but batch/async → Cloud Run Jobs or Cloud Tasks
└── No, stateful with complex requirements → Compute Engine (VMs)

Does service require special hardware (GPU, large memory)?
├── Yes → Compute Engine with sole-tenant nodes
└── No → Standard compute options above

Database migration:
├── Relational (PostgreSQL, MySQL) → Cloud SQL
├── Relational (Oracle, SQL Server) → Cloud SQL with DMS
├── Global scale, strong consistency → Cloud Spanner
├── NoSQL, real-time → Firestore
├── High-throughput, analytical → Bigtable
└── Legacy, complex → Bare Metal Solution (Oracle)
10. Migration Readiness Assessment
Score your organization (1-5):

yaml
Technical Readiness:
___ Applications containerized or containerizable
___ Code ready for cloud (no hardcoded paths, IPs)
___ Database schemas documented and optimized
___ Network architecture supports hybrid connectivity
___ CI/CD pipelines can target multiple environments

Operational Readiness:
___ Team trained on GCP fundamentals
___ Monitoring and alerting defined for cloud
___ Backup and DR procedures documented
___ Incident response plan includes cloud
___ On-call rotation covers cloud services

Financial Readiness:
___ Budget approved for migration
___ Cost estimation completed
___ Committed use discounts planned
___ Chargeback/showback model defined

Security Readiness:
___ IAM strategy defined
___ Encryption key management planned
___ Compliance requirements mapped to GCP
___ Security monitoring integrated
___ Penetration testing scheduled

Scoring:
5-10: Not ready - Start with training and pilot
11-20: Getting there - Begin foundation setup
21-25: Ready - Proceed with migration
This comprehensive guide should help you plan and execute a successful migration from on-premise to GCP. The key is to start small, learn fast, and iterate rather than attempting a "big bang" migration of everything at once.

Managing Microservice Configuration in Spring Boot: Complete Hands-On Guide
Configuration management is critical in microservices. Unlike monoliths with a single application.properties, microservices need externalized, environment-specific, and often dynamic configuration. This guide covers the complete spectrum from simple to advanced patterns.

The Configuration Challenge

Step 1: Spring Cloud Config Server
1.1 Create Config Server Project
java
// ConfigServerApplication.java
package com.example.config;

import org.springframework.boot.SpringApplication;
import org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.SpringBootApplication;
import org.springframework.cloud.config.server.EnableConfigServer;

/**
 * Spring Cloud Config Server
 * 
 * This service acts as a central configuration hub for all microservices.
 * It serves configuration from a Git repository as REST endpoints.
 * 
 * Endpoints:
 * - GET /{application}/{profile}/{label}
 * - GET /{application}-{profile}.yml
 * - GET /{application}-{profile}.properties
 * 
 * Example: GET /order-service/prod/latest
 * Returns configuration for order-service in production environment
 */
@SpringBootApplication
@EnableConfigServer  // Enables Config Server functionality
public class ConfigServerApplication {
    
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        SpringApplication.run(ConfigServerApplication.class, args);
    }
}

1.4 Config Server Security Configuration
java
// SecurityConfig.java
package com.example.config;

import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;
import org.springframework.security.config.annotation.web.builders.HttpSecurity;
import org.springframework.security.config.annotation.web.configuration.EnableWebSecurity;
import org.springframework.security.core.userdetails.User;
import org.springframework.security.core.userdetails.UserDetails;
import org.springframework.security.provisioning.InMemoryUserDetailsManager;
import org.springframework.security.crypto.bcrypt.BCryptPasswordEncoder;
import org.springframework.security.crypto.password.PasswordEncoder;

/**
 * Security configuration for Config Server
 * 
 * Protects configuration endpoints with HTTP Basic authentication.
 * In production, consider OAuth2/JWT integration.
 */
@Configuration
@EnableWebSecurity
public class SecurityConfig {
    
    @Bean
    public PasswordEncoder passwordEncoder() {
        return new BCryptPasswordEncoder();
    }
    
    @Bean
    public InMemoryUserDetailsManager userDetailsService() {
        UserDetails configUser = User.builder()
            .username("config-service")
            .password(passwordEncoder().encode("${CONFIG_SERVER_PASSWORD}"))
            .roles("CONFIG")
            .build();
        
        UserDetails adminUser = User.builder()
            .username("config-admin")
            .password(passwordEncoder().encode("${CONFIG_ADMIN_PASSWORD}"))
            .roles("CONFIG", "ADMIN")
            .build();
        
        return new InMemoryUserDetailsManager(configUser, adminUser);
    }
    
    @Bean
    public SecurityFilterChain filterChain(HttpSecurity http) throws Exception {
        http
            .csrf().disable()
            .authorizeHttpRequests()
                .requestMatchers("/encrypt/**", "/decrypt/**").hasRole("ADMIN")  // Encrypt endpoints protected
                .anyRequest().authenticated()
            .and()
            .httpBasic();  // HTTP Basic authentication
        
        return http.build();
    }
}

2.3 Configuration Properties Classes
java
// OrderServiceProperties.java
package com.example.order.config;

import org.springframework.boot.context.properties.ConfigurationProperties;
import org.springframework.cloud.context.config.annotation.RefreshScope;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;

import java.math.BigDecimal;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Map;

/**
 * Configuration properties for Order Service
 * 
 * Binds configuration from Spring Cloud Config to this class.
 * Properties are automatically refreshed when configuration changes.
 * 
 * @RefreshScope: Allows properties to be updated without restarting
 * @ConfigurationProperties: Maps properties with prefix "order-service"
 */
@Component
@RefreshScope  // Enables dynamic refresh without restart
@ConfigurationProperties(prefix = "order-service")
public class OrderServiceProperties {
    
    // ===== Business Configuration =====
    
    /** Maximum number of items allowed in a single order */
    private int maxItemsPerOrder = 50;
    
    /** Whether backorders are allowed for out-of-stock items */
    private boolean allowBackorders = false;
    
    /** Default shipping method if none specified */
    private String defaultShippingMethod = "STANDARD";
    
    // ===== Feature Flags =====
    
    /** Feature flags for toggling functionality */
    private FeatureFlags features = new FeatureFlags();
    
    // ===== External Service Endpoints =====
    
    /** URLs for dependent microservices */
    private Map<String, String> services;
    
    // ===== Retry Configuration =====
    
    /** Retry configuration for failed operations */
    private RetryConfig retry = new RetryConfig();
    
    // ===== Discount Configuration =====
    
    /** Discount rates by customer tier */
    private Map<String, BigDecimal> discountRates;
    
    /** Minimum order amount for free shipping */
    private BigDecimal freeShippingThreshold;
    
    // Getters and Setters
    public static class FeatureFlags {
        private boolean enableDiscounts = true;
        private boolean enableGiftWrapping = false;
        private boolean enableRushShipping = true;
        
        // Getters and setters
    }
    
    public static class RetryConfig {
        private int maxAttempts = 3;
        private long backoffDelay = 1000;
        
        // Getters and setters
    }
    
    // Getters and setters for all fields
}
2.4 Service Using Configuration
java
// OrderService.java
package com.example.order.service;

import com.example.order.config.OrderServiceProperties;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Value;
import org.springframework.cloud.context.config.annotation.RefreshScope;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;
import org.springframework.web.client.RestTemplate;

import javax.annotation.PostConstruct;

/**
 * Order Service with dynamic configuration
 * 
 * Configuration can be updated at runtime via:
 * POST /actuator/refresh (single service)
 * POST /bus/refresh (all services via Spring Cloud Bus)
 */
@Service
@Slf4j
@RefreshScope  // Recreates this bean when configuration changes
public class OrderService {
    
    // Option 1: Type-safe configuration using @ConfigurationProperties
    @Autowired
    private OrderServiceProperties orderProperties;
    
    // Option 2: Direct injection using @Value
    @Value("${order-service.max-items-per-order:50}")
    private int maxItemsPerOrder;
    
    @Value("${order-service.features.enable-discounts:true}")
    private boolean discountsEnabled;
    
    @Value("${spring.datasource.url}")
    private String databaseUrl;  // From config server
    
    @Value("${secrets.api-key:}")
    private String apiKey;  // Encrypted in config server
    
    private final RestTemplate restTemplate = new RestTemplate();
    
    /**
     * Creates a new order with configuration-aware validation
     */
    public Order createOrder(CreateOrderRequest request) {
        log.info("Creating order with max items: {}", maxItemsPerOrder);
        log.info("Discounts enabled: {}", discountsEnabled);
        log.info("Current max items per order: {}", orderProperties.getMaxItemsPerOrder());
        
        // Validate using configuration
        if (request.getItems().size() > maxItemsPerOrder) {
            throw new BusinessException(
                String.format("Order exceeds maximum items (%d)", maxItemsPerOrder)
            );
        }
        
        // Apply discount if enabled
        BigDecimal finalAmount = request.getTotalAmount();
        if (discountsEnabled) {
            BigDecimal discountRate = orderProperties.getDiscountRates()
                .getOrDefault(request.getCustomerTier(), BigDecimal.ZERO);
            finalAmount = finalAmount.multiply(BigDecimal.ONE.subtract(discountRate));
            log.info("Applied {}% discount for tier: {}", 
                     discountRate.multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(100)), 
                     request.getCustomerTier());
        }
        
        // Use configured service URLs
        String inventoryUrl = orderProperties.getServices().get("inventory-service");
        restTemplate.postForObject(inventoryUrl + "/reserve", request, Void.class);
        
        Order order = Order.builder()
            .items(request.getItems())
            .totalAmount(finalAmount)
            .shippingMethod(orderProperties.getDefaultShippingMethod())
            .build();
        
        log.info("Order created successfully");
        return order;
    }
    
    /**
     * Logs current configuration on startup
     */
    @PostConstruct
    public void logConfiguration() {
        log.info("=== Order Service Configuration ===");
        log.info("Max Items Per Order: {}", orderProperties.getMaxItemsPerOrder());
        log.info("Allow Backorders: {}", orderProperties.isAllowBackorders());
        log.info("Default Shipping Method: {}", orderProperties.getDefaultShippingMethod());
        log.info("Features: {}", orderProperties.getFeatures());
        log.info("Services: {}", orderProperties.getServices());
        log.info("Database URL: {}", databaseUrl);
        log.info("===================================");
    }
    
    /**
     * Refreshes configuration programmatically
     */
    public void refreshConfiguration() {
        // This method would be called by a management endpoint
        log.info("Configuration refresh triggered");
        // The bean will be recreated due to @RefreshScope
        // New values will be injected on next method call
    }
}
2.5 Configuration Refresh Controller
java
// ConfigRefreshController.java
package com.example.order.controller;

import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.cloud.context.refresh.ContextRefresher;
import org.springframework.core.env.Environment;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;

import java.util.Arrays;
import java.util.HashMap;
import java.util.Map;
import java.util.Set;

/**
 * Management endpoint for configuration operations
 * 
 * Endpoints:
 * - POST /actuator/refresh (built-in)
 * - POST /api/config/refresh - Custom refresh endpoint
 * - GET /api/config/properties - List all properties
 * - GET /api/config/diff - Show configuration changes
 */
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/config")
@Slf4j
public class ConfigRefreshController {
    
    @Autowired
    private ContextRefresher contextRefresher;
    
    @Autowired
    private Environment environment;
    
    /**
     * Manually refresh configuration
     * 
     * Usage: POST /api/config/refresh
     * 
     * This triggers a reload of configuration from Config Server
     * and recreates all @RefreshScope beans
     */
    @PostMapping("/refresh")
    public Map<String, Object> refreshConfiguration() {
        log.info("Manual configuration refresh triggered");
        
        long startTime = System.currentTimeMillis();
        Set<String> updatedProperties = contextRefresher.refresh();
        long duration = System.currentTimeMillis() - startTime;
        
        Map<String, Object> response = new HashMap<>();
        response.put("status", "refreshed");
        response.put("updatedProperties", updatedProperties);
        response.put("count", updatedProperties.size());
        response.put("durationMs", duration);
        response.put("timestamp", System.currentTimeMillis());
        
        log.info("Refreshed {} properties in {} ms", updatedProperties.size(), duration);
        
        return response;
    }
    
    /**
     * Get all configuration properties
     * 
     * Usage: GET /api/config/properties
     * 
     * Returns all properties from all sources (config server, local, env vars)
     */
    @GetMapping("/properties")
    public Map<String, Object> getAllProperties() {
        Map<String, Object> properties = new HashMap<>();
        
        // Get all active property sources
        for (org.springframework.core.env.PropertySource<?> propertySource : 
             ((org.springframework.core.env.AbstractEnvironment) environment).getPropertySources()) {
            
            String sourceName = propertySource.getName();
            if (sourceName.contains("configserver") || 
                sourceName.contains("application") ||
                sourceName.contains("bootstrap")) {
                
                Map<String, Object> sourceProperties = new HashMap<>();
                if (propertySource.getSource() instanceof Map) {
                    ((Map<String, Object>) propertySource.getSource()).forEach((key, value) -> {
                        // Mask sensitive values
                        if (isSensitiveKey(key)) {
                            sourceProperties.put(key, "********");
                        } else {
                            sourceProperties.put(key, value);
                        }
                    });
                }
                properties.put(sourceName, sourceProperties);
            }
        }
        
        return properties;
    }
    
    /**
     * Check if a property key is sensitive
     */
    private boolean isSensitiveKey(String key) {
        String lowerKey = key.toLowerCase();
        return lowerKey.contains("password") ||
               lowerKey.contains("secret") ||
               lowerKey.contains("key") ||
               lowerKey.contains("token") ||
               lowerKey.contains("credential");
    }
}

// OrderServiceApplication.java
package com.example.order;

import org.springframework.boot.SpringApplication;
import org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.SpringBootApplication;

/**
 * Order Service Main Application
 * 
 * This service demonstrates configuration management:
 * 1. Bootstrap.yml points to Config Server
 * 2. Configuration loaded from Git repository
 * 3. @RefreshScope enables dynamic updates
 * 4. Environment-specific config (dev/prod)
 * 5. Encrypted sensitive values
 */
@SpringBootApplication
@EnableDiscoveryClient  // Optional: For service discovery integration
@EnableScheduling       // For scheduled config refresh (if needed)
@EnableConfigurationProperties(com.example.order.config.OrderServiceProperties.class)
public class OrderServiceApplication {
    
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        SpringApplication.run(OrderServiceApplication.class, args);
    }
}
