Step 3: Dynamic Configuration Update
3.1 Configuration Refresh Endpoints
bash
# Refresh a single service
curl -X POST http://order-service:8080/actuator/refresh \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json"

# Response example:
# ["order-service.max-items-per-order", "order-service.features.enable-discounts"]

# Refresh all services via Spring Cloud Bus (with RabbitMQ)
curl -X POST http://config-server:8888/monitor \
  -H "X-Github-Event: push" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json"

# Or use bus endpoint directly
curl -X POST http://order-service:8080/actuator/bus-refresh \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json"
3.2 Webhook for Automatic Refresh
java
// ConfigWebhookController.java
package com.example.config;

import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.cloud.context.refresh.ContextRefresher;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;

import java.util.Map;

/**
 * GitHub/GitLab webhook receiver
 * 
 * Automatically refreshes configuration when config repository changes
 */
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/webhook")
@Slf4j
public class ConfigWebhookController {
    
    @Autowired
    private ContextRefresher contextRefresher;
    
    /**
     * Receives GitHub push events and triggers config refresh
     * 
     * Configure GitHub webhook to POST to this endpoint
     */
    @PostMapping("/github")
    public Map<String, String> handleGitHubWebhook(
            @RequestHeader("X-GitHub-Event") String event,
            @RequestBody Map<String, Object> payload) {
        
        log.info("Received GitHub event: {}", event);
        
        if ("push".equals(event)) {
            String repository = (String) ((Map) payload.get("repository")).get("full_name");
            String branch = ((String) payload.get("ref")).replace("refs/heads/", "");
            
            log.info("Configuration changed in {}/{} - refreshing", repository, branch);
            
            // Trigger refresh
            Set<String> updatedProperties = contextRefresher.refresh();
            log.info("Refreshed properties: {}", updatedProperties);
            
            return Map.of(
                "status", "refreshed",
                "repository", repository,
                "branch", branch,
                "properties", updatedProperties.toString()
            );
        }
        
        return Map.of("status", "ignored", "event", event);
    }
}
3.3 Scheduled Configuration Refresh
java
// ScheduledConfigRefresh.java
package com.example.order.scheduler;

import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.cloud.context.refresh.ContextRefresher;
import org.springframework.scheduling.annotation.Scheduled;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;

/**
 * Scheduled configuration refresh
 * 
 * Periodically fetches latest configuration from Config Server
 * Useful if webhooks aren't feasible
 */
@Component
@Slf4j
public class ScheduledConfigRefresh {
    
    @Autowired
    private ContextRefresher contextRefresher;
    
    /**
     * Refresh configuration every 5 minutes
     * 
     * Adjust based on how often your configuration changes
     */
    @Scheduled(fixedDelay = 300000)  // 5 minutes
    public void refreshConfiguration() {
        log.debug("Scheduled configuration refresh");
        
        try {
            Set<String> updatedProperties = contextRefresher.refresh();
            
            if (!updatedProperties.isEmpty()) {
                log.info("Configuration refreshed. Changed properties: {}", updatedProperties);
            }
        } catch (Exception e) {
            log.error("Failed to refresh configuration", e);
        }
    }
}
Step 4: Encryption and Decryption
4.1 Encrypting Sensitive Values
bash
# Encrypt a value using Config Server
curl -X POST http://localhost:8888/encrypt \
  -H "Content-Type: text/plain" \
  -d "my-secret-password"

# Response:
# AQAn...encrypted-value...

# Decrypt (requires admin role)
curl -X POST http://localhost:8888/decrypt \
  -H "Content-Type: text/plain" \
  -d "AQAn...encrypted-value..."

# Response:
# my-secret-password

# Use encrypted value in config
# password: '{cipher}AQAn...encrypted-value...'
4.2 Encryption Utility Class
java
// EncryptionUtil.java
package com.example.config.util;

import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Value;
import org.springframework.core.env.Environment;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;
import org.springframework.web.client.RestTemplate;

import javax.crypto.Cipher;
import javax.crypto.SecretKey;
import javax.crypto.spec.SecretKeySpec;
import java.util.Base64;

/**
 * Utility for encrypting/decrypting configuration values
 * 
 * Used for generating encrypted values for config files
 */
@Component
public class EncryptionUtil {
    
    @Autowired
    private Environment environment;
    
    private final RestTemplate restTemplate = new RestTemplate();
    
    /**
     * Encrypt a value using Config Server
     */
    public String encrypt(String plainText) {
        String configServerUrl = environment.getProperty("spring.cloud.config.uri", "http://localhost:8888");
        String username = environment.getProperty("spring.cloud.config.username", "config-service");
        String password = environment.getProperty("spring.cloud.config.password", "");
        
        String url = configServerUrl + "/encrypt";
        
        // Use RestTemplate with basic auth
        // Implementation omitted for brevity
        
        return restTemplate.postForObject(url, plainText, String.class);
    }
    
    /**
     * Encrypt using local symmetric key (fallback)
     */
    public String encryptLocal(String plainText, String key) throws Exception {
        SecretKey secretKey = new SecretKeySpec(key.getBytes(), "AES");
        Cipher cipher = Cipher.getInstance("AES/GCM/NoPadding");
        cipher.init(Cipher.ENCRYPT_MODE, secretKey);
        
        byte[] encryptedBytes = cipher.doFinal(plainText.getBytes());
        return Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(encryptedBytes);
    }
}
Step 5: Configuration Validation
5.1 Configuration Health Check
java
// ConfigHealthIndicator.java
package com.example.order.health;

import org.springframework.boot.actuate.health.Health;
import org.springframework.boot.actuate.health.HealthIndicator;
import org.springframework.core.env.ConfigurableEnvironment;
import org.springframework.core.env.PropertySource;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;

import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;

/**
 * Health indicator for configuration status
 * 
 * Reports:
 * - Config server connectivity
 * - Configuration source (remote vs local)
 * - Required properties presence
 */
@Component
public class ConfigHealthIndicator implements HealthIndicator {
    
    private final ConfigurableEnvironment environment;
    
    public ConfigHealthIndicator(ConfigurableEnvironment environment) {
        this.environment = environment;
    }
    
    @Override
    public Health health() {
        List<String> configSources = new ArrayList<>();
        boolean hasRemoteConfig = false;
        
        // Check configuration sources
        for (PropertySource<?> propertySource : environment.getPropertySources()) {
            String name = propertySource.getName();
            configSources.add(name);
            
            if (name.contains("configserver")) {
                hasRemoteConfig = true;
            }
        }
        
        Health.Builder builder;
        
        if (hasRemoteConfig) {
            builder = Health.up();
            builder.withDetail("status", "Using remote configuration from Config Server");
        } else {
            builder = Health.down();
            builder.withDetail("status", "Using LOCAL configuration only - Config Server unreachable!");
        }
        
        builder
            .withDetail("configSources", configSources)
            .withDetail("activeProfiles", environment.getActiveProfiles())
            .withDetail("defaultProfiles", environment.getDefaultProfiles());
        
        // Check critical configuration
        checkRequiredProperty(builder, "spring.datasource.url", "Database URL");
        checkRequiredProperty(builder, "order-service.max-items-per-order", "Business rule");
        
        return builder.build();
    }
    
    private void checkRequiredProperty(Health.Builder builder, String key, String description) {
        if (environment.containsProperty(key)) {
            String value = environment.getProperty(key);
            if (value != null && !value.isEmpty() && !value.startsWith("${")) {
                builder.withDetail(key + "_status", "✓ " + description + " configured");
            } else {
                builder.withDetail(key + "_status", "⚠ " + description + " has unresolved placeholder");
            }
        } else {
            builder.withDetail(key + "_status", "✗ " + description + " MISSING");
        }
    }
}
5.2 Configuration Validation on Startup
java
// ConfigValidationListener.java
package com.example.order.config;

import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Value;
import org.springframework.boot.context.event.ApplicationReadyEvent;
import org.springframework.context.ApplicationListener;
import org.springframework.core.env.AbstractEnvironment;
import org.springframework.core.env.EnumerablePropertySource;
import org.springframework.core.env.PropertySource;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;

import java.util.Arrays;
import java.util.Set;
import java.util.stream.Collectors;

/**
 * Validates configuration after application startup
 * 
 * Logs all configuration sources and validates critical properties
 */
@Component
@Slf4j
public class ConfigValidationListener implements ApplicationListener<ApplicationReadyEvent> {
    
    @Value("${order-service.max-items-per-order:50}")
    private int maxItemsPerOrder;
    
    @Value("${order-service.allow-backorders:false}")
    private boolean allowBackorders;
    
    @Override
    public void onApplicationEvent(ApplicationReadyEvent event) {
        AbstractEnvironment environment = (AbstractEnvironment) event.getApplicationContext().getEnvironment();
        
        log.info("=== Configuration Validation ===");
        
        // Log all property sources
        log.info("Property Sources:");
        for (PropertySource<?> propertySource : environment.getPropertySources()) {
            log.info("  - {} ({} properties)", 
                     propertySource.getName(),
                     propertySource.getSource() instanceof EnumerablePropertySource ? 
                         ((EnumerablePropertySource) propertySource).getPropertyNames().length : "?");
        }
        
        // Log active profiles
        log.info("Active Profiles: {}", Arrays.toString(environment.getActiveProfiles()));
        
        // Validate critical properties
        log.info("Critical Configuration Validation:");
        log.info("  ✓ maxItemsPerOrder: {} (default: 50)", maxItemsPerOrder);
        log.info("  ✓ allowBackorders: {}", allowBackorders);
        
        // Check if using remote config
        boolean hasRemoteConfig = environment.getPropertySources().stream()
            .anyMatch(ps -> ps.getName().contains("configserver"));
        
        if (!hasRemoteConfig) {
            log.warn("⚠ WARNING: Using local configuration only! Config Server may be unavailable.");
        }
        
        log.info("================================");
    }
}
Step 6: Configuration Migration from Local to Centralized
6.1 Configuration Migration Script
bash
#!/bin/bash
# migrate-config.sh
# Migrates configuration from local application.yml to centralized Config Server

set -e

CONFIG_REPO_PATH="/path/to/microservices-config"
SERVICE_NAME="order-service"

echo "Migrating configuration for ${SERVICE_NAME}"

# Extract configuration from local application.yml
# This is a simplified example - use yq (YAML processor) for production

# Create service-specific config file
cat > ${CONFIG_REPO_PATH}/${SERVICE_NAME}.yml << EOF
# ${SERVICE_NAME} configuration
# Migrated from local application.yml on $(date)

order-service:
  # Business rules
  max-items-per-order: 50
  allow-backorders: false
  default-shipping-method: STANDARD
  
  # Feature flags
  features:
    enable-discounts: true
    enable-gift-wrapping: false
    enable-rush-shipping: true
  
  # External services
  services:
    inventory-service: http://inventory-service:8080
    payment-service: http://payment-service:8080

# Database configuration (without credentials - use secrets)
spring:
  datasource:
    url: jdbc:postgresql://postgres:5432/order_db
    # username and password moved to secrets

# Keep sensitive values in environment
secrets:
  api-key: \${API_KEY}
EOF

# Create environment-specific overrides
cat > ${CONFIG_REPO_PATH}/${SERVICE_NAME}-prod.yml << EOF
order-service:
  max-items-per-order: 20  # Lower in production
  features:
    enable-gift-wrapping: false  # Disabled in prod
  
  services:
    inventory-service: http://inventory-service.microservices.svc.cluster.local:8080
EOF

echo "Configuration migrated to ${CONFIG_REPO_PATH}"
echo "Commit and push the changes, then restart services"

Summary: Configuration Management Decision Tree
yaml
Need configuration management for microservices?

Is your configuration static?
├── Yes → Use simple Config Server with Git backend
└── No → Configuration changes frequently?

Does configuration contain secrets?
├── Yes → Use HashiCorp Vault or GCP/AWS Secrets Manager
└── No → Config Server with encryption

Need real-time updates?
├── Yes → Use @RefreshScope + Spring Cloud Bus
└── No → Simple Config Server (restart on change)

Multiple environments?
├── Yes → Use profile-specific files (dev, staging, prod)
└── No → Single configuration file

Service-specific configuration?
├── Yes → Use service-specific files ({service-name}.yml)
└── No → Use application.yml only

Need configuration versioning and audit?
├── Yes → Git backend (strongly recommended)
└── No → File system backend (not recommended)

Recommended Stack:
1. Spring Cloud Config Server (centralized)
2. Git repository (versioning, audit)
3. Vault or Secrets Manager (secrets)
4. Spring Cloud Bus (dynamic refresh)
5. @RefreshScope (per-service updates)
6. Environment-specific profiles
This comprehensive guide gives you a production-ready configuration management setup for Spring Boot microservices!

How to manage microservice registration and discovery using Eureka, spring boot and java?
Microservice Registration and Discovery with Eureka: Complete Hands-On Guide
Service discovery is a fundamental pattern in microservices architecture. Instead of hardcoding IP addresses and ports, services register themselves with a discovery server, and clients query that server to find service locations dynamically.

Why Service Discovery?

1.2 Eureka Server Application
java
// EurekaServerApplication.java
package com.example.discovery;

import org.springframework.boot.SpringApplication;
import org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.SpringBootApplication;
import org.springframework.cloud.netflix.eureka.server.EnableEurekaServer;

/**
 * Eureka Server - Service Discovery Hub
 * 
 * This is the central registry where all microservices register themselves.
 * 
 * Key Responsibilities:
 * 1. Maintain registry of all available services
 * 2. Receive heartbeats to track service health
 * 3. Remove unhealthy services automatically
 * 4. Provide discovery API for clients
 * 
 * Dashboard: http://localhost:8761
 */
@SpringBootApplication
@EnableEurekaServer  // Enables Eureka Server functionality
public class EurekaServerApplication {
    
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        SpringApplication.run(EurekaServerApplication.class, args);
    }
}
1.3 Eureka Server Configuration
yaml
# application.yml (Eureka Server)
server:
  port: 8761  # Default Eureka port

spring:
  application:
    name: discovery-server
  
  # Security configuration (for production)
  security:
    user:
      name: eureka-user
      password: ${EUREKA_PASSWORD:eureka-password}
      roles: EUREKA

# Eureka Server Configuration
eureka:
  instance:
    hostname: localhost
    # For production, use actual hostname or IP
    # hostname: eureka-server.internal.example.com
    
    # Metadata for identification
    metadata-map:
      zone: us-east-1
      version: 1.0.0
      environment: ${ENVIRONMENT:development}
  
  client:
    # Eureka server does NOT register with itself
    register-with-eureka: false
    fetch-registry: false
    
    # Service URL for clients
    service-url:
      defaultZone: http://${eureka.instance.hostname}:${server.port}/eureka/
  
  server:
    # Cleanup interval for evicting dead instances (milliseconds)
    eviction-interval-timer-in-ms: 60000  # 60 seconds
    
    # Disable self-preservation in development (ENABLE in production!)
    enable-self-preservation: ${ENABLE_SELF_PRESERVATION:false}
    
    # Renewal threshold (percentage of expected heartbeats)
    renewal-percent-threshold: 0.85
    
    # Response cache update interval
    response-cache-update-interval-ms: 30000
    
    # Use read-only cache for better performance
    use-read-only-response-cache: true
    
    # Wait time for sync before removing instances
    wait-time-in-ms-when-sync-empty: 0
    
    # Peer node connectivity (for multi-node setup)
    peer-node-connect-timeout-ms: 2000
    peer-node-read-timeout-ms: 2000
    peer-node-total-connections: 1000
    peer-node-total-connections-per-host: 50
    
    # Rate limiter
    rate-limiter:
      enabled: false
      limit: 100
      burst-size: 50
    
    # Delta fetch configuration
    delta-retention-timer-interval-in-ms: 0
    
    # Disable dashboard CSRF (for development only)
    disable-dashboard-csrf: true

# Logging configuration
logging:
  level:
    com.netflix.eureka: INFO
    com.netflix.discovery: INFO
    org.springframework.cloud: INFO
  pattern:
    console: "%d{ISO8601} [%thread] %-5level %logger{36} - %msg%n"

# Management endpoints
management:
  endpoints:
    web:
      exposure:
        include: health,info,metrics
      base-path: /actuator
  endpoint:
    health:
      show-details: always
1.4 Eureka Server Security Configuration (Optional)
java
// SecurityConfig.java
package com.example.discovery.config;

import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;
import org.springframework.security.config.annotation.web.builders.HttpSecurity;
import org.springframework.security.config.annotation.web.configuration.EnableWebSecurity;
import org.springframework.security.core.userdetails.User;
import org.springframework.security.core.userdetails.UserDetails;
import org.springframework.security.provisioning.InMemoryUserDetailsManager;
import org.springframework.security.web.SecurityFilterChain;
import org.springframework.security.crypto.bcrypt.BCryptPasswordEncoder;
import org.springframework.security.crypto.password.PasswordEncoder;

/**
 * Security configuration for Eureka Server
 * 
 * Protects Eureka dashboard and API with HTTP Basic authentication.
 * Services must include credentials when registering.
 */
@Configuration
@EnableWebSecurity
public class SecurityConfig {
    
    @Bean
    public PasswordEncoder passwordEncoder() {
        return new BCryptPasswordEncoder();
    }
    
    @Bean
    public InMemoryUserDetailsManager userDetailsService() {
        UserDetails eurekaUser = User.builder()
            .username("eureka-user")
            .password(passwordEncoder().encode("${EUREKA_PASSWORD:eureka-password}"))
            .roles("EUREKA")
            .build();
        
        UserDetails adminUser = User.builder()
            .username("admin")
            .password(passwordEncoder().encode("${ADMIN_PASSWORD:admin-password}"))
            .roles("EUREKA", "ADMIN")
            .build();
        
        return new InMemoryUserDetailsManager(eurekaUser, adminUser);
    }
    
    @Bean
    public SecurityFilterChain filterChain(HttpSecurity http) throws Exception {
        http
            .csrf().disable()  // Disable CSRF for Eureka API
            .authorizeHttpRequests()
                .requestMatchers("/actuator/health", "/actuator/info").permitAll()
                .anyRequest().authenticated()
            .and()
            .httpBasic();  // HTTP Basic authentication
        
        return http.build();
    }
}

    implementation 'org.springframework.cloud:spring-cloud-starter-openfeign'
}
2.2 Client Application
java
// OrderServiceApplication.java
package com.example.order;

import org.springframework.boot.SpringApplication;
import org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.SpringBootApplication;
import org.springframework.cloud.client.discovery.EnableDiscoveryClient;
import org.springframework.cloud.openfeign.EnableFeignClients;

/**
 * Order Service with Eureka Discovery Client
 * 
 * This service:
 * 1. Registers itself with Eureka on startup
 * 2. Sends heartbeats every 30 seconds
 * 3. Deregisters on graceful shutdown
 * 4. Discovers other services via Eureka
 */
@SpringBootApplication
@EnableDiscoveryClient      // Enables Eureka service registration/discovery
@EnableFeignClients         // Enables Feign client for declarative REST calls
public class OrderServiceApplication {
    
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        SpringApplication.run(OrderServiceApplication.class, args);
    }
}
2.3 Client Configuration

# application.yml (Order Service)

  port: ${PORT:8080}  # Random port for multiple instances

  application:
    name: order-service  # IMPORTANT: This is the service ID used for discovery
  
  # Cloud configuration
  cloud:
    loadbalancer:
      ribbon:
        enabled: false  # Use Spring Cloud LoadBalancer (default)
      cache:
        ttl: 35s  # Cache service instances for 35 seconds

# Eureka Client Configuration
eureka:
  instance:
    # Unique instance ID (hostname:port)
    instance-id: ${spring.application.name}:${random.value}
    
    # Hostname (use IP for containerized environments)
    hostname: ${HOSTNAME:localhost}
    
    # Prefer IP address over hostname (important for Docker/K8s)
    prefer-ip-address: true
    
    # IP address (auto-detected, or set manually)
    ip-address: ${POD_IP:}
    
    # Non-secure port (the port this service listens on)
    non-secure-port: ${server.port}
    
    # Lease renewal (heartbeat) interval in seconds
    lease-renewal-interval-in-seconds: 30
    
    # Lease expiration duration (if no heartbeat, service is removed)
    lease-expiration-duration-in-seconds: 90
    
    # Metadata for filtering/grouping
    metadata-map:
      zone: us-east-1
      version: ${SERVICE_VERSION:1.0.0}
      environment: ${ENVIRONMENT:development}
      instance-type: ${INSTANCE_TYPE:default}

    # Enable registration with Eureka
    register-with-eureka: true

    fetch-registry: true
    
    # Eureka server URLs
    service-url:
      defaultZone: ${EUREKA_URL:http://localhost:8761/eureka/}

    # defaultZone: http://eureka-user:eureka-password@localhost:8761/eureka/
    
    # Health check
    healthcheck:
      enabled: true  # Use Spring Actuator health endpoint
    
    # Registry fetch interval
    registry-fetch-interval-seconds: 30

      only-up-instances: true
    
    # GZip compression for registry fetch
    g-zip-content: true

# Actuator configuration (for health checks)

  endpoints:
    web:
      exposure:
        include: health,info,metrics
  endpoint:
    health:
      show-details: always
      probes:
        enabled: true  # Enable Kubernetes probes
  health:
    defaults:
      enabled: true

# Logging
logging:
  level:
    com.netflix.discovery: INFO
    com.example: DEBUG
    org.springframework.cloud: DEBUG
Step 3: Service-to-Service Communication with Discovery
3.1 Using RestTemplate with Discovery and Load Balancing
java
// RestTemplateConfig.java
package com.example.order.config;

import org.springframework.cloud.client.loadbalancer.LoadBalanced;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;
import org.springframework.web.client.RestTemplate;

/**
 * RestTemplate configuration with load balancing
 * 
 * @LoadBalanced enables service discovery and client-side load balancing.
 * Instead of using IP addresses, use service names: "http://inventory-service/..."
 */
@Configuration
public class RestTemplateConfig {
    
    /**
     * Creates a load-balanced RestTemplate
     * 
     * Usage: restTemplate.getForObject("http://inventory-service/api/inventory/{id}", ...)
     * 
     * The service name "inventory-service" is resolved to actual instances via Eureka
     */
    @Bean
    @LoadBalanced  // Enables service discovery and load balancing
    public RestTemplate loadBalancedRestTemplate() {
        RestTemplate restTemplate = new RestTemplate();
        
        // Optional: Add custom interceptors
        restTemplate.getInterceptors().add((request, body, execution) -> {
            // Add correlation ID header
            request.getHeaders().add("X-Correlation-Id", java.util.UUID.randomUUID().toString());
            return execution.execute(request, body);
        });
        
        return restTemplate;
    }
    
    /**
     * Standard RestTemplate (without load balancing)
     * Use this for external APIs or direct IP calls
     */
    @Bean
    public RestTemplate restTemplate() {
        return new RestTemplate();
    }
}
3.2 Service Using Discovery with RestTemplate
java
// OrderService.java
package com.example.order.service;

import com.example.order.model.InventoryResponse;
import com.example.order.model.Order;
import com.example.order.model.PaymentResponse;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Qualifier;
import org.springframework.cloud.client.ServiceInstance;
import org.springframework.cloud.client.discovery.DiscoveryClient;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;
import org.springframework.web.client.RestTemplate;

import java.util.List;

@Service
@Slf4j
public class OrderService {
    
    // Load-balanced RestTemplate (uses Eureka discovery)
    @Autowired
    @Qualifier("loadBalancedRestTemplate")
    private RestTemplate lbRestTemplate;
    
    // DiscoveryClient for programmatic service lookup
    @Autowired
    private DiscoveryClient discoveryClient;
    
    /**
     * Option 1: Using LoadBalanced RestTemplate (Simplest)
     * 
     * Spring Cloud automatically resolves "inventory-service" to actual instance
     * using Eureka and performs client-side load balancing.
     */
    public InventoryResponse checkInventoryUsingLb(String productId) {
        log.info("Checking inventory for product: {}", productId);
        
        // Use service name (not IP/hostname!)
        String url = "http://inventory-service/api/inventory/" + productId;
        
        // RestTemplate with @LoadBalanced will:
        // 1. Query Eureka for "inventory-service" instances
        // 2. Apply load balancing (Round Robin by default)
        // 3. Make actual HTTP call to selected instance
        InventoryResponse response = lbRestTemplate.getForObject(url, InventoryResponse.class);
        
        log.info("Inventory check complete: {}", response);
        return response;
    }
    
    /**
     * Option 2: Programmatic discovery using DiscoveryClient
     * 
     * Use this when you need more control over instance selection
     * or want to implement custom load balancing logic.
     */
    public PaymentResponse processPaymentUsingDiscovery(String orderId, double amount) {
        log.info("Processing payment for order: {}, amount: {}", orderId, amount);
        
        // Get all instances of payment-service
        List<ServiceInstance> instances = discoveryClient.getInstances("payment-service");
        
        if (instances.isEmpty()) {
            throw new ServiceUnavailableException("No instances of payment-service available");
        }
        
        // Select instance (simple round-robin)
        ServiceInstance instance = selectInstance(instances);
        
        String url = instance.getUri().toString() + "/api/payments/process";
        log.info("Selected payment instance: {}", url);
        
        PaymentRequest request = new PaymentRequest(orderId, amount);
        PaymentResponse response = lbRestTemplate.postForObject(url, request, PaymentResponse.class);
        
        log.info("Payment processed: {}", response);
        return response;
    }
    
    /**
     * Option 3: Using service name with fallback
     */
    public InventoryResponse checkInventoryWithFallback(String productId) {
        String url = "http://inventory-service/api/inventory/" + productId;
        
        try {
            return lbRestTemplate.getForObject(url, InventoryResponse.class);
        } catch (Exception e) {
            log.error("Inventory service unavailable, using fallback: {}", e.getMessage());
            
            // Fallback response
            InventoryResponse fallback = new InventoryResponse();
            fallback.setProductId(productId);
            fallback.setAvailable(false);
            fallback.setMessage("Service temporarily unavailable");
            return fallback;
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Simple round-robin instance selector
     */
    private int currentIndex = 0;
    
    private synchronized ServiceInstance selectInstance(List<ServiceInstance> instances) {
        ServiceInstance instance = instances.get(currentIndex % instances.size());
        currentIndex++;
        return instance;
    }
}
3.3 Using Feign Client with Discovery (Declarative)
java
// InventoryFeignClient.java
package com.example.order.client;

import com.example.order.model.InventoryResponse;
import com.example.order.model.ReserveStockRequest;
import org.springframework.cloud.openfeign.FeignClient;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;

/**
 * Feign client with service discovery
 * 
 * @FeignClient(name = "inventory-service") - Uses Eureka to find the service
 * 
 * Feign automatically:
 * 1. Discovers service instances via Eureka
 * 2. Load balances requests
 * 3. Handles retries and circuit breakers
 */
@FeignClient(
    name = "inventory-service",  // Service name in Eureka
    fallback = InventoryClientFallback.class,  // Circuit breaker fallback
    configuration = FeignConfig.class
)
public interface InventoryFeignClient {
    
    /**
     * Get inventory for a product
     * 
     * Feign will resolve "inventory-service" to actual instances
     */
    @GetMapping("/api/inventory/{productId}")
    InventoryResponse getInventory(@PathVariable("productId") String productId);
    
    /**
     * Reserve stock
     */
    @PostMapping("/api/inventory/reserve")
    ReservationResponse reserveStock(@RequestBody ReserveStockRequest request);
    
    /**
     * Release stock
     */
    @PostMapping("/api/inventory/release")
    void releaseStock(@RequestBody ReleaseStockRequest request);
    
    /**
     * Batch inventory check
     */
    @PostMapping("/api/inventory/batch-check")
    List<InventoryResponse> batchCheckInventory(@RequestBody List<String> productIds);
}

// Feign Configuration
@Configuration
public class FeignConfig {
    
    /**
     * Feign request interceptor - adds headers for tracing
     */
    @Bean
    public RequestInterceptor requestInterceptor() {
        return requestTemplate -> {
            // Add correlation ID
            String correlationId = MDC.get("correlationId");
            if (correlationId != null) {
                requestTemplate.header("X-Correlation-Id", correlationId);
            }
            
            // Add service version
            requestTemplate.header("X-Service-Version", "1.0.0");
            
            // Add authentication token (if needed)
            // requestTemplate.header("Authorization", "Bearer " + getToken());
        };
    }
    
    /**
     * Feign error decoder for better error handling
     */
    @Bean
    public ErrorDecoder errorDecoder() {
        return (methodKey, response) -> {
            if (response.status() == 404) {
                return new ResourceNotFoundException("Service not found");
            }
            if (response.status() >= 500) {
                return new ServiceUnavailableException("Service temporarily unavailable");
            }
            return new RuntimeException("Feign error: " + response.status());
        };
    }
}

// Fallback implementation (circuit breaker)
@Component
@Slf4j
class InventoryClientFallback implements InventoryFeignClient {
    
    @Override
    public InventoryResponse getInventory(String productId) {
        log.warn("Fallback: Inventory service unavailable for product: {}", productId);
        
        InventoryResponse fallback = new InventoryResponse();
        fallback.setProductId(productId);
        fallback.setAvailable(false);
        fallback.setQuantity(0);
        fallback.setMessage("Service unavailable - using cached data");
        return fallback;
    }
    
    @Override
    public ReservationResponse reserveStock(ReserveStockRequest request) {
        log.error("Fallback: Cannot reserve stock, service unavailable");
        throw new ServiceUnavailableException("Inventory service is currently unavailable");
    }
    
    @Override
    public void releaseStock(ReleaseStockRequest request) {
        log.warn("Fallback: Cannot release stock, service unavailable");
        // Don't throw - this is a compensation action
    }
    
    @Override
    public List<InventoryResponse> batchCheckInventory(List<String> productIds) {
        log.warn("Fallback: Batch inventory check failed");
        return productIds.stream()
            .map(id -> {
                InventoryResponse response = new InventoryResponse();
                response.setProductId(id);
                response.setAvailable(false);
                return response;
            })
            .collect(Collectors.toList());
    }
}
3.4 Service Using Feign Client
java
// OrderServiceWithFeign.java
package com.example.order.service;

import com.example.order.client.InventoryFeignClient;
import com.example.order.client.PaymentFeignClient;
import com.example.order.model.Order;
import com.example.order.model.OrderRequest;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;

@Service
@Slf4j
public class OrderServiceWithFeign {
    
    @Autowired
    private InventoryFeignClient inventoryClient;
    
    @Autowired
    private PaymentFeignClient paymentClient;
    
    /**
     * Create order using Feign clients
     * 
     * Feign handles:
     * - Service discovery (via Eureka)
     * - Load balancing
     * - Retries (configured)
     * - Circuit breaking (with fallback)
     */
    public Order createOrder(OrderRequest request) {
        log.info("Creating order for customer: {}", request.getCustomerId());
        
        // 1. Check inventory using Feign (discovers inventory-service)
        InventoryResponse inventory = inventoryClient.getInventory(request.getProductId());
        
        if (inventory.getQuantity() < request.getQuantity()) {
            throw new InsufficientInventoryException("Not enough stock");
        }
        
        log.info("Inventory available for product: {}", request.getProductId());
        
        // 2. Reserve stock
        ReservationResponse reservation = inventoryClient.reserveStock(
            new ReserveStockRequest(request.getProductId(), request.getQuantity())
        );
        
        log.info("Stock reserved: {}", reservation.getReservationId());
        
        // 3. Process payment
        PaymentResponse payment = paymentClient.processPayment(
            new PaymentRequest(request.getCustomerId(), request.getTotalAmount())
        );
        
        log.info("Payment processed: {}", payment.getTransactionId());
        
        // 4. Create order
        Order order = Order.builder()
            .customerId(request.getCustomerId())
            .productId(request.getProductId())
            .quantity(request.getQuantity())
            .totalAmount(request.getTotalAmount())
            .paymentId(payment.getPaymentId())
            .reservationId(reservation.getReservationId())
            .status("CONFIRMED")
            .build();
        
        log.info("Order created successfully: {}", order.getId());
        return order;
    }
}
Step 4: Advanced Eureka Features
4.1 Custom Load Balancing Strategy
java
// CustomLoadBalancerConfiguration.java
package com.example.order.config;

import org.springframework.cloud.client.ServiceInstance;
import org.springframework.cloud.loadbalancer.core.ReactorLoadBalancer;
import org.springframework.cloud.loadbalancer.core.RoundRobinLoadBalancer;
import org.springframework.cloud.loadbalancer.core.ServiceInstanceListSupplier;
import org.springframework.cloud.loadbalancer.support.LoadBalancerClientFactory;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;
import org.springframework.core.env.Environment;

import java.util.List;
import java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicInteger;

/**
 * Custom load balancing strategies
 */
@Configuration
public class CustomLoadBalancerConfiguration {
    
    /**
     * Custom load balancer that prefers instances in the same zone
     */
    @Bean
    public ReactorLoadBalancer<ServiceInstance> zonePreferenceLoadBalancer(
            Environment environment,
            LoadBalancerClientFactory loadBalancerClientFactory) {
        
        String serviceId = loadBalancerClientFactory.getName(environment);
        
        return new ZonePreferenceLoadBalancer(
            loadBalancerClientFactory.getLazyProvider(serviceId, ServiceInstanceListSupplier.class),
            serviceId
        );
    }
    
    /**
     * Zone preference load balancer implementation
     */
    static class ZonePreferenceLoadBalancer extends RoundRobinLoadBalancer {
        
        private final String zone;
        
        public ZonePreferenceLoadBalancer(
                ObjectProvider<ServiceInstanceListSupplier> serviceInstanceListSupplierProvider,
                String serviceId) {
            super(serviceInstanceListSupplierProvider, serviceId);
            this.zone = System.getenv("ZONE");  // Read zone from environment
        }
        
        @Override
        protected List<ServiceInstance> filterInstances(List<ServiceInstance> instances) {
            if (zone == null) {
                return instances;
            }
            
            // Filter instances in same zone
            List<ServiceInstance> sameZone = instances.stream()
                .filter(instance -> zone.equals(instance.getMetadata().get("zone")))
                .collect(Collectors.toList());
            
            // If no instances in same zone, use all
            return sameZone.isEmpty() ? instances : sameZone;
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Weighted load balancer (based on instance capacity)
     */
    @Bean
    public ReactorLoadBalancer<ServiceInstance> weightedLoadBalancer(
            LoadBalancerClientFactory loadBalancerClientFactory) {
        
        return new WeightedResponseTimeLoadBalancer();
    }
}
4.2 Eureka Events and Listeners
java
// EurekaEventListener.java
package com.example.order.listener;

import com.netflix.appinfo.InstanceInfo;
import com.netflix.discovery.shared.Applications;
import com.netflix.discovery.shared.event.ApplicationsChangedEvent;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.cloud.netflix.eureka.event.EurekaInstanceCanceledEvent;
import org.springframework.cloud.netflix.eureka.event.EurekaInstanceRegisteredEvent;
import org.springframework.cloud.netflix.eureka.event.EurekaInstanceRenewedEvent;
import org.springframework.context.event.EventListener;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;

/**
 * Listen to Eureka events for monitoring and debugging
 */
@Component
@Slf4j
public class EurekaEventListener {
    
    /**
     * Called when a service instance registers with Eureka
     */
    @EventListener
    public void onInstanceRegistered(EurekaInstanceRegisteredEvent event) {
        InstanceInfo instanceInfo = event.getInstanceInfo();
        log.info("Service registered: {} - {}:{}", 
                 instanceInfo.getAppName(),
                 instanceInfo.getIPAddr(),
                 instanceInfo.getPort());
        
        // You can trigger actions here (e.g., warm up caches)
        warmUpService(instanceInfo.getAppName());
    }
    
    /**
     * Called when a service instance renews its lease (heartbeat)
     */
    @EventListener
    public void onInstanceRenewed(EurekaInstanceRenewedEvent event) {
        log.debug("Heartbeat received from: {} - {}", 
                  event.getAppName(), event.getInstanceId());
    }
    
    /**
     * Called when a service instance is cancelled (graceful shutdown)
     */
    @EventListener
    public void onInstanceCanceled(EurekaInstanceCanceledEvent event) {
        log.info("Service deregistered: {} - {}", 
                 event.getAppName(), event.getServerId());
        
        // Clear caches for this service
        clearCachesForService(event.getAppName());
    }
    
    /**
     * Called when the registry changes
     */
    @EventListener
    public void onApplicationsChanged(ApplicationsChangedEvent event) {
        Applications applications = event.getApplications();
        log.info("Registry changed. Current applications: {}", 
                 applications.getRegisteredApplications().size());
        
        // Log all registered services
        applications.getRegisteredApplications().forEach(app -> {
            log.debug("  - {} ({} instances)", 
                      app.getName(), app.getInstances().size());
        });
    }
    
    private void warmUpService(String serviceName) {
        // Implementation: Preload cache, establish connections, etc.
        log.debug("Warming up connections to: {}", serviceName);
    }
    
    private void clearCachesForService(String serviceName) {
        log.debug("Clearing caches for: {}", serviceName);
    }
}
4.3 Health Check Configuration
java
// CustomHealthIndicator.java
package com.example.order.health;

import org.springframework.boot.actuate.health.Health;
import org.springframework.boot.actuate.health.HealthIndicator;
import org.springframework.cloud.client.discovery.DiscoveryClient;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;

/**
 * Custom health indicator that checks Eureka connectivity
 */
@Component
public class EurekaHealthIndicator implements HealthIndicator {
    
    private final DiscoveryClient discoveryClient;
    
    public EurekaHealthIndicator(DiscoveryClient discoveryClient) {
        this.discoveryClient = discoveryClient;
    }
    
    @Override
    public Health health() {
        try {
            // Check if we can get services from Eureka
            List<String> services = discoveryClient.getServices();
            
            if (services.isEmpty()) {
                return Health.down()
                    .withDetail("error", "No services registered with Eureka")
                    .build();
            }
            
            return Health.up()
                .withDetail("services", services.size())
                .withDetail("registeredServices", services)
                .build();
                
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return Health.down()
                .withDetail("error", e.getMessage())
                .build();
        }
    }
}

5.3 Kubernetes Deployment with Eureka
yaml
# k8s/eureka-server.yaml
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: eureka-server
  namespace: microservices
spec:
  replicas: 2  # Multiple replicas for HA
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app: eureka-server
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app: eureka-server
    spec:
      containers:
      - name: eureka-server
        image: eureka-server:latest
        ports:
        - containerPort: 8761
        env:
        - name: EUREKA_PASSWORD
          valueFrom:
            secretKeyRef:
              name: eureka-secrets
              key: password
        - name: EUREKA_URL
          value: "http://eureka-server-0.eureka-server:8761/eureka/,http://eureka-server-1.eureka-server:8761/eureka/"
        - name: HOSTNAME
          valueFrom:
            fieldRef:
              fieldPath: metadata.name
        livenessProbe:
          httpGet:
            path: /actuator/health
            port: 8761
          initialDelaySeconds: 60
          periodSeconds: 10
        readinessProbe:
          httpGet:
            path: /actuator/health
            port: 8761
          initialDelaySeconds: 30
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: eureka-server
  namespace: microservices
spec:
  clusterIP: None  # Headless service for stateful set
  selector:
    app: eureka-server
  ports:
  - port: 8761
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: Secret
metadata:
  name: eureka-secrets
  namespace: microservices
type: Opaque
stringData:
  password: "your-secure-password"

curl http://localhost:8761/eureka/apps

# Get specific application
curl http://localhost:8761/eureka/apps/ORDER-SERVICE

# Get application instance details
curl http://localhost:8761/eureka/apps/ORDER-SERVICE/localhost:order-service:8080

# Get delta changes (only changed instances)
curl http://localhost:8761/eureka/apps/delta

# Force registry update
curl -X PUT http://localhost:8761/eureka/apps/ORDER-SERVICE/localhost:order-service:8080/status?value=UP

# Take instance out of service
curl -X PUT http://localhost:8761/eureka/apps/ORDER-SERVICE/localhost:order-service:8080/status?value=OUT_OF_SERVICE
6.2 Discovery Client Utility
java
// DiscoveryUtil.java
package com.example.order.util;

import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.cloud.client.ServiceInstance;
import org.springframework.cloud.client.discovery.DiscoveryClient;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

import java.util.HashMap;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Map;
import java.util.stream.Collectors;

/**
 * Utility endpoints for debugging service discovery
 */
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/discovery")
@Component
public class DiscoveryUtil {
    
    @Autowired
    private DiscoveryClient discoveryClient;
    
    /**
     * List all registered services
     */
    @GetMapping("/services")
    public Map<String, Object> getServices() {
        List<String> services = discoveryClient.getServices();
        
        Map<String, Object> response = new HashMap<>();
        response.put("total", services.size());
        response.put("services", services);
        response.put("timestamp", System.currentTimeMillis());
        
        return response;
    }
    
    /**
     * Get instances of a specific service
     */
    @GetMapping("/instances/{serviceName}")
    public Map<String, Object> getInstances(@PathVariable String serviceName) {
        List<ServiceInstance> instances = discoveryClient.getInstances(serviceName);
        
        List<Map<String, Object>> instanceDetails = instances.stream()
            .map(instance -> {
                Map<String, Object> details = new HashMap<>();
                details.put("serviceId", instance.getServiceId());
                details.put("host", instance.getHost());
                details.put("port", instance.getPort());
                details.put("uri", instance.getUri());
                details.put("secure", instance.isSecure());
                details.put("metadata", instance.getMetadata());
                details.put("scheme", instance.getScheme());
                return details;
            })
            .collect(Collectors.toList());
        
        Map<String, Object> response = new HashMap<>();
        response.put("serviceName", serviceName);
        response.put("instanceCount", instanceDetails.size());
        response.put("instances", instanceDetails);
        response.put("timestamp", System.currentTimeMillis());
        
        return response;
    }
    
    /**
     * Get local service information
     */
    @GetMapping("/local")
    public Map<String, Object> getLocalInfo() {
        Map<String, Object> info = new HashMap<>();
        
        // Get local service instances (this service)
        List<ServiceInstance> instances = discoveryClient.getInstances("order-service");
        
        info.put("localService", "order-service");
        info.put("knownInstances", instances.size());
        info.put("allServices", discoveryClient.getServices());
        
        return info;
    }
}

Common Eureka Issues and Fixes:
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

├──────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Services not registering         │ Check eureka.client.service-url        │
│                                  │ Verify credentials                     │
│                                  │ Check network connectivity             │
├──────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Services showing as DOWN         │ Increase lease-renewal-interval        │
│                                  │ Check health check endpoint            │
│                                  │ Verify application.yml configuration   │
├──────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ UnknownHostException             │ Use service names not IPs              │
│                                  │ Verify @LoadBalanced on RestTemplate   │
│                                  │ Check Feign client configuration       │
├──────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Registry not updating            │ Check enable-self-preservation         │
│                                  │ Reduce eviction-interval-timer-in-ms   │
│                                  │ Verify heartbeat intervals             │
├──────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Instance ID conflicts            │ Use unique instance-id with random     │
│                                  │ eureka.instance.instance-id: ${random} │
└──────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

│ appropriately                │                                            │

This comprehensive guide provides a complete, production-ready Eureka implementation for service discovery in Spring Boot microservices!

How to Monitor Microservices?
Complete Guide to Monitoring Microservices
Monitoring microservices is fundamentally different from monitoring monoliths. Instead of monitoring a single application, you now have dozens or hundreds of distributed services, each with multiple instances, running across multiple environments. This guide covers everything from metrics collection to actionable alerting.

The Monitoring Challenge
text

1.2 Configuration

management:

        enabled: true
        step: 30s  # Scrape interval
        descriptions: true
    
    # Common tags for all metrics
    tags:
      application: ${spring.application.name}
      environment: ${ENVIRONMENT:development}
      version: ${SERVICE_VERSION:1.0.0}
      instance: ${HOSTNAME:unknown}

      exposure:
        include: health,info,metrics,prometheus
      base-path: /actuator

    metrics:
      enabled: true
    prometheus:
      enabled: true

    diskspace:
      enabled: true
    livenessstate:
      enabled: true
    readinessstate:
      enabled: true

1.3 Custom Metrics Implementation
java
// MetricsService.java
package com.example.order.monitoring;

import io.micrometer.core.instrument.*;
import io.micrometer.core.instrument.Timer;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;

import javax.annotation.PostConstruct;
import java.time.Duration;
import java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap;
import java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicInteger;

/**
 * Centralized metrics service for Order Service
 * 
 * Types of metrics:
 * - Counter: Only increase (request count, error count)
 * - Gauge: Goes up and down (active connections, queue size)
 * - Timer: Measures duration and counts events
 * - DistributionSummary: Tracks distribution of values (request size)
 */
@Service
@Slf4j
public class MetricsService {
    
    @Autowired
    private MeterRegistry meterRegistry;
    
    // ===== COUNTERS (Only increase) =====
    
    /** Total number of orders created */
    private Counter ordersCreatedCounter;
    
    /** Total number of failed orders */
    private Counter ordersFailedCounter;
    
    /** Total number of payment retries */
    private Counter paymentRetryCounter;
    
    // ===== GAUGES (Up and down values) =====
    
    /** Current number of active orders being processed */
    private AtomicInteger activeOrdersGauge;
    
    /** Current queue size for order processing */
    private AtomicInteger queueSizeGauge;
    
    // ===== TIMERS (Duration measurements) =====
    
    /** Timer for order creation latency */
    private Timer orderCreationTimer;
    
    /** Timer for database query latency */
    private Timer databaseQueryTimer;
    
    /** Timer for external API calls */
    private Timer externalApiTimer;
    
    // ===== DISTRIBUTION SUMMARIES =====
    
    /** Distribution of order sizes (number of items) */
    private DistributionSummary orderSizeSummary;
    
    /** Distribution of order amounts */
    private DistributionSummary orderAmountSummary;
    
    @PostConstruct
    public void init() {
        // Initialize Counters
        ordersCreatedCounter = Counter.builder("orders.created")
            .description("Total number of orders created")
            .tag("service", "order-service")
            .register(meterRegistry);
        
        ordersFailedCounter = Counter.builder("orders.failed")
            .description("Total number of failed orders")
            .tag("service", "order-service")
            .register(meterRegistry);
        
        paymentRetryCounter = Counter.builder("payment.retries")
            .description("Number of payment retry attempts")
            .tag("service", "order-service")
            .register(meterRegistry);
        
        // Initialize Gauges
        activeOrdersGauge = meterRegistry.gauge("orders.active", 
            new AtomicInteger(0));
        
        queueSizeGauge = meterRegistry.gauge("orders.queue.size", 
            new AtomicInteger(0));
        
        // Initialize Timers
        orderCreationTimer = Timer.builder("orders.creation.duration")
            .description("Time taken to create an order")
            .publishPercentiles(0.5, 0.95, 0.99)
            .publishPercentileHistogram()
            .sla(Duration.ofMillis(100), Duration.ofMillis(500), 
                 Duration.ofSeconds(1), Duration.ofSeconds(2))
            .register(meterRegistry);
        
        databaseQueryTimer = Timer.builder("database.query.duration")
            .description("Database query execution time")
            .tag("type", "order")
            .register(meterRegistry);
        
        externalApiTimer = Timer.builder("external.api.duration")
            .description("External API call duration")
            .register(meterRegistry);
        
        // Initialize Distribution Summaries
        orderSizeSummary = DistributionSummary.builder("orders.size")
            .description("Number of items per order")
            .baseUnit("items")
            .publishPercentiles(0.5, 0.75, 0.95, 0.99)
            .register(meterRegistry);
        
        orderAmountSummary = DistributionSummary.builder("orders.amount")
            .description("Order amount in USD")
            .baseUnit("usd")
            .publishPercentiles(0.5, 0.75, 0.95, 0.99)
            .scale(1.0)
            .register(meterRegistry);
        
        log.info("MetricsService initialized with all metric types");
    }
    
    // ===== Business Metrics Recording Methods =====
    
    /**
     * Record successful order creation
     */
    public void recordOrderCreated(int itemCount, double amount) {
        ordersCreatedCounter.increment();
        orderSizeSummary.record(itemCount);
        orderAmountSummary.record(amount);
        activeOrdersGauge.incrementAndGet();
        
        log.debug("Recorded order creation - items: {}, amount: ${}", itemCount, amount);
    }
    
    /**
     * Record failed order
     */
    public void recordOrderFailed(String reason) {
        ordersFailedCounter.increment();
        activeOrdersGauge.decrementAndGet();
        
        // Add reason as a tag for better analysis
        Counter.builder("orders.failed.by.reason")
            .tag("reason", reason)
            .register(meterRegistry)
            .increment();
    }
    
    /**
     * Record order creation duration using Timer.Sample
     */
    public Timer.Sample startOrderCreationTimer() {
        return Timer.start(meterRegistry);
    }
    
    public void stopOrderCreationTimer(Timer.Sample sample, String status) {
        sample.stop(orderCreationTimer);
        
        // Record status-specific metrics
        Timer.builder("orders.creation.duration.by.status")
            .tag("status", status)
            .register(meterRegistry)
            .record(sample.stop(() -> 0L), java.util.concurrent.TimeUnit.NANOSECONDS);
    }
    
    /**
     * Record database query with timing
     */
    public <T> T recordDatabaseQuery(String query, java.util.function.Supplier<T> operation) {
        return databaseQueryTimer.record(operation);
    }
    
    /**
     * Update active orders count
     */
    public void incrementActiveOrders() {
        activeOrdersGauge.incrementAndGet();
    }
    
    public void decrementActiveOrders() {
        activeOrdersGauge.decrementAndGet();
    }
    
    /**
     * Update queue size
     */
    public void updateQueueSize(int size) {
        queueSizeGauge.set(size);
    }
    
    /**
     * Record payment retry
     */
    public void recordPaymentRetry(int attemptNumber) {
        paymentRetryCounter.increment();
        
        // Record by attempt number
        Counter.builder("payment.retries.by.attempt")
            .tag("attempt", String.valueOf(attemptNumber))
            .register(meterRegistry)
            .increment();
    }
}
1.4 Controller with Metrics
java
// OrderController.java
package com.example.order.controller;

import com.example.order.monitoring.MetricsService;
import io.micrometer.core.instrument.Timer;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/orders")
@Slf4j
public class OrderController {
    
    @Autowired
    private MetricsService metricsService;
    
    @Autowired
    private OrderService orderService;
    
    @PostMapping
    public OrderResponse createOrder(@RequestBody CreateOrderRequest request) {
        // Start timer for this operation
        Timer.Sample timerSample = metricsService.startOrderCreationTimer();
        
        try {
            // Update active orders count
            metricsService.incrementActiveOrders();
            
            // Record queue size (if you have a queue)
            metricsService.updateQueueSize(orderService.getQueueSize());
            
            // Execute with database timing
            Order order = metricsService.recordDatabaseQuery(
                "INSERT INTO orders", 
                () -> orderService.createOrder(request)
            );
            
            // Record success metrics
            metricsService.recordOrderCreated(
                request.getItems().size(), 
                order.getTotalAmount()
            );
            
            // Stop timer with success status
            metricsService.stopOrderCreationTimer(timerSample, "success");
            
            return OrderResponse.success(order);
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            // Record failure
            metricsService.recordOrderFailed(e.getClass().getSimpleName());
            metricsService.stopOrderCreationTimer(timerSample, "failure");
            
            log.error("Order creation failed", e);
            throw e;
        } finally {
            metricsService.decrementActiveOrders();
        }
    }
    
    @GetMapping("/metrics/test")
    public String testMetrics() {
        // Test all metric types
        metricsService.recordOrderCreated(5, 99.99);
        metricsService.recordPaymentRetry(1);
        metricsService.recordPaymentRetry(2);
        metricsService.updateQueueSize(10);
        
        return "Metrics recorded. Check /actuator/prometheus";
    }
}

2.2 Tracing Configuration
yaml
# application.yml
management:
  tracing:
    enabled: true
    sampling:
      probability: 1.0  # Sample 100% of traces (lower in production: 0.1)
    
    propagation:
      type: b3  # B3 propagation (standard for Zipkin)
    
    # Span tags to include
    span:
      naming:
        conventional: true
    
    # Export to Zipkin
    zipkin:
      tracing:
        endpoint: ${ZIPKIN_URL:http://localhost:9411/api/v2/spans}
        connect-timeout: 5s
        read-timeout: 5s
    
    # Additional tags
    baggage:
      remote-fields:
        - userId
        - correlationId
        - tenantId
      tag-fields:
        - userId
        - correlationId
        - tenantId

# Enable trace ID in logs
logging:
  pattern:
    console: "%d{ISO8601} [%thread] %-5level [%X{traceId:-},%X{spanId:-}] %logger{36} - %msg%n"
2.3 Custom Trace Annotations
java
// TracingService.java
package com.example.order.monitoring;

import brave.Span;
import brave.Tracer;
import brave.propagation.TraceContext;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;

import java.util.HashMap;
import java.util.Map;

/**
 * Custom tracing service for creating custom spans
 */
@Service
@Slf4j
public class TracingService {
    
    @Autowired
    private Tracer tracer;
    
    /**
     * Create a custom span for any operation
     * 
     * Usage:
     * try (TracingService.SpanWrapper span = tracingService.startSpan("operation-name")) {
     *     span.tag("key", "value");
     *     // do work
     * }
     */
    public SpanWrapper startSpan(String name) {
        Span span = tracer.nextSpan().name(name).start();
        return new SpanWrapper(span);
    }
    
    /**
     * Create a child span from current context
     */
    public SpanWrapper startChildSpan(String name, Map<String, String> tags) {
        Span span = tracer.nextSpan().name(name).start();
        
        if (tags != null) {
            tags.forEach(span::tag);
        }
        
        return new SpanWrapper(span);
    }
    
    /**
     * Get current trace ID (for logging correlation)
     */
    public String getCurrentTraceId() {
        TraceContext context = tracer.currentSpan() != null ? 
            tracer.currentSpan().context() : null;
        return context != null ? context.traceIdString() : "no-trace";
    }
    
    /**
     * Get current span ID
     */
    public String getCurrentSpanId() {
        TraceContext context = tracer.currentSpan() != null ? 
            tracer.currentSpan().context() : null;
        return context != null ? context.spanIdString() : "no-span";
    }
    
    /**
     * Wrapper for AutoCloseable spans
     */
    public static class SpanWrapper implements AutoCloseable {
        private final Span span;
        
        public SpanWrapper(Span span) {
            this.span = span.start();
            try (Tracer.SpanInScope ws = span.tracer().withSpanInScope(span)) {
                // Span is now current
            }
        }
        
        public SpanWrapper tag(String key, String value) {
            span.tag(key, value);
            return this;
        }
        
        public SpanWrapper annotate(String value) {
            span.annotate(value);
            return this;
        }
        
        public SpanWrapper error(Throwable throwable) {
            span.error(throwable);
            return this;
        }
        
        @Override
        public void close() {
            span.finish();
        }
    }
}
2.4 Service with Tracing
java
// OrderServiceWithTracing.java
package com.example.order.service;

import brave.Span;
import brave.Tracer;
import com.example.order.monitoring.TracingService;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;
import org.springframework.web.client.RestTemplate;

import java.util.HashMap;
import java.util.Map;

@Service
@Slf4j
public class OrderServiceWithTracing {
    
    @Autowired
    private Tracer tracer;
    
    @Autowired
    private TracingService tracingService;
    
    @Autowired
    private RestTemplate restTemplate;
    
    /**
     * Order processing with custom traces
     */
    public Order processOrder(OrderRequest request) {
        // Current span is automatically propagated by Spring Cloud Sleuth
        
        log.info("Processing order for customer: {}", request.getCustomerId());
        
        // Create a custom span for validation
        try (TracingService.SpanWrapper validationSpan = 
                tracingService.startChildSpan("validate-order", 
                    Map.of("customerId", request.getCustomerId()))) {
            
            validateOrder(request);
            validationSpan.tag("validation.result", "success");
        }
        
        // Create a custom span for inventory check
        Map<String, String> inventoryTags = new HashMap<>();
        inventoryTags.put("productId", request.getProductId());
        inventoryTags.put("quantity", String.valueOf(request.getQuantity()));
        
        try (TracingService.SpanWrapper inventorySpan = 
                tracingService.startChildSpan("check-inventory", inventoryTags)) {
            
            InventoryResponse inventory = checkInventory(request.getProductId());
            
            if (inventory.getQuantity() < request.getQuantity()) {
                inventorySpan.tag("result", "insufficient");
                throw new InsufficientInventoryException("Not enough stock");
            }
            
            inventorySpan.tag("result", "sufficient");
            inventorySpan.tag("availableQuantity", String.valueOf(inventory.getQuantity()));
        }
        
        // Create a span for payment processing with timing
        Span paymentSpan = tracer.nextSpan().name("process-payment").start();
        
        try (Tracer.SpanInScope ws = tracer.withSpanInScope(paymentSpan)) {
            paymentSpan.tag("amount", String.valueOf(request.getTotalAmount()));
            paymentSpan.tag("currency", "USD");
            
            PaymentResponse payment = processPayment(request);
            
            paymentSpan.tag("paymentId", payment.getId());
            paymentSpan.tag("status", payment.getStatus());
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            paymentSpan.error(e);
            throw e;
        } finally {
            paymentSpan.finish();
        }
        
        // Create order span
        try (TracingService.SpanWrapper orderSpan = 
                tracingService.startSpan("create-order-record")) {
            
            Order order = saveOrder(request);
            orderSpan.tag("orderId", order.getId().toString());
            orderSpan.tag("totalAmount", String.valueOf(order.getTotalAmount()));
            
            return order;
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Demonstrates trace propagation across HTTP calls
     * The trace ID is automatically propagated via headers
     */
    private InventoryResponse checkInventory(String productId) {
        // RestTemplate with tracing automatically propagates trace context
        String url = "http://inventory-service/api/inventory/" + productId;
        
        // The trace ID will be passed to inventory service
        return restTemplate.getForObject(url, InventoryResponse.class);
    }
}
Step 3: Centralized Logging with ELK Stack
3.1 Logback Configuration (JSON format for Logstash)
xml
<!-- logback-spring.xml -->
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<configuration>
    <!-- Include Spring Boot defaults -->
    <include resource="org/springframework/boot/logging/logback/base.xml"/>
    
    <!-- Property for service name -->
    <springProperty scope="context" name="serviceName" source="spring.application.name"/>
    <springProperty scope="context" name="environment" source="ENVIRONMENT" defaultValue="development"/>
    <springProperty scope="context" name="version" source="SERVICE_VERSION" defaultValue="1.0.0"/>
    
    <!-- JSON Appender for Logstash -->
    <appender name="LOGSTASH" class="net.logstash.logback.appender.LogstashTcpSocketAppender">
        <destination>${LOGSTASH_HOST:-localhost}:${LOGSTASH_PORT:-5000}</destination>
        <reconnectionDelay>5000</reconnectionDelay>
        <keepAliveDuration>30000</keepAliveDuration>
        
        <encoder class="net.logstash.logback.encoder.LogstashEncoder">
            <!-- Include MDC fields (traceId, spanId, etc.) -->
            <includeMdcKeyName>traceId</includeMdcKeyName>
            <includeMdcKeyName>spanId</includeMdcKeyName>
            <includeMdcKeyName>parentId</includeMdcKeyName>
            <includeMdcKeyName>userId</includeMdcKeyName>
            <includeMdcKeyName>correlationId</includeMdcKeyName>
            
            <!-- Custom fields -->
            <customFields>{
                "serviceName": "${serviceName}",
                "environment": "${environment}",
                "serviceVersion": "${version}",
                "hostname": "${HOSTNAME:-unknown}"
            }</customFields>
            
            <!-- Timezone -->
            <timeZone>UTC</timeZone>
        </encoder>
    </appender>
    
    <!-- Console appender with trace ID for development -->
    <appender name="CONSOLE" class="ch.qos.logback.core.ConsoleAppender">
        <encoder>
            <pattern>%d{ISO8601} [%thread] %-5level [%X{traceId:-},%X{spanId:-}] %logger{36} - %msg%n</pattern>
        </encoder>
    </appender>
    
    <!-- Async appender for performance -->
    <appender name="ASYNC_LOGSTASH" class="ch.qos.logback.classic.AsyncAppender">
        <appender-ref ref="LOGSTASH"/>
        <queueSize>10000</queueSize>
        <discardingThreshold>0</discardingThreshold>
        <includeCallerData>false</includeCallerData>
    </appender>
    
    <!-- Root logger -->
    <root level="INFO">
        <appender-ref ref="CONSOLE"/>
        <appender-ref ref="ASYNC_LOGSTASH"/>
    </root>
    
    <!-- Service-specific loggers -->
    <logger name="com.example" level="DEBUG" additivity="false">
        <appender-ref ref="CONSOLE"/>
        <appender-ref ref="ASYNC_LOGSTASH"/>
    </logger>
    
    <!-- Reduce noise from frameworks -->
    <logger name="org.springframework" level="INFO"/>
    <logger name="org.apache.kafka" level="WARN"/>
</configuration>
3.2 Structured Logging Implementation
java
// StructuredLogger.java
package com.example.order.monitoring;

import com.fasterxml.jackson.core.JsonProcessingException;
import com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.ObjectMapper;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.slf4j.MDC;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;

import java.time.Instant;
import java.util.HashMap;
import java.util.Map;
import java.util.UUID;

/**
 * Structured logging for better searchability in ELK
 * 
 * Instead of:
 * log.info("Order created: " + orderId + " for customer: " + customerId);
 * 
 * Use:
 * structuredLogger.logOrderCreated(orderId, customerId, amount);
 */
@Component
@Slf4j
public class StructuredLogger {
    
    @Autowired
    private ObjectMapper objectMapper;
    
    /**
     * Log order creation with structured data
     */
    public void logOrderCreated(UUID orderId, UUID customerId, double amount, int itemCount) {
        Map<String, Object> logData = new HashMap<>();
        logData.put("event", "order_created");
        logData.put("orderId", orderId);
        logData.put("customerId", customerId);
        logData.put("amount", amount);
        logData.put("itemCount", itemCount);
        logData.put("currency", "USD");
        logData.put("timestamp", Instant.now().toString());
        logData.put("traceId", MDC.get("traceId"));
        
        log.info(toJson(logData));
    }
    
    /**
     * Log order payment with structured data
     */
    public void logPaymentProcessed(UUID orderId, String paymentId, String status, double amount) {
        Map<String, Object> logData = new HashMap<>();
        logData.put("event", "payment_processed");
        logData.put("orderId", orderId);
        logData.put("paymentId", paymentId);
        logData.put("status", status);
        logData.put("amount", amount);
        logData.put("timestamp", Instant.now().toString());
        
        if ("FAILED".equals(status)) {
            log.error(toJson(logData));
        } else {
            log.info(toJson(logData));
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Log external API call
     */
    public void logExternalCall(String service, String endpoint, long durationMs, int statusCode) {
        Map<String, Object> logData = new HashMap<>();
        logData.put("event", "external_call");
        logData.put("service", service);
        logData.put("endpoint", endpoint);
        logData.put("durationMs", durationMs);
        logData.put("statusCode", statusCode);
        logData.put("timestamp", Instant.now().toString());
        
        if (statusCode >= 400) {
            log.warn(toJson(logData));
        } else {
            log.debug(toJson(logData));
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Log business event
     */
    public void logBusinessEvent(String eventType, Map<String, Object> data) {
        Map<String, Object> logData = new HashMap<>();
        logData.put("event", eventType);
        logData.put("data", data);
        logData.put("timestamp", Instant.now().toString());
        logData.put("traceId", MDC.get("traceId"));
        
        log.info(toJson(logData));
    }
    
    /**
     * Log error with context
     */
    public void logError(String errorType, String message, Map<String, Object> context, Throwable throwable) {
        Map<String, Object> logData = new HashMap<>();
        logData.put("event", "error");
        logData.put("errorType", errorType);
        logData.put("message", message);
        logData.put("context", context);
        logData.put("timestamp", Instant.now().toString());
        logData.put("traceId", MDC.get("traceId"));
        
        if (throwable != null) {
            log.error(toJson(logData), throwable);
        } else {
            log.error(toJson(logData));
        }
    }
    
    private String toJson(Map<String, Object> data) {
        try {
            return objectMapper.writeValueAsString(data);
        } catch (JsonProcessingException e) {
            log.error("Failed to serialize log data", e);
            return data.toString();
        }
    }
}

        regex: true

        action: replace
        target_label: __metrics_path__
        regex: (.+)
      - source_labels: [__address__, __meta_kubernetes_pod_annotation_prometheus_io_port]
        action: replace
        regex: (.+):(?:\d+);(\d+)
        replacement: $1:$2
        target_label: __address__

        regex: __meta_kubernetes_pod_label_(.+)
      - source_labels: [__meta_kubernetes_namespace]
        action: replace
        target_label: kubernetes_namespace
      - source_labels: [__meta_kubernetes_pod_name]
        action: replace

    static_configs:
