4.2 Prometheus Alerts
yaml
# prometheus/alerts/service-alerts.yml
groups:
  - name: microservice_alerts
    interval: 30s
    rules:
      # High error rate alert
      - alert: HighErrorRate
        expr: |
          sum(rate(http_server_requests_seconds_count{status=~"5.."}[5m])) 
          / 
          sum(rate(http_server_requests_seconds_count[5m])) > 0.05
        for: 2m
        labels:
          severity: critical
          team: microservices
        annotations:
          summary: "High error rate in {{ $labels.application }}"
          description: "Error rate is {{ $value | humanizePercentage }} for {{ $labels.application }} in the last 5 minutes"
      
      # Service down alert
      - alert: ServiceDown
        expr: up{job="spring-boot-microservices"} == 0
        for: 1m
        labels:
          severity: critical
        annotations:
          summary: "Service {{ $labels.application }} is down"
          description: "{{ $labels.instance }} has been down for more than 1 minute"
      
      # High latency alert (P95 > 2 seconds)
      - alert: HighLatency
        expr: |
          histogram_quantile(0.95, 
            sum(rate(http_server_requests_seconds_bucket[5m])) by (le, application)
          ) > 2
        for: 5m
        labels:
          severity: warning
        annotations:
          summary: "High latency in {{ $labels.application }}"
          description: "95th percentile latency is {{ $value }}s for {{ $labels.application }}"
      
      # High memory usage alert
      - alert: HighMemoryUsage
        expr: |
          (jvm_memory_used_bytes{area="heap"} / jvm_memory_max_bytes{area="heap"}) > 0.85
        for: 5m
        labels:
          severity: warning
        annotations:
          summary: "High memory usage in {{ $labels.application }}"
          description: "Memory usage is {{ $value | humanizePercentage }} for {{ $labels.application }}"
      
      # High CPU usage alert
      - alert: HighCPUUsage
        expr: |
          process_cpu_usage{application!=""} > 0.8
        for: 5m
        labels:
          severity: warning
        annotations:
          summary: "High CPU usage in {{ $labels.application }}"
          description: "CPU usage is {{ $value | humanizePercentage }} for {{ $labels.application }}"
      
      # Database connection pool exhaustion
      - alert: DatabaseConnectionPoolExhaustion
        expr: |
          (hikaricp_connections_active / hikaricp_connections_max) > 0.9
        for: 2m
        labels:
          severity: critical
        annotations:
          summary: "Database connection pool near exhaustion"
          description: "Active connections: {{ $value | humanizePercentage }} of max in {{ $labels.application }}"
      
      # Kafka consumer lag
      - alert: KafkaConsumerLag
        expr: |
          kafka_consumer_lag > 1000
        for: 5m
        labels:
          severity: warning
        annotations:
          summary: "High Kafka consumer lag"
          description: "Consumer lag is {{ $value }} for consumer group {{ $labels.consumergroup }}"

Step 5: Health Check Endpoints
5.1 Custom Health Indicators
java
// CustomHealthIndicators.java
package com.example.order.monitoring;

import org.springframework.boot.actuate.health.Health;
import org.springframework.boot.actuate.health.HealthIndicator;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;

import java.net.Socket;
import java.sql.Connection;
import java.sql.DriverManager;

/**
 * Custom health indicators for detailed health checks
 */
@Component
public class CustomHealthIndicators {
    
    /**
     * Database health indicator
     */
    @Component
    public static class DatabaseHealthIndicator implements HealthIndicator {
        
        @Override
        public Health health() {
            try (Connection conn = DriverManager.getConnection(
                System.getenv("DB_URL"),
                System.getenv("DB_USER"),
                System.getenv("DB_PASSWORD"))) {
                
                boolean isValid = conn.isValid(5); // 5 second timeout
                
                if (isValid) {
                    return Health.up()
                        .withDetail("database", "connected")
                        .withDetail("url", System.getenv("DB_URL"))
                        .build();
                } else {
                    return Health.down()
                        .withDetail("database", "invalid connection")
                        .build();
                }
                
            } catch (Exception e) {
                return Health.down(e)
                    .withDetail("database", "connection failed")
                    .build();
            }
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Kafka health indicator
     */
    @Component
    public static class KafkaHealthIndicator implements HealthIndicator {
        
        @Override
        public Health health() {
            String kafkaBootstrap = System.getenv("KAFKA_BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS");
            
            try {
                // Attempt to connect to Kafka
                String[] servers = kafkaBootstrap.split(",");
                for (String server : servers) {
                    String[] parts = server.split(":");
                    try (Socket socket = new Socket(parts[0], Integer.parseInt(parts[1]))) {
                        if (socket.isConnected()) {
                            return Health.up()
                                .withDetail("kafka", "connected")
                                .withDetail("bootstrap", kafkaBootstrap)
                                .build();
                        }
                    }
                }
                
                return Health.down()
                    .withDetail("kafka", "unable to connect")
                    .build();
                
            } catch (Exception e) {
                return Health.down(e)
                    .withDetail("kafka", "connection failed")
                    .build();
            }
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Disk space health indicator
     */
    @Component
    public static class DiskSpaceHealthIndicator implements HealthIndicator {
        
        @Override
        public Health health() {
            java.io.File file = new java.io.File("/");
            long freeBytes = file.getFreeSpace();
            long totalBytes = file.getTotalSpace();
            long usedBytes = totalBytes - freeBytes;
            double usedPercentage = (double) usedBytes / totalBytes * 100;
            
            if (usedPercentage < 90) {
                return Health.up()
                    .withDetail("free", freeBytes / 1024 / 1024 / 1024 + " GB")
                    .withDetail("used_percentage", String.format("%.1f", usedPercentage) + "%")
                    .build();
            } else {
                return Health.down()
                    .withDetail("error", "Disk space critically low")
                    .withDetail("free", freeBytes / 1024 / 1024 / 1024 + " GB")
                    .withDetail("used_percentage", String.format("%.1f", usedPercentage) + "%")
                    .build();
            }
        }
    }
}
5.2 Liveness and Readiness Probes
java
// KubernetesProbesConfiguration.java
package com.example.order.monitoring;

import org.springframework.boot.actuate.health.HealthEndpoint;
import org.springframework.boot.actuate.health.Status;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

/**
 * Kubernetes liveness and readiness probes
 * 
 * - liveness: Is the application running? If not, restart the pod
 * - readiness: Is the application ready to receive traffic? If not, remove from service
 */
@RestController
public class KubernetesProbesConfiguration {
    
    private final HealthEndpoint healthEndpoint;
    
    public KubernetesProbesConfiguration(HealthEndpoint healthEndpoint) {
        this.healthEndpoint = healthEndpoint;
    }
    
    /**
     * Liveness probe - checks if the application is alive
     * 
     * Kubernetes will restart the pod if this fails
     */
    @GetMapping("/actuator/health/liveness")
    public HealthResponse liveness() {
        HealthResponse response = new HealthResponse();
        response.setStatus("UP");
        response.setMessage("Application is running");
        return response;
    }
    
    /**
     * Readiness probe - checks if the application can accept traffic
     * 
     * Kubernetes will stop sending traffic to the pod if this fails
     */
    @GetMapping("/actuator/health/readiness")
    public HealthResponse readiness() {
        var health = healthEndpoint.health();
        
        HealthResponse response = new HealthResponse();
        response.setStatus(health.getStatus().getCode());
        
        if (health.getStatus().getCode().equals(Status.UP.getCode())) {
            response.setMessage("Ready to accept traffic");
        } else {
            response.setMessage("Not ready - health check failed");
            response.setDetails(health.getDetails());
        }
        
        return response;
    }
    
    @lombok.Data
    public static class HealthResponse {
        private String status;
        private String message;
        private Object details;
    }
}
Step 6: Docker Compose for Monitoring Stack
yaml
# docker-compose-monitoring.yml
version: '3.8'

services:
  # ===== Metrics =====
  prometheus:
    image: prom/prometheus:latest
    container_name: prometheus
    ports:
      - "9090:9090"
    volumes:
      - ./prometheus/prometheus.yml:/etc/prometheus/prometheus.yml
      - ./prometheus/alerts:/etc/prometheus/alerts
      - prometheus-data:/prometheus
    command:
      - '--config.file=/etc/prometheus/prometheus.yml'
      - '--storage.tsdb.path=/prometheus'
      - '--web.console.libraries=/etc/prometheus/console_libraries'
      - '--web.console.templates=/etc/prometheus/consoles'
      - '--web.enable-lifecycle'
    networks:
      - monitoring-net
  
  grafana:
    image: grafana/grafana:latest
    container_name: grafana
    ports:
      - "3000:3000"
    environment:
      - GF_SECURITY_ADMIN_PASSWORD=admin
      - GF_INSTALL_PLUGINS=grafana-piechart-panel

Step 7: AlertManager Configuration
yaml
# prometheus/alertmanager.yml
global:
  resolve_timeout: 5m
  smtp_smarthost: 'smtp.gmail.com:587'
  smtp_from: 'alerts@microservices.com'
  smtp_auth_username: '${SMTP_USER}'
  smtp_auth_password: '${SMTP_PASSWORD}'

route:
  group_by: ['alertname', 'cluster', 'service']
  group_wait: 10s
  group_interval: 10s
  repeat_interval: 12h
  
  routes:
    # Critical alerts - page on-call engineer
    - match:
        severity: critical
      receiver: pagerduty
      continue: true
    
    # Warning alerts - send to Slack
    - match:
        severity: warning
      receiver: slack
      continue: true
    
    # Info alerts - send to email
    - match:
        severity: info
      receiver: email

receivers:
  - name: pagerduty
    pagerduty_configs:
      - service_key: '${PAGERDUTY_SERVICE_KEY}'
        description: '{{ .CommonAnnotations.summary }}'
        severity: 'critical'
  
  - name: slack
    slack_configs:
      - api_url: '${SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL}'
        channel: '#microservices-alerts'
        title: '{{ .GroupLabels.alertname }}'
        text: |
          *Alert:* {{ .CommonAnnotations.summary }}
          *Description:* {{ .CommonAnnotations.description }}
          *Severity:* {{ .CommonLabels.severity }}
          *Service:* {{ .CommonLabels.application }}
          *Time:* {{ .StartsAt }}
  
  - name: email
    email_configs:
      - to: 'team@microservices.com'
        from: 'alerts@microservices.com'
        smarthost: 'smtp.gmail.com:587'
        auth_username: '${SMTP_USER}'
        auth_password: '${SMTP_PASSWORD}'
        require_tls: true
Step 8: Monitoring Dashboard Examples
8.1 Key Metrics Dashboard (Grafana Query Examples)
sql
-- Request Rate by Service
sum(rate(http_server_requests_seconds_count[$interval])) by (application)

-- Error Rate by Service
sum(rate(http_server_requests_seconds_count{status=~"5.."}[$interval])) 
/ 
sum(rate(http_server_requests_seconds_count[$interval])) 
* 100

-- P99 Latency
histogram_quantile(0.99, 
  sum(rate(http_server_requests_seconds_bucket[$interval])) by (le, application)
)

-- Service Availability
avg_over_time(up{job="spring-boot-microservices"}[$interval]) * 100

-- JVM Memory Usage
sum(jvm_memory_used_bytes{area="heap"}) by (application) / 1024 / 1024

-- CPU Usage
process_cpu_usage{application!=""}

-- Active Database Connections
hikaricp_connections_active{application!=""}

-- Kafka Consumer Lag
sum(kafka_consumer_lag) by (consumergroup, topic)

-- Business Metrics (Custom)
sum(increase(orders_created_total[$interval])) by (application)

Summary: Best Practices
Practice	Why	Implementation
Use correlation IDs	Link logs, metrics, traces	Generate once, propagate via headers
Sampling in production	Reduce storage costs	1-10% trace sampling, 100% metrics
Structured logging	Better searchability	JSON format with consistent fields
RED method	Key metrics to track	Rate, Errors, Duration per service
USE method	Resource metrics	Utilization, Saturation, Errors
Alert on symptoms, not causes	Reduce noise	Alert on high error rate, not low disk
Define SLOs	Measure reliability	99.9% availability, <500ms latency
Automate runbooks	Faster recovery	Link alerts to remediation steps
Test monitoring	Ensure it works	Chaos engineering, alert testing
Retention policy	Manage costs	Metrics: 30d, Logs: 30d, Traces: 7d
This comprehensive guide gives you a production-ready monitoring solution for your microservices!
